## 0. How to read this document

Version 3 was written before MAIIC's database could be read directly. It described the engine around three extracts (A, B and C) that MAIIC's ICT team produced with their own scripts, and it listed twenty-three choices that were still open. Between 5 and 7 October 2026 Barry Makumba ran twenty-seven data-dictionary queries and eighteen follow-up extracts for us, then five more on request. Those extracts are the whole loan ledger and every supporting table, and they changed the picture: the data gaps of version 3 are closed, the arithmetic of the core system is reproduced to the cent, and most of the open choices can now be settled on evidence rather than on argument.

This document records that. It is written, as before, for three readers at once: the CFO, who has to be confident that the numbers will stand up to Deloitte; the ICT manager, who supplies the data and hosts the system; and the developers, who build it. Every section starts in plain language. The same three words are used in the same way as in version 3:

- **Agreed** means a decision that has been taken, by whom and when.
- **Decided and seeded** is new. It means a choice that has been settled by Dupleix on the evidence, written into the Governance Centre as the default in force, and is waiting only for Dr Thom to confirm or change it. Section 4 lists every one, with its default.
- **Proven** means a fact tested against MAIIC's own data and reproduced, with the file and the count given so that anyone can repeat the test.

Where a file is named, it is one of the extracts committed under `docs/bootstrap/` in the repository (section 6.10), with a copy in `2. Documents from clients\Raw Query Scripts\Query Requests to MAIIC\Follow-Up Scripts Resutls\`. The scripts that produced every figure in this document are in `Build files\` beside them, with a README that says which script makes which number.

Where a section says a design is taken from the Dupleix suite (sections 6, 11, 12, 13, 14 and 15), the code it was taken from is copied verbatim under `docs/reference/` in the repository, indexed by section in its README and pinned to the source commits in its manifest, so that the completeness of this document can be verified against working code and the build can port rather than reinvent. That folder is code only. The regulatory texts the document relies on are under `docs/regulatory/` (section 3.6).

## 2. The data the engine now runs on

### 2.1 What replaced Extracts A, B and C

Version 3 built the engine around a contract master (Extract A), a transaction file (Extract B) and an interest file (Extract C), each produced by a MAIIC script with its own faults: a fixed/floating label that was the wrong way round, amounts with their signs stripped, and a 2025 that was counted three times. None of the three is needed any more. The engine reads the tables behind them.

| Need | Version 3 source | Now | What is proven about it |

| Who the borrower is, the facility, its terms | Extract A | `P1_02` account master (184 accounts) and `P1_03` loan master, every column, with the Interest Policy and the Floating Flag read directly | 184 accounts in the 18 loan schemes: 89 active (A), 46 closed (C), 25 dormant (D), 21 code H, 3 frozen (F). 109 were taken on at migration, 75 opened since. |
| Every posting on every account | Extract B | `P1_01` the ledger (CUMVOUCH), signed amounts, every transaction type | Ties to the month-end balance history on 2,533 of 2,549 account-months; to the stored loan book's carrying amount on 2,264 of 2,264; to the trial balance on 1050101, 1050102 and 1050401 in every month. The 16 open balance-history rows are one account (section 3.5). |

(5) Drawdowns: the tranche register is rebuilt from the ledger's type-301 postings, with fee debits folded into their drawdown (section 3.11); `P3_12` is a cross-check.

### 2.2 Two tables that do not help, and why

`INTEREST_SUMMARY` (`P1_05`) looked like the calculation behind every interest posting. It is a working table that the month-end run rebuilds; it holds the latest month only (82 rows, all August 2026), confirmed by a second run on 7 October. `INTEREST_PRODUCT` (`P1_05b`, 8 October) does keep history, October 2024 to September 2026 on 126 accounts, and its monthly totals equal the ledger's interest postings on 1,763 of 1,818 shared account-months; but its columns are not what their names say: the "product" column holds the computed interest, not the balance times days, and the days column is zero. It confirms the postings and gives a month's interest before the ledger is pulled; the arithmetic behind each posting still comes from the posting's own narration, which states the period and the rate (section 3.1), and from the daily accrual where it exists.

The instalment chart (`P2_10`) is current, not historical, and marks a missed instalment as paid. It is a reference, not a cash-flow source.

### 2.3 The take-on loans

The 109 loans that existed before E-Banker went live were taken on at 31 July 2024, 77 of them with a single posting on that day totalling MWK 8,297,388,309.25. Their history before that date is not in E-Banker. Tamanda's amortisation schedules at 31 October 2024 (100 blocks) have been mapped to the E-Banker accounts: 105 of 109 facilities matched, 104 of them with high confidence, the principal equal to the take-on posting on 77 and the value date equal to the first disbursement-schedule date on 82. The four not matched are Favoured Farms 2022 and three equity-type positions that the engine must refuse in any case (MMC, Wi Jays, Good Hope). The workbook is with Tamanda to tick; its Upload summary sheet is the take-on loader's input (section 7.6).

**Status 14 October 2026:** the workbook came back on 13 October 2026 (`Take-on schedules with mapping - fees completed by MAIIC - received 13 Oct 2026.xlsx`, committed under `docs/bootstrap/takeon/`, the 7 October copy moved to `takeon/superseded/`) with the fees of 101 facilities, 362,307,251.23 in total (manifest.json note). The mapping confirmations (Mapping columns X and Y) are still blank, so every take-on loan carries the flag "mapping not yet confirmed by Finance" (TakeonLandingService.php:218-220).

## 3. What is proven about E-Banker's arithmetic

Everything in this section was tested on the ledger. The engine's contractual-interest service reproduces it, and the reconciliation names a cause for any month that does not agree.

### 3.1 Interest is charged for the whole calendar month at one rate

Every month-end interest posting carries a narration of the form "To Trf IntDR Fm 01-JUN-25 To 30-JUN-25 @ 32.65%". Reading all 2,047 of them:

- The period runs from the 1st to the month-end in every ordinary month (299 of the 303 months that had a rate change inside them; the other four are drawdown months). The period is never split at the date the rate changed.
- Where the daily accrual exists, its daily amount is constant through the month and the month-end posting equals the sum of the daily rows to the cent.
- The amount is the previous month-end balance, including interest already capitalised, times the rate, times the days in the month, over 365. Rebuilt this way, 1,595 of 2,047 postings agree within 0.1 percent and 1,828 within 1 percent; the postings that agree within 1 percent are 91.6 percent of the value. The remainder are drawdown months, moratorium months and the 28 year-end adjustments of section 3.4.
- **When the rate changes inside a month, the whole month is charged at the new rate.** In the 229 account-months where the loan-book rate changed from the previous month-end, the narration carries the new rate in 217, the old rate in 2 and neither in 10. A PLR cut on the 20th reduces the whole month's interest. This decides open choice O2 (section 4.2).

### 3.2 The charged rate is the loan book's rate, not the set-up table's

The rate set-up table (`P1_04`) holds, for each account, a row for every PLR change with a `PLR_RATE`, a variation and an `INTEREST_RATE`. It is not the rate E-Banker charges. Account 000104420000005 is charged 32.45 percent through April 2025 and 32.65 percent from May while the set-up table says 30.35 falling to 30.15; the difference is constant for months at a time. The charged rate is the one on the loan master, which the monthly loan book reports: the narration equals the loan book's month-end rate on 1,453 of 1,600 testable account-months, and in every month where the two disagree the loan book was re-run later with a back-dated change.

On the accounts that behave as the product intends, the charged rate is exactly the PLR plus a fixed spread, applied in the month of the PLR change: account 000104420000010 is 25.2 + 6.0, then 24.7 + 6.0, 22.4 + 6.0, 20.8 + 6.0, 20.6 + 6.0 and 20.4 + 6.0 as the PLR fell through 2026. On others the spread moves (account 000104420000005: 7.05, 7.45, 7.55, 7.45). A moving spread is MAIIC re-pricing at its own option, which is the reset-or-modification question of open choice O17, and the engine reports every such move.

Consequence for the build: the rate-history importer takes the **monthly loan-book rate** as the rate charged, the **PLR master** as the reference rate, and the set-up table as the register of when a change was keyed. The spread is derived as charged rate less PLR, month by month, and an account whose spread drifts is flagged (the `SpreadDerivationService` of P2 already does this).

### 3.3 Signs, transaction types and the six GL codes

Debits are negative in the ledger. The transaction types that matter: 301 drawdown, 303 monthly interest, 305 repayment, 306 reversal of a repayment, 120 the year-end "Diff Int Credit by ROI" adjustment, 300 write-off, 343 reversal of a credit, 900 and 901 a fee posted and reversed, 400 and 401 the Nascomex equity entries. The loan accounts sit on six GL codes: 1050101 and 1050102 (MAIIC Agricultural and Industrial), 1050201 and 1050202 (FInES Agricultural and Industrial, the 10 percent concessional book), 1050401 (term loans) and 1050103.

**The income GLs, and the second chart of accounts.** Interest income sits on one E-Banker GL per loan GL: 4215 (1050101), 4216 (1050102), 42019 (1050401; printed as 4201 in the code column of the trial balance export, with the full code in its title), 4218 (1050201), 4219 (1050202), and 4221 to 4224, 4260 and 4262 for the Mega Farm schemes. Fees sit on 4871 legal, 4873 arrangement, 4874 insurance and 4875 PCG guarantee. Fees are not always posted to the loan account: in 2025 about 44 percent of fee income, and nearly all of it from March 2026, was credited to the fee GL in the drawdown batch with the loan account carrying the gross drawdown (section 3.7). The annual accounts are prepared in a second ledger (QuickBooks) with its own codes, and E-Banker's interest GLs roll up into it: 4215, 4216 and 42019 into 4206 Interest on Term Loans-MAIIC; 4218 and 4219 into 4209 Term loan interest-FInES (section 7.7). Any extract, reconciliation or interface to Finance has to carry that mapping.

### 3.4 The 28 year-end adjustments of 31 December 2025

On 31 December 2025 E-Banker re-rated the year's interest on the PLR-linked book to the rate on each account at year end and posted the difference: 22 debits (type 303) totalling MWK 96,390,096.16 charged to customers and 6 credits (type 120) totalling MWK 85,166,683.31 in their favour, on 1050101 and 1050102 only, in the same interest-posting batches as December's ordinary interest (INTP 41 and 42). The 6 credits are exactly the difference between Extracts B and C that version 3 could not explain.

`GL_03` of 8 October shows the other side: **one net voucher per batch to interest income**. Batch 41 credits 4215 by 32,956,675.55 (the 1050101 debits of 35,940,279.17 less credits of 2,983,603.62); batch 42 debits 4216 by 21,733,262.70 (the 1050102 credits of 82,183,079.69 less debits of 60,449,816.99). All 28 adjustments therefore went through 2025 interest income, net 11,223,412.85, and with that reading the December movement on both income GLs equals the ledger to the cent (1050101: ordinary interest 27,129,967.64 plus 32,956,675.55 equals the trial balance's 60,086,643.19; 1050102: 173,635,459.72 less 21,733,262.70 equals 151,902,197.02). An earlier reading that the debits had not reached income was a double count, since the debits are type 303 and sat inside the ordinary figure; it is withdrawn.

The engine treats the 28 as interest of December 2025 and shows them as their own line in the reconciliation. The remaining question for Finance is one of intent, not of posting: whether the year-end re-rating to the closing rate was meant to be the policy, since the engine's contractual interest follows the month-end rate month by month (section 3.1) and the two differ by exactly these amounts.

### 3.5 Two reconciling differences, both explained

**The FInES GLs.** The trial balance for 1050201 is MWK 400,000.00 above the sum of its accounts in every month and 1050202 is MWK 1,000,000.00 below, while the other GLs agree to the cent. Barry's queries of 7 October showed there is no posting without an account (`GL_01` is empty) and that the yearly GL opening balances are keyed by hand (`GL_02`): the 2025 opening for the two FInES GLs was keyed 400,000 above and 1,000,000 below the accounts, and the 2026 opening was taken from the trial balance, carrying the difference forward. Fix: Finance confirms which figure agreed to the 2024 audited accounts and adjusts the two GL openings. Until then the engine carries the 600,000 net as one named line.

**Zaithwa Farms (000104450000015).** The month-end balance table shows the customer MWK 1,827,633.62 better off than the ledger from May 2025. The ledger is complete and right (24 postings, closing at 1,225,683.03 in the customer's favour) and so is the stored loan book. The balance table holds 30 April 2025 twice, and its May row restarted from nil and omitted both May receipts; every later row inherits the error. It is the only duplicate month-end row in the whole table. Fix: the vendor rebuilds the account's rows from May 2025; Finance checks any statement sent to the customer. No change to the engine, which reads the ledger.

### 3.6 The Reserve Bank's directive for development finance institutions, and staging

MAIIC is licensed as a development finance institution, and the Reserve Bank of Malawi classifies its lending under the **Financial Services (Credit Risk Management for Development Finance Institutions) Directive, 2018** (Malawi Gazette Supplement of 13 July 2018, No. 18A, pages 42 to 47; a copy is under `docs/regulatory/` in the repository and beside this document). It is the authority behind MAIIC's staging, and the engine's governed thresholds follow it.

The directive sorts facilities by repayment period (section 2): short term, not more than 12 months; medium term, 12 to 60 months; long term, over 60 months. It then classifies each by how long instalments are overdue (section 10) and sets the provision for each class (the Schedule):

| Class | Short-term facility | Medium- and long-term facility | Provision |
|---|---|---|---|
| Standard | current, to 30 days overdue | 31 to 90 days | 0 percent |
| Special mention | 31 to 90 days | 91 to 180 days | 5 percent |
| Substandard | 91 to 180 days | 181 to 365 days | 20 percent |
| Doubtful | 181 to 365 days | 366 to 746 days | 50 percent |
| Loss | over 365 days | over 746 days | 100 percent |

Substandard, doubtful and loss are **non-performing**: the facility goes on non-accrual (section 13), interest accrued but not collected is reversed and interest is recognised only when received in cash, regardless of collateral; a loss facility is written off in the following quarter (section 12); where the directive's provision exceeds the IFRS one, the excess is an appropriation to a loan-loss reserve, not capital (section 15).

**What the engine takes from it.** Stage 3 (credit-impaired) is the directive's non-performing line: **181 days past due for a medium- or long-term facility, 91 days for a short-term one.** That is where MAIIC's long-standing rule of "over 181 days" comes from, and it rebuts the IFRS 9 presumption that default occurs no later than 90 days past due (B5.5.37) on the authority of the regulator's own classification of development-finance lending; the 2025 financial statements rest on it. Stage 2 is kept at 31 days past due for every tenor: the IFRS 9 presumption of a significant increase in credit risk at 30 days (B5.5.11) is not rebutted, because the directive's wider "standard" band for medium- and long-term facilities is a prudential classification, not evidence that credit risk has not risen. The rebuttal of that 30-day presumption stays available as a governed proposal, inactive until Dr Thom signs it (the staging rebuttal of O13).

The thresholds are seeded by tenor class into `staging_thresholds`, each row carrying the directive section it rests on: short term 31 and 91; medium and long term 31 and 181; the Mega Farm programme, which is seasonal input finance repaid after harvest and therefore short term whatever tenor the account carries, 31 and 91; and the long-term Stage 2 proposal at 91, future-dated. The classifier reads days past due from the stored report's overdue date and arrears buckets (section 6.2).

**Status 14 October 2026:** the classifier is `StagingService` (not the legacy `StagingClassifier`). Days past due are still the report's, with three corrections made in the loan book build and named on the row: a loan past maturity that still owes is overdue from maturity; where the chart in force at the month-end has nothing due yet, the reported ageing is not accepted; where E-Banker's chart dates several instalments on one day, the days are counted on the chart dated by its monthly definition against the ledger receipts (LoanBookBuildService.php:557-620, `InstalmentChartHistory`). A cured loan stays in its stage for the governed probation (`stage_cure_months`, seeded 3 months, StagingService.php:232), which is not in the table above. **Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** the threshold rows are now under maker-checker: a row is proposed by one person and approved by another on Settings, Staging basis (`StagingThresholdService`), forward only and never into a locked month, and staging reads only approved rows. The legacy `StagingClassifier` (the Excel loan-book importer) reads the rule at the import's period end and stops with the reason when none is in force; it no longer falls back to 31 and 181 days (system audit H11; `GoLiveControlsTest`, `StagingClassifierTest`).


**The three rules MAIIC lives with, compared.** The signed 2025 accounting policy, the ECL model that produced the 2025 figures and the directive do not say the same thing, and the engine has to carry all three in a way the auditors can follow.

| | The accounting policy (2025 statements, notes 3.8 and 22.8.1) | The November 2025 ECL model | The directive, as seeded |
|---|---|---|---|
| Stage 3, default | "when the borrower has missed four consecutive contractual payments, or the loan becomes 90 days past due", with note 3.8's door: "unless the Entity has reasonable and supportable information to demonstrate that a more lagging default criterion is more appropriate" | Over 181 days past due. Nothing at 90 | Non-performing from 181 days (medium and long term), 91 (short term) |
| Stage 2, significant increase in credit risk | Changes in the probability of default against inception, qualitative events, the 30-day backstop | 31 to 180 days past due. The probability-change test exists in the sheet but fired on none of the 100 loans | 31 days (IFRS 9 B5.5.11, not rebutted) |
| The directive's classes | not mentioned | a column, blank on every loan | Standard, special mention, substandard, doubtful, loss, by tenor |
| A tenor split | none | none: 181 for every facility | 91 days at 12 months or less, 181 above |
| November 2025 | | 100 loans: 72, 10 and 18 by stage; exposure 11.54, 1.22 and 1.08 billion | the E-Banker book of 125 loans on the demo: 79, 25 and 21; 7.39, 4.38 and 2.06 billion |

Five things follow. **The policy note says 90 days and the model applied 181**; the note's door was walked through but the note does not say so, and for the 2026 statements it must, with the directive as the reason, or the model must move to 90. **The four-consecutive-payments test** is in the policy and in neither the model nor the directive; on monthly instalments it falls at about 120 days, and the engine will carry it as a second Stage 3 trigger from the instalment plan and the ledger. (**Status 14 October 2026:** built: `stage3_missed_instalments` is read at StagingService.php:265 and the unpaid instalments are counted on the chart in force at the month-end against the ledger receipts, StagingService.php:114-128; a loan with no readable chart, or whose chart asks for more than the ledger ever debited, is reported as not evaluated rather than staged.) **Stage 2 was, in practice, days past due alone**; the engine's 31 days reproduces that, and the probability-change test is the work of section 14. **No tenor split existed**, so the eight short-term facilities of November were staged at 181 where the directive says 91: small on the MAIIC book, decisive on the Mega Farm book. **The model held 100 loans where E-Banker held 125**; the 25 it leaves out (closed, code H, nil balances) are to be named by Finance before the two stagings can be reconciled.

**Which days past due.** The directive counts days from the overdue instalment. The stored report carries both the date of the oldest unpaid instalment and the ageing of the overdue amounts by bucket, and on some accounts they disagree: an account partly cured keeps an old overdue date while its buckets empty. The engine takes the instalment date, as the directive does, and shows the bucket ageing beside it; the choice is governed (`dpd_basis`) so that it can be changed with a reason. (**Status 14 October 2026:** `dpd_basis` is read when the loan book is built, LoanBookBuildService.php:632, but a missing approved value falls back to the oldest-instalment basis in code instead of stopping the build, LoanBookBuildService.php:630-634.)

**Decision D31.** The governed thresholds stand as seeded: Stage 3 at 181 days for medium- and long-term facilities and 91 for short-term and Mega Farm, Stage 2 at 31 days. They follow the directive and reproduce the 2025 model, so the 2025 figures do not move. The rebuttal of the 90-day presumption is written down as the O13 sign-off, with the directive cited by gazette and section, and a sentence is drafted for the 2026 policy note. The four-payment trigger and the days-past-due basis become governed items beside the thresholds.

Two differences between the directive and IFRS 9 that the engine must show rather than resolve: the directive stops interest on a non-performing facility, where IFRS 9 5.4.1(b) continues it on the net carrying amount (the trial balance's interest-suspense account, 1320, is the directive's rule in practice, and the reconciliation carries the difference as its own line); and the directive's provision percentages are prudential floors, so the loan-loss reserve of section 15 is where any excess over the ECL sits.

**Status 14 October 2026:** GL 1320 is not read by any code. The RBM return's section C reports one month's contractual interest less the EIR interest on non-performing loans (RbmReturnService.php:102-126), not the directive's cumulative, cash-basis suspense, and the reconciliation carries no 1320 line. The directive's section 13(3) (back to accrual only once all arrears are paid) is not built. The loan-loss reserve is shown only as the minimum-provision total in section B of the return, with no labelled reserve line. The section 12 write-off of loss facilities in the following quarter has no loss-age flag (section 3.8).

**Status 14 October 2026 (reports build):** the RBM return now carries (RbmReturnService, RbmReturnController, Reports/RbmReturn.vue; RbmReturnServiceTest): the directive's paragraph 12 as its own section, "Loss facilities to be written off", each loss facility with the month it became loss on the loan books (the unbroken run of loss classification by days past due and term, "at or before" where the run reaches the first month held), its age, the quarter-end by which it must be written off and its status (overdue, due this quarter, due next quarter); on the 13 October bootstrap at September 2026, 10 loss facilities of 1,510,956,083.45, 7 of them (1,030,047,803.06) past the write-off quarter. Section C keeps the month's figure and adds the cumulative measure of paragraph 13(1): the contractual interest charged on each facility non-performing at the month-end since the month it became non-performing (821,916,269.33 on 50 facilities at September 2026), the cash received in the same window beside it (760,304,079.21, not split between principal and interest because the ledger does not split a repayment, so the cash-basis income the directive allows is a named gap), and GL 1320 from the trial balance as the tie (the trial balances from March 2026 carry no 1320 line; the last held is 21,865,867.35 credit at February 2026, unchanged since January 2025). The loan loss reserve of paragraph 15(1) is a labelled line (the minimum provision over the IFRS 9 allowance). Paragraph 13(3) is still not built. The return's paragraph references were corrected against the Gazette: it had cited section 17 (the monetary penalties) for the return, section 12 for the provisions (paragraph 14 and the Schedule) and section 15 for restructuring (paragraph 16(1)).

### 3.7 Fee income and the year-end true-up into 4206 (added 11 October 2026)

**In plain language.** The fees MAIIC charges at drawdown are booked in full to two income accounts, 4871 Legal Fees and 4873 Arrangement Fees. When the annual accounts are prepared, Finance moves the part of those fees that belongs in the effective interest rate, and a few interest adjustments, into one interest account, 4206 Interest on Term Loans-MAIIC. Dr Thom described this at the meeting of 24 September 2026. The figures below show exactly what moved in 2025 and where each figure was found.

**The fee ledgers agree to the trial balance.** Tamanda's GL spools of 19 August (`GL 4871` and `GL 4873`, as at 31 December 2025 and 31 July 2026) agree to the trial balance year-to-date in every month from January to November 2025 and from January to July 2026, to the cent. Before the closing voucher of 31 December 2025 they stand at **171,128,135.00** (legal) and **311,342,792.00** (arrangement), which are also the figures in the final E-Banker trial balance for December 2025 and in the "Mapping TBs" sheet of Dr Thom's workbook of 24 September (`Mapping TBs August 2026 to Audited Financial Statements4.xlsx`). Within the year the spools hold two corrections only: a legal fee over-deduction reversed on 24 October 2025 (net 100,000) and 7,629,800 moved from 4873 to 4871 for Prominade Medical Centre on 30 December 2025.

**Not every fee touches the loan account.** Each spool line was matched to the account ledger (`P1_01`) by date and amount. In 2025 about 56 percent of each fee account (legal 96,579,275 on 20 lines; arrangement about 175 million on 21 lines) has a matching fee debit on the loan account at drawdown. The rest was credited to income in the drawdown batch with no fee leg on the account: the loan account carries the gross drawdown instead (Malawi Police SACCO, 7 May 2025: 1,046,210,000 drawn, being 1,000,000,000 plus 30,000,000 arrangement and 16,210,000 legal). From March 2026 almost no fee reaches a loan account. The 23 "BRFF" fee lines of June and July 2026 have no drawdown in the eighteen schemes at all; whether BRFF is a programme outside them is asked in section 5. **So the fee importer reconciles to the fee GL spools, not to the account ledger** (section 7.4).

**What 4206 carries in the 2025 accounts.**

| 4206 Interest on Term Loans-MAIIC, 2025 | MWK | Source |
|---|---:|---|
| E-Banker 4215 Interest on MAIIC Agricultural Loans (includes the year-end adjustment +32,956,675.55, section 3.4) | 554,447,006.83 | GL 4215 spool; final E-Banker TB |
| E-Banker 4216 Interest on MAIIC Industrial Loans (includes the year-end adjustment -21,733,262.70) | 1,513,020,260.19 | GL 4216 spool; final E-Banker TB |
| E-Banker 42019 Interest on MAIIC Term Loan | 39,093,897.99 | Final E-Banker TB |
| **4206 in the initial TB submitted to the auditor** | **2,106,561,165.01** | AFS mapping workbook |
| Audit adjustment "Interest in suspense reversals" (the 1320 Interest Suspense balance) | 21,865,867.35 | AFS mapping workbook, Final AFS TB of 19 March 2026 |
| **4206 in the final AFS TB** | **2,128,427,032.36** | Same |
| Arrangement fees presented as EIR interest income | 259,317,000.00 | Dr Thom's workbook, "Income Statement" sheet against "Mapping TBs" |
| **EIR Interest Income line for 4206 in the audited accounts** | **2,387,744,032.36** | Same |

E-Banker 4218 and 4219 together (447,829,531.72) are 4209 Term loan interest-FInES, unchanged.

**The fee move is a manual adjustment, not a posted journal.** The final AFS TB lists every audit adjustment (31 of them) and 4873 is still 311,342,792 in it. The 259,317,000 exists only in the income-statement mapping, where 4873 shows 52,025,792 and 4206 shows the 259,317,000 more; profit is the same in both sheets (4,850,745,713). No spool will ever show it, and it is not a set of whole fee postings: no combination of the 35 lines of GL 4873 for 2025 leaves 52,025,792. It was a manual adjustment recommended by the auditors, who tested a sample of fees and extrapolated the result (MAIIC, 12 October 2026), applied in the TB-to-AFS mapping and not posted in E-Banker or in the trial balance: in Dr Thom's workbook 4873 is 311,342,792 in the trial balance sheet and 52,025,792 in the income statement sheet, both typed values with no adjusting entry. E-Banker has no effective-interest calculation, which is why this engine is being built. Its detailed working is therefore not needed; the figure is kept only as the 2025 comparator for the engine's output. Legal fees were not moved in 2025. In 2024 the move went the other way: 37,610,000 out of 4206 into 4873, so 2024 arrangement fees are 1,299,964,206 in the ledger and 1,337,574,206 in the accounts.

**What this corrects.** Version 3 (fact F18 and open item O23) and the note of 24 September read 52,025,792 as the 2025 ledger figure for arrangement fees. It is the figure after the move; the ledger figure is 311,342,792. O23, which asked why 2024 arrangement fees were so much larger than 2025, is smaller than it looked: 1,299,964,206 against 311,342,792, not against 52,025,792.

**What the engine does with it.** The engine's inputs are the fees as charged, which are the ledger figures above. Its output for 2025 is compared with what the accounts recognised: 259,317,000 of arrangement fees treated as interest, plus the 21,865,867.35 suspense reversal, both inside 4206. The journal the engine proposes (O10, O21) replaces this presentation entry. Until Finance opens a dedicated EIR adjustment account, 4206 is the account the true-up lands in, because that is where it lands today.

**Status 14 October 2026 (review item R06):** what is proven here is the presentation bridge: where the 259,317,000 and the 21,865,867.35 were presented, read from the two workbooks and the trial balances. How the 259,317,000 was measured is not proven by the files; "an audit estimate extrapolated from a sample" is MAIIC's explanation of 12 October 2026, and the fact that no subset of whole 4873 lines leaves 52,025,792 is consistent with it but does not prove it. Replacing the presentation entry needs a fee deferral roll-forward (opening deferred fees, amortisation in the year, closing) and separate entries for the deferral, the amortisation, prior-period differences and the suspense reversal; it is designed with the true-up account (O10) and the fee journal (O21). The journal proposal is not built: the engine proposes no journal yet, and the settings `trueup_gl_account` and `fee_reclass_journal` are read by no engine (section 4.2).

Scripts: `docs/build-files/fee_gl_recon_2026-10-11.py` (the spools against the trial balances and the account ledger). The 4206 bridge is read from the AFS mapping workbook ("TB December 2025", "Final E-Banker TB Dec 2025" and "FINAL AFS TB as of 19th Mar 26") and Dr Thom's workbook. Dr Thom's email of 24 September 2026 is saved beside the workbook in `New Doc Received 21 Sep`.

### 3.8 Status H, and the write-offs of 2026 (added 12 October 2026)

**In plain language.** Twenty-one loan accounts carry the status code H in E-Banker's account master. The status history says they were paid up; the balances say they were not. In June 2026 Finance cleared what they still owed by a transfer into the write-off account, which E-Banker recorded as a repayment. Read at face value, the ledger turns MWK 38.5 million of losses into cash collected. This section sets out the evidence, what H means on it, and the rule the engine follows.

**What the records show.**

| Source | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Account master (`P1_02`) | 21 accounts with STATUS_CODE H, status reason "Paid Up", status dates 11 December 2025 (3 accounts), 16 December 2025 (13), 30 December 2025 (1) and 30 June 2026 (4) (corrected 14 October 2026 from `ACCOUNT_STATUS_DATE` in `P1_02`; it said "status dates 11 to 16 December 2025 (17 accounts) and 30 June 2026 (4)") |
| Status history (`P3_13`) | No H row at all. Each of the 21 went from A to D "Paid Up" on those dates; the H is set in the master without a history entry |
| Balance history (`P2_09`, `M09_02`) | The balances did not change between December 2025 and May 2026: Lenziemill Milling owed 5,667,578.18, COMSIV 8,318,966.49, Mwachisosa Women Producers 6,676,759.40, and so on. "Paid Up" was not a repayment |
| Ledger (`P1_01`) | On 29 June 2026 one batch, TRF 3, credited MWK 38,492,032.54 to 20 of the accounts, type 305 (the repayment type), narrated "ACCOUNT CLOSURE"; no leg of the batch is on another loan account |
| Trial balance (Finance) | GL 1064 Term Loans Write Offs rose by exactly 38,492,032.54 between May and June 2026 |
| E-Banker manual (section 7.4) | A genuine account closure is posted with batch type ACCLO; this batch was a TRF transfer |
| Finance's loan books | All 21 accounts carry balances in the May 2026 loan book and are nil from June 2026 |

**What H means.** On this evidence H marks an account **written off and closed**. Finance has been asked to confirm (section 5). (**Status 14 October 2026, review item R07:** proven: H is set in the account master on 21 accounts; the status history has no H row; 20 of them received the ACCOUNT CLOSURE credits of TRF 3, 38,492,032.54, whose other leg is GL 1064 (`GL_05`). Inferred, not confirmed: that H means written off. Finance has not yet confirmed it. A write-off is dated from its posting, never from the H status date.) The other codes, for the record: A active; C closed (15 of the 46 are UAT test accounts); D closed, with the reason in the history (paid up, foreclosure, lapsed); F seen on the accounts whose balance was re-booked to another account (The Food Empire Limited, moved from MAIIC Agricultural to FInES in December 2025 and to a term loan in May 2026, each move a closure credit matched by an equal debit on the next account).

**Three accounts the batch did not settle cleanly.** Imani Investments (000104460000061): the transfer, 1,102,536.37, exceeded what was owed, and the account is **in credit by 861,723.83**. Tac-Maz Investment (000104450000038): 37,291.56 is still owed after the transfer and interest is still charged monthly. Perisha Agro and Packaging (000104450000030): not in the batch; 1,513.12 still owed.

**The write-off account over time.**

| When | GL 1064 movement | What it is | Loans |
|---|---:|---|---|
| 31 August 2024 | 22,286,434.32 | Two write-offs posted as type 300 | Micholess Creameries, Maluso Cooperative |
| January 2026 | 138,135,680.80 | Debit 1064, credit 1876 ECL Write Offs: a memorandum pair; **no loan account moved** | Not identified (asked of Finance, section 5). **Status 14 October 2026:** MUSECO (104420000005) is the candidate: proposed in the write-off register of the demo database for 31 December 2025, 138,135,680.80, waiting for a second approver and Finance's confirmation; the register of a fresh bootstrap is empty, since a proposal is a person's act |
| 29 June 2026 | 38,492,032.54 | TRF 3, type 305 on the loans, debit 1064 | The 20 H accounts above |

The write-off tables of E-Banker (LOAN_WRITEOFF_MASTER, LOAN_WRITEOFF_TRANS) are empty (CT_01), so the GL and the ledger are the only record. Of the three, only the 2024 write-offs carry the write-off transaction type.

**The rule the engine follows.** A credit to a loan account is a **write-off, not a collection**, when any of these holds: its transaction type is 300; it sits in a batch whose other leg is GL 1064 (read from `GL_05`, below, and later from the monthly ledger); or it is narrated "ACCOUNT CLOSURE" in a TRF batch on an account whose status is H. The loan book build (method B) records it as written off; the LGD engine counts the amount as a loss, not a recovery, and does not treat the account as settled; the EIR stops at the write-off date with the written-off amount as derecognition, not cash received; the RBM return shows it as written off. An account in credit after a write-off (Imani) is reported as a liability to the customer, not as negative exposure. Status H is mapped to written off in the engine's status table, and D "Paid Up" is not taken as evidence of repayment unless the balance went to nil by cash.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the rule is asked by the loan book build (method B), the contract inputs, the EIR's ledger cash and the instalment-chart receipts (`WriteOffClassifier`; ContractInputsBuildService, LoanBookBuildService, EirLedgerCashService, InstalmentChartHistory). The rest of this paragraph is still to build: the LGD engine does not use the classifier (it reads a loan that leaves the book as a write-off, LgdEngineService.php:88); the RBM return does not show write-offs and has no loss-age flag; there is no status table mapping H. The classification is by batch: any credit in a batch whose other leg debits 1064 is a write-off in full (WriteOffClassifier.php:46-64). Review item R08 (accepted, P1) is not built: classify by the individual loan voucher allocated against a confirmed 1064 leg, write off only the confirmed amount (whole or part) with the remaining asset continuing, and hold Imani's 861,723.83 as a reconciliation exception for Finance, not book it as a liability.

**Status 14 October 2026 (reports build):** the write-offs now reach the reports from one source, `WriteOffSource` (the ledger's write-offs by `WriteOffClassifier`, through `WriteOffRegisterService::ledgerWriteOffs`, and the APPROVED entries of the write-off register; a register entry repeating a ledger write-off of the same contract, date and amount is counted once): the IFRS 9 note (IFRS 7.35H and 35I, the write-off line of both reconciliations; the allowance line takes the allowance each write-off used, the opening allowance up to the amount written off; a contract still in the book at nil after a write-off is attributed as well as one that left the book), the ECL reconciliation CSV, the Loan Book Reconciliation (a written-off line, with the list; the old filter on contract_status never matched), the hub's opening-to-closing ECL and ECL charge, and the RBM return's paragraph 12 section. IFRS 7.35L (amounts written off still subject to enforcement activity) is stated as not recorded: no enforcement status is held. On the 13 October bootstrap the note from December 2025 to September 2026 carries 20 contracts written off, 38,492,032.54 gross (TRF 3 of 29 June 2026), using 22,262,632.66 of allowance; it had shown nil. `M09_01` (the monthly ledger feed) is read for write-off batch legs as well as `GL_05` and `GL_03` (WriteOffClassifier::GL_LEG_QUERIES). Still open: the LGD engine does not use the classifier, R08 (by voucher, partial), and the write-off status table for H.

**Built (13 October 2026).** The rule above is one class, `WriteOffClassifier`, asked by the loan book build (method B), the contract inputs and the EIR's ledger cash, so all three agree. It reads the write-off batches from `GL_05` (received in pack 4 and landed: three batches debit GL 1064, TRF 95 of 31 August 2024, TRF 26 of 31 January 2026 and TRF 3 of 29 June 2026) and the status from the account master. On the demo database it finds 22 write-off postings, 60,778,466.86: the two type-300 write-offs of 2024 (22,286,434.32) and the 20 ACCOUNT CLOSURE credits of TRF 3 (38,492,032.54), which the ledger cash had loaded as repayments and now reclassifies as write-offs. A write-off that is not on the loan account goes in the **write-off register** (EIR & Revenue Recognition, EIR Data, Write-offs; table `loan_write_offs`): one person proposes with the reason and the evidence, a different person approves, and only an approved entry reaches the engines, as a Write-off on the loan's actual transactions (source `WRITE_OFF_REGISTER`), which the EIR treats as derecognition, not cash; a rejected or withdrawn entry never reaches them, and reversing an approved one removes its row. The January 2026 entry is MUSECO's: its carrying amount was exactly 138,135,680.80 from November 2025 to January 2026, the amount of the 1064 / 1876 pair. It is proposed in the register for 31 December 2025 and waits for a second person and Finance's confirmation.

**What is asked for.** `GL_05` (pack 4, RUN 7): every leg of batch TRF 3 of 29 June 2026, and every posting to 1064 and 1876 in January 2026, in the same shape as `GL_03`. Finance: the confirmation that H means written off, the loans behind the January 2026 entry of 138,135,680.80, and what is to be done about Imani's credit and Tac-Maz's residual.

**Status 14 October 2026:** `GL_05` was received on 12 October 2026 in pack 4 and is landed and read (WriteOffClassifier.php:33). The Finance items are still outstanding (section 5).

### 3.9 Reschedules: rebuilt from E-Banker's own tables (added 12 October 2026)

**In plain language.** Restructured loans are in version-1 scope (a negotiated change can be a modification under IFRS 9 5.4.3), but E-Banker's reschedule tables are empty (CT_01: LOAN_RESCHEDULE_DETAILS and T_RESCHEDULE hold no rows), so the plan was to wait for the vendor's Reschedule Report. The data dictionary shows that E-Banker keeps the evidence elsewhere, so the history can be rebuilt from the database instead.

**What the stored loan books show, and why it is not enough.** Between December 2024 and September 2026, 65 of the 144 accounts in the stored Loan Book Report change a term from one month to the next, but almost all of it is noise: in December 2025 the value date and the maturity change together (Lenziemill Milling's value date moves from 30 October 2020 to 1 September 2020), which is a correction of the report, not a reschedule, and some maturities flip between runs and back. Requiring the value date to stay the same and the change to last leaves four candidates (Nature's Gift twice, Ifracon, House of Men), three of them in that December. The loan books cannot stand in for a reschedule register.

**Where the evidence is.**

| Table | Rows | What it shows |
|---|---:|---|
| `FIXLOAN_INST_CHART`, rows with DELETE_FLAG Y or ACTIVE_FLAG N | part of 95,609 | Superseded instalment rows: earlier versions of a schedule, with the date and the user that replaced them. The 6 October extract (`P2_10`) took live rows only |
| `FIXLOAN_INST_CHART_300824`, `FIXLOAN_INST_CHART_141124`, `FIX_INST_CHART28SEP`, `FIXLOAN_INST_CHART1` | 4,977; 9,339; 9,106; 3,211 | Copies of the instalment chart taken on those dates; a loan whose schedule differs from today's was rescheduled in between |
| `MODIFICATION_LOG` | 5,991 | Every edit: the table, the field, the old value and the new value, the date, who entered it and who passed it |
| `ACCOUNT_CLOSER_HISTORY` | 21 | The closure records of the 21 accounts with status H (section 3.8) |

**The method.** `RS_02a` to `RS_02g` (pack 4, RUN 8) bring these in. A loan is taken as rescheduled when its schedule was replaced (a superseded set of instalments, or a dated copy that differs from the live chart in dates, amounts or the number of instalments) or when `MODIFICATION_LOG` records an edit to its maturity, tenor, moratorium, instalment or rate. Each reschedule is written with its date, the old and the new terms, and who made it, and feeds the reset-or-modification test of `rate_change_classification` (O17) and the derecognition threshold (O16). The vendor's Reschedule Report is held, not cancelled: it is asked for again only if `RS_02` leaves gaps.

**Two probes in the same pack.** `TRIAL_BALANCE_HISTORY` (346,261 rows) is E-Banker's own trial balance history; if it holds every month-end, the monthly trial balance can come with the pack instead of separately from Finance. `CUMOVUCH_OLD` (13,359 rows) is an older copy of the ledger that may hold postings from before the July 2024 migration, the history the take-on loans lack (section 6.9). `TB_02` and `CV_01` (RUN 9) count what each holds before either is asked for in full, and `TB_03` (RUN 9c) brings the trial balance rows for December 2025, August 2026 and September 2026: December and August are proved against the trial balances Finance supplied, to the cent (December also shows how the year-end closing is held), and if they tie, September's rows are the September trial balance and the monthly trial balance joins the pack (section 7.7).

**Status 14 October 2026 (review items R10 and Cp3): proposed; `RS_02` received; register not built.** `RS_02a` to `RS_02g`, `TB_02`, `CV_01` and `TB_03` were received on 12 October 2026 in pack 4 and are landed by the bootstrap. No reschedule register is built: there is no table of reschedule events, no `contract_modifications`, and nothing writes `rate_reset_events` (0 rows on the fresh bootstrap of 13 October). `RS_02a` is read for one purpose only: the instalment chart in force at a month-end, for staging and days past due (`InstalmentChartHistory`, 14 October 2026). `RS_02f` (`MODIFICATION_LOG`) and the dated copies are read by no code, and no code reads `TB_03`, so the proof of December 2025 and August 2026 against Finance's trial balances is not built. When the register is built, an edit or a differing chart copy is a candidate, not a confirmed contractual change: each is resolved to its loan, uses the accepted or passed flag, is classified as a correction, a contractual reset, a modification or uncertain, and fails closed without an effective date or the old terms; `FIXLOAN_INST_CHART1` carries no date. The RBM return still reports restructured facilities as awaited (RbmReturnService.php:128-129).

**Status 14 October 2026 (restructure register built):** the register is built, on the pattern of the write-off register (`RestructureCandidateService`, `RestructureRegisterService`, `ModificationAccountingService`, migration `2026_10_14_230000`, screen EIR Data, **Restructures & Resets**, `eir-restructures.*`, command `php artisan eir:restructure-candidates`). The detector finds candidates in four places: a regeneration of the instalment chart (`RS_02a` and `P2_10`; the old terms are the chart in force the day before, the new terms the instalments created that day) that extends the maturity by more than one instalment period and keeps it, that amortises more than the old chart expected beyond the money the ledger advanced in between (capitalised arrears), or that falls on a date with no rate change and no drawdown; two dated copies of the chart (`RS_02b`, `RS_02d`, `RS_02c`) that differ, for a loan in no other extract, which fail closed without a date (`RS_02e` has none); an accepted `MODIFICATION_LOG` edit to a loan's maturity, tenor, sanction, moratorium, rate or interest policy, resolved to its account through `P1_03` or `P1_02` (a rejected edit is left out, an unpassed one fails closed, an edit before the first drawdown and a tenor written again in other units are suggested as corrections); and the migration worksheet `CV_10` (pack 9, 14 October 2026), whose eight restructures before the take-on are suggested as modifications. A regeneration that only re-amortises the balance for a PLR change is a reset, on the reset register (P5), not a candidate; a chart that amortises more than the whole ledger balance on its date fails closed (it counts money not lent). Every candidate carries the engine's suggestion (correction, contractual reset, modification, revised estimate or uncertain) and stays a candidate: one person classifies it with a reason (and, for a modification, the Board approval of directive paragraph 16(1) and the qualitative factors), a different person approves; the bootstrap never confirms one. On the clone of the fresh bootstrap with pack 9 landed (`maiic_ifrs9_restructure`): 70 candidates on 40 loans (12 chart regenerations, 11 copy differences, 14 log edits, 8 worksheet restructures, 25 rate moves of fixed-rate loans), 23 of which cannot be booked; the engine suggests a modification for 17 (MWK 1,108.7 million restructured in the worksheet before the take-on; MWK 81.8 million capitalised on Pinnacle Financial Services on 30 June 2026). None is confirmed. `InstalmentChartHistory` now also reads the dated copies for a loan held only there (BenCal Technologies: its chart is in force from 28 March 2024 because its plans 1326 to 1328 are live in `P3_11`, and its collapsed serials are dated by the monthly plan, so its days past due count 0 at August 2026 against the 254 reported).

### 3.10 Where the revenue roll-forward starts, and facilities drawn in tranches (added 13 October 2026)

**In plain language.** The engine builds each loan's amortised cost month by month: the opening balance, plus the interest at the EIR, less the cash. Everything after the first month follows from the month before, so the first month has to be right. Two faults were found in it on 13 October 2026 and both are corrected.

**The first month was dated a month late.** The opening was the present value of the remaining instalments taken at the end of the first month, and the month's interest was then added on top, so that month's interest was counted twice. A loan with one repayment of 1,030 due at the end of March, run from January at 1 percent a month, was left with about 10 outstanding after it was repaid in full. The opening is now the present value at the end of the month before (decision D32).

**A facility drawn in tranches was treated as if it had been lent in full on day one.** The schedule assumes every tranche is advanced at the start, so its present value counts money not yet lent, and the roll-forward never added a later tranche. Proven on Mphunzitsi SACCO (104430000085): approved 1,047.7 million, drawn 397.7 million in August 2025, 350 million in November 2025 and 300 million in February 2026; the engine opened December 2025 at 887.5 million against a ledger of 421.2 million and closed August 2026 at 349.9 million against 133.6 million. Malawi Police SACCO (3,139.3 million drawn on 19 January 2026) and KWACHA Boutique Hotel (800 million on 20 May and 700 million on 2 June 2026) were lent within their first engine month and were opened at the full present value with a full month's interest.

**The rule now (decision D33).** The first month opens on one of two bases, recorded on every row as `opening_basis` and carried forward with the closing balance:

| Basis | When | Opening | Later advances |
|---|---|---|---|
| SCHEDULE | The usual case | Present value, at the locked EIR, of the contractual flows due after the end of the month before | Never added: the schedule's value already counts every tranche |
| LEDGER | The facility is still drawing down (an advance falls after the end of the month before), or was lent within the month (opening nil), or the borrower has prepaid (the ledger is below half the present value, decision D34) | The drawn balance in the E-Banker ledger at the end of the month before | Each tranche added in the month it is drawn, earning interest from that day; held on the row as `advances` |

Every ledger opening is written to the audit log (`EIR Opening From Ledger`) with the present value, the ledger balance and the reason. The row now ties as opening + advances + interest + unwind - cash - derecognised = closing.

**What it did to the figures (demo database, August 2026), first step.** The ten loans on the ledger basis tie to the loan book within 2.8 million on 6,675.7 million: Mphunzitsi 127.2 million against 133.6 million, Malawi Police SACCO 3,065.1 million against 3,054.2 million. EIR interest for January to August 2026 read 1,812.3 million against contractual interest of 2,031.4 million, a shift of -219.1 million (it read -51.6 million before, because the double-counted first months raised the EIR side).

**The headline number changed, and two further causes were found.** After that step the other 21 loans, all on the schedule basis, were 479.5 million below the ledger by August 2026 and the gap grew every month. The largest were Chipala Investment (-117.6 million), Saile Financial Services (-70.1 million) and Chikomanyama Farms (-41.2 million). Until both causes were built, that part of the shift was overstated. Both are now built (13 October 2026, Edward's instruction to build it):

1. **Floating-rate loans whose rate has moved (decision D38, IFRS 9 B5.4.5).** The version 1 schedules of the floating-rate loans were generated at the contractual rate in the contract master on the day they were built, which is today's rate, not the rate the loan was lent at: Chipala Investment was lent at 30.0 percent and charged 29.1 to 30.2 percent through 2025, but its schedule was built at 26.1 percent, its August 2026 rate, and the EIR locked on it is 29.43 percent effective (26.07 nominal). The roll-forward therefore accrued about four points less than E-Banker charged for most of the period. The EIR in force for a month is now the locked nominal EIR plus the change from the rate the schedule was built on (`contract_eir.contractual_rate`) to the rate E-Banker charged that month (the loan book's rate, section 3.2), converted to an effective rate; the margin the fees add is kept. A fixed-rate loan keeps its locked EIR. Each row records the EIR it accrued at (`eir_annual`), and the GL reconciliation's rate effect and the EIR as at a date read it.
2. **Arrears at the opening (decision D39, section 6.9).** The present value at the end of the month before assumes every instalment up to then was paid as scheduled. Where the ledger owes more than the balance the schedule expects at that date (the same flows at the contractual rate) by more than one percent, the difference is booked as arrears and added to the opening, undiscounted because it is owed now. The first row records it (`opening_arrears`) and the audit log records the present value, the scheduled balance, the ledger balance and the arrears. Chipala had paid 152.2 million of 219.5 million due by 30 November 2025; its arrears at the opening are 81.3 million. In total 363.1 million of arrears were added across the book.

**Where the figures stand now (demo database, 31 August 2026).** Amortised cost 11,472.0 million against a gross ledger balance of 11,466.2 million, a difference of 5.8 million: the ten ledger-basis loans within 9.0 million, the 21 schedule-basis loans within 3.2 million (Chipala 907.9 million against 916.0 million). EIR interest for January to August 2026 is 1,913.7 million against contractual interest of 2,031.4 million: **the revenue shift is -117.7 million**. It was -51.6 million when the first months were double-counted and -219.1 million after the first correction alone.

### 3.11 Facilities drawn in tranches, the take-on EIRs and the GL fee route (built 14 October 2026)

**In plain language.** The results review of 13 October 2026 found three gaps in the EIR (its open items E1 to E3). A facility drawn in tranches was scheduled as if it had all been lent on the signing date, so its EIR was far above its rate. The take-on loans had an EIR solved in the take-on build that never reached the EIR engine, and 21 of the 73 were implausible. And the fees credited to income in the drawdown batch never reached any loan's EIR. All three are built (Edward's instruction of 14 October 2026), each with tests, and the figures below are from the rerun on a copy of the fresh bootstrap of 13 October (`maiic_ifrs9_eircheck`).

**E1: the schedule follows the drawdowns (decision D40).** The tranche register is rebuilt from the ledger with one row per drawdown date. A type-301 fee debit (a debit in a batch that only credits a fee GL, or narrated as an arrangement or legal fee) is added to the drawdown of its date and recorded as `capitalised_fees`; it is never a tranche of its own (section 7.8.4). Eighteen facilities had appeared to be drawn in tranches that were drawn once with their fees. The opening balance posted at the take-on of 31 July 2024 is the migration and is no longer a drawdown either. Where the register shows money going out after the date interest starts, the generator builds the schedule on it: each tranche is added on its own date and earns interest from that day, a period with a tranche in it is charged part on the balance before and part on the balance after, and the level instalment is sized again so that, paid on every date left, it retires the balance exactly. A due date before anything is drawn has nothing due. The solve starts on the first drawdown, not the signing date, and every later tranche is an outflow on its date.

While such a facility was partly drawn, interest could have been charged on the sanction or on the drawn balance (open choice O6). Where neither the contract nor its scheme states it, the basis is read from E-Banker's own interest postings in those months, at the rate each posting narrates ("IntDR Fm 02-MAY-25 To 31-MAY-25 @ 29%", counted inclusively), against what each basis would give; the nearer one is the basis E-Banker applied, recorded as `OBSERVED_EBANKER_INTEREST` with both figures in the audit log. With no postings to read the schedule is refused (`INTEREST_BASIS_MISSING`), never assumed. The second option of `partly_drawn_interest_basis` takes the drawn balance everywhere. A facility still partly drawn today with no flags is refused as before.

| Facility | Rate | EIR with no fee, before | EIR with no fee, now | EIR with the fees on record (illustration) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Mphunzitsi SACCO 104430000085 | 29.00 | 41.32 | 29.07 | 35.68 (47.7 million) |
| Nyamunyamu Processors 104430000068 | 29.20 | 40.63 | 29.14 | 32.63 |
| Sycamore Credit 104430000074 | 26.10 | 34.28 | 26.08 | 31.85 |
| Mchinji Civil Service SACCO 104430000086 | 27.20 | 32.50 | 28.64 | 32.49 |
| Namuleri Finance 104460000114 | 10.00 | 10.72 | 10.00 | 12.00 |
| Chipala Investment 104430000071 | 26.10 | 26.29 | 26.06 | no fee on record |

Across the 33 facilities the review listed, every one with a schedule now solves with no fee within 0.2 point of its rate on a monthly loan; the few further away pay quarterly or half-yearly, where the nominal rate at the payment frequency is the monthly rate compounded (Chikwendeni General Dealers: 34.95 percent monthly is 37.68 percent half-yearly). The illustration column treats the arrangement and legal lines of the source of record as integral inside a transaction that is rolled back; no fee has been reviewed.

**E2: the take-on EIRs reach the engine (decision D41).** The take-on build now reads the workbook as a balance chain, line by line in the order the lines stand in the sheet: each line's principal and interest is what falls due on its end date; a rise in the balance the line does not explain (between two lines, or within a line beyond its capitalised interest) is money advanced on that date; a fall outside the instalments is money received. The solve starts on the date the schedule starts charging interest, at the balance it opens with. Why 21 of 73 solves were implausible, case by case:

| Cause | Loans | Whose |
|---|---|---|
| A later tranche, or arrears added to the balance when the loan was rescheduled, never paid out in the vector (Infracon 265.7 million in June 2024: 63.8 percent; Mchinji SACCO 50 million; Kayola, Allied Bricks, Kajikhomele, African Honey, Micholess, Lenziemill, Mwachisosa, Kambewu) | 10 | Dupleix: fixed (the chain) |
| The header principal is the approved amount while the schedule opens at the first drawdown (Ruo Textiles 70 against 9.7 million: -46.5 percent; Mphanga 150 against 54.9 million) | 2 | Dupleix: fixed (the opening balance is the schedule's) |
| Interest-only months with a nil instalment column fell out of the vector (Nsanama: 14.2 against 19.8 percent) | 1 | Dupleix: fixed (principal plus interest on every line) |
| The Loan Book value date is a year before the schedule starts (Steel Base: 2021-01-24 against 2022-01-24, 7.2 percent) | 1 | Tamanda to confirm the date; the solve now runs from the schedule's start and flags the difference |
| Repayments recorded inside the schedule (Jescal 1.8 million in October 2023: 1.8 percent) and rows out of date order (Beaulaland, Msusa) | 3 | Dupleix: fixed (read as receipts; the chain runs in sheet order) |
| The schedule does not retire the loan: 9.2 million still owed at its last line after a repayment of 334.1 million in June 2022 (COMSIV 104430000004) | 1 | Tamanda: the block is an account history; refused |
| 36 instalments typed on one due date in February 2025 (BenCal Technologies 104460000059) | 1 | Tamanda: the dates; refused |
| Floating loans whose workbook carries the PLR moves (Nature's Gift, VNC Bricks, The Food Empire 2022, MUSECO, Edge View): the solve is the rate the schedule charged, not the header rate | 5 | Not an error: plausible once checked against the lines' own rates |

The governed check `takeon_eir_plausibility` (seeded: inside the schedule's own rates, 0.5 point either side) asks the schedule, solved with no fee, to give back the rates its lines charge, and refuses one that does not retire the loan. On the workbook received on 13 October 2026, 71 of the 73 pass and 2 are refused (COMSIV and BenCal), and every passing solve is between 9.98 and 33.31 percent with no fee. A refused block stays on its take-on balance with the reason on the row. The mappings in Mapping column X are still not ticked by Finance; that is a flag on every take-on loan, not a refusal.

The solved take-on EIR is carried into `contract_eir` through the normal path: the workbook schedule arrives on Schedule Review as `PENDING_REVIEW`, is approved like a generated draft (the bootstrap approves it under its own label, never as MAIIC), and the EIR is calculated by the engine on the chain the take-on build checked, then locked by the maker-checker lock. Only the take-on workbook's own fees enter it (a fee on another source after the take-on belongs to a later event), and like every fee they are landed PENDING (139 lines, 280.0 million) and count only once a person has reviewed them. A floating take-on loan's EIR moves each month from the rate its workbook schedule carries (its no-fee rate, recorded on the snapshot as `schedule_rate`) to the rate E-Banker charged (D38). A loan migrated at the take-on is rolled forward from August 2024 from its balance at 31 July 2024 with the arrears of D39; its July 2024 opening legs are balances, not cash.

**E3: the GL fee route (decision D42).** `GlFeeBuildService` (in the bootstrap, and `php artisan eir:build-gl-fees`) joins `GL_04a` to `GL_04b` on the batch and writes one fee line per fee posting into `contract_fees` with the source `E-Banker GL fee posting`, PENDING (section 7.8.4). On the pack of 12 October 2026: 178 fee postings (year-end closing vouchers and deleted rows left out); 51 tied to their loan (445.1 million: 49 the only loan in the batch, 1 by the loan's own debit of the same amount, 1 by the account name in the narration); 127 held (961.5 million), 125 because no loan account is a leg of the batch (fees credited by transfer) and 2 because the only loan is a Mega Farm account. A held line is never guessed: 30 of them (485.9 million) carry a candidate loan, one whose full account name the narration carries and which drew money that day, for a person to tie with a reason (`assignHeld`, audit-logged; the tie survives a rebuild). The fee GL gate agrees on every month GL_04a and the trial balance both hold (39 agree, 0 differ, 12 without a trial balance month to compare), and the 2025 totals are 171,128,135.00 (4871) and 311,342,792.00 (4873), the trial balance to the cent.

`fee_source_precedence` (seeded: GL posting, Finance workbook, LOS capture, Extract A) is built: for each contract the highest source holding a fee wins and the lower lines are marked `superseded_by_source`, kept, shown, never in the EIR, the readiness check or the fee GL check. On the rerun the GL postings are the source for 24 contracts and the LOS charges for 38; 39 LOS lines are superseded and 17 differences between the two are listed for Finance. The take-on workbook's fees never compete. Every line still needs a person's classification and review before it affects an EIR: on a ledger-basis month the cash advanced is taken net of the reviewed integral fees of that month, as the EIR is solved net of them.

**Where the figures stand after the rerun (fees not yet reviewed).** 13 EIRs are locked under the bootstrap label: 11 generated (Chipala 26.06 percent, Ebenezer Midian 27.16, the FInES 10 percent loans at 9.98 to 10.09, MUDI SACCO 34.52) and 2 take-on loans with no fee (Nature's Gift 24.19 against a schedule rate of 23.05 moved to the rate charged; Kambewu 32.27). 107 are blocked, every one by the fee review (`FEE_CLASSIFICATION_PENDING`), 2 of them also by the take-on check (COMSIV, BenCal); 24 have no schedule, as before (16 closed or never drawn, 8 refused on missing terms). Mphunzitsi, Mthirakuwiri 104430000077 and Microloan Foundation 104430000069, locked without fees on 13 October, are now blocked because their GL fees are on record and await review. Amortised cost of the locked loans: 2,788.7 million against a ledger of 2,800.2 million at 31 December 2025 (11 loans, -11.5 million) and 2,432.6 million against 2,408.5 million at 30 September 2026 (12 loans, +24.2 million, of which 104430000061 +26.7 million: E-Banker stopped charging interest when it went non-performing while the gross amortised cost keeps accruing under IFRS 9). EIR interest on them: 503.1 million plus 10.3 million unwind for 2025 against 524.6 million posted, and 366.7 million plus 81.1 million unwind for January to September 2026 against 412.2 million posted.

**Status 14 October 2026:** in the bootstrap the EIR lock is not a maker-checker lock: `eir:bootstrap` calculates and locks each EIR itself with the administrator override (Bootstrap.php:310-315, `lock(..., true)`), and `contract_eir` records the bootstrap user as `locked_by` with no bootstrap label; only the schedule approval carries the label, as its review note (Bootstrap.php:275). Schedule approval has no different-person check either (ScheduleWorkflowService.php:305-319; O18, section 4.2). The figures in this section are from the rerun on `maiic_ifrs9_eircheck` and were not re-run for this note. **Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** schedule approval now reads `schedule_approval_control` (O18): the person who generated a draft (`contract_eir.schedule_prepared_by`) cannot approve it, an approval needs a named person, and the bootstrap approves under its label, recorded in `contract_eir.schedule_approver_label` rather than as a review note (`ScheduleWorkflowService::approve`). The take-on Build is proposed and approved, the bootstrap's under its label (`takeon_build_requests`). The EIR lock in the bootstrap is still the administrator override: recording it under the label needs a label parameter on `EirCalculationService::lock`, which is another work stream's file.

With the fees on record treated as integral (an illustration in a rolled-back transaction, not a review), 118 EIRs lock (71 of them take-on loans, 10.66 to 35.17 percent) and 2 stay refused; amortised cost is 13,334.3 million against a ledger of 13,284.3 million at 31 December 2025 (103 loans, +50.0 million) and 20,332.9 against 19,834.0 million at 30 September 2026 (111 loans, +498.9 million, led by The Food Empire's take-on accounts 104450000068 and 104420000043, whose balances were re-booked to other accounts in December 2025 and May 2026 (status F, section 3.8) while their roll-forward continues, and by Stage 3 loans E-Banker no longer charges); EIR interest is 2,463.7 million plus 43.2 million unwind for 2025 against 2,328.9 million posted, and 3,231.5 plus 563.8 million for January to September 2026 against 3,249.7 million.

### 3.12 The Stage 3 basis follows the stage at the start of the month, and the Stage 3 history (built 15 October 2026)

**The basis.** IFRS 9 5.4.1(b) applies the EIR to the amortised cost (gross less the loss allowance) of an asset that has become credit-impaired "in subsequent reporting periods", and 5.4.2 returns to the gross carrying amount in the periods after it cures. The revenue engine (`EirRevenueService::run`) therefore takes the basis of a month from the loan's stage at the **start** of the month, the prior month-end's loan-book row (`stageAtStart`): the month a loan enters Stage 3 accrues on gross; the net basis starts the month after, on the allowance booked at that month-end; the month after a cure is still net. A loan with no prior row (its first month) starts performing, on gross. Until 15 October 2026 the month's own end stage decided, which netted the entry month on an allowance measured while the loan was still performing, and refused the month where that allowance did not exist. Tests: `EirRevenueServiceTest`.

**The Stage 3 history.** Before December 2025 the book held 3 or 4 Stage 2 loans at each PD window start, below the governed minimum of 10 loans and 1 default, so every month's PD failed closed whole (`segment_parameter_runs`, failed) and no loan carried an allowance; the revenue engine then refused every Stage 3 month, and 34 locked loans stopped accruing. A Stage 3 loan is in default: its PD is 1 whatever the transitions. `ifrs9:stage3-history {from} {to}` books, for each month that holds no ECL, on MAIIC's own book (Mega Farm aside): the LGD by portfolio (`LgdSegmentationService`, unchanged, failing closed when thin), the PD on the Stage 3 loans only (`PdSegmentationService::run(..., ['3'])`: PD 1, the run recorded with its scope), and their ECL discounted at the EIR (`TimePhasedEclService` on the pre-FLI PD, which needs no scenario set; with PD 1 every scenario gives the same figure; projection run `TIME_PHASED_V1_STAGE3_HISTORY`). Stage 1 and 2 loans of those months keep no PD and no ECL: the month is not a full allowance and is never presented as one. `eir:bootstrap` runs it for the months before the first month with a full PD, before the revenue. On a copy of production (15 October 2026), July 2024 to November 2025 booked 24 to 50 Stage 3 loans a month (2,524 million in November 2025 against 2,566 million on the full engine in December 2025); with the start-of-month basis every locked loan then accrues to its last month (37 stopped before), and 2025 EIR interest rises from 1,591.2 million on 80 loans to 1,974.7 million on 101.

## 4. Decisions, and the Governance Centre

### 4.1 Agreed this week

**Status 14 October 2026, numbering:** version 3's decisions D1 to D21 still apply where this document is silent, but version 3's own D20 (the interest acceptance test, now section 9) and D21 (the test-suite rule) are retired: the numbers D20 and D21 below are new decisions of version 4 and do not continue version 3's. D23 stands at the end of the table out of numerical order. Version 3's D8 (`eir_rate_periods`) was never built and is superseded by D38 (the EIR accrued at is recorded on each roll-forward row, `eir_annual`), and its D14 (cash from the loan book) is superseded by D35 (cash from the E-Banker ledger).

| # | Decision | By | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| D20 | **The E-Banker record is the contractual record** (O19). The engine reads terms, rates, fees and cash flows from E-Banker. The signed offer letter is evidence; a difference between letter and system is reported to Credit as a control exception, never booked as a modification. | Dupleix, 7 Oct 2026; Dr Thom to confirm in writing | The signed terms were never keyed into E-Banker (F14), while the system now carries fees, margins and cash flows in full. It is the only record the engine can read completely. The twelve offer letters requested from Credit become test evidence, not a data source. |
| D21 | **Every open choice of version 3 is a governed setting** with Dupleix's recommendation seeded as the value in force from 1 January 2025. Dr Thom confirms or changes each one in the Governance Centre when he is ready, with maker-checker and an effective date, and the engine records which value every month was run under. | Dupleix, 7 Oct 2026, at Edward's request | Nothing waits on a meeting. A choice that is changed later applies forward only; a month already run keeps the settings it was run under. |
| D22 | **E-Banker is ingested through a landing zone and the monthly loan book is derived from the ledger** (section 6): raw tables that mirror E-Banker, loaded append-only by whichever of five routes MAIIC uses (three built, section 6.5), with gates that refuse a pack that does not tie, and one re-runnable build by any of three methods, the bootstrap of the stored report, the derivation from the ledger, or the printed report importer MAIIC uses today, chosen at any time. | Dupleix, 7 Oct 2026; the landing zone and the derivation added the same day at Edward's direction, the bootstrap and the report importer kept as options | The ledger is the primary record and ties to every other table; the report starts only in December 2024 and stores every re-run. Every derived figure traces to raw rows an auditor can open. |
| D24 | **The Dupleix-suite layout is adopted for the user interface** (section 11): the six working groups with their colours, the icon rail, the page header with breadcrumb, the period chip, and light and dark mode as a per-user setting, as the Dupleix suite builds them. Routes, permissions and calculations are unchanged; screens move to where the suite puts them. | Edward, 7 Oct 2026 | One shape of screen across every Dupleix system; the EIR screens of P8 are placed in it from the start |
| D25 | **Dupleix's compliance audit workbooks are adopted** (section 12): five workbooks (IFRS 9 EIR, IFRS 9 impairment, IFRS 7 and IAS 1, RBM classification, Contract Schedule 1), each row naming the governance setting that governs it and the test that proves it; a register in the Governance Centre where MAIIC signs; an auditor's pack per period in the Report Hub. | Edward, 7 Oct 2026 | Deloitte walks from paragraph to screen to test; the status counts are the project's status in one line |
| D26 | **One-command bootstrap from committed inputs** (section 6.10): the E-Banker pack, the data-dictionary results, the trial balances, the take-on workbook and the queries are committed under `docs/bootstrap/` with a manifest; `eir:bootstrap` builds a clean install, runs every engine in dependency order and verifies the result against the baselines and MAIIC's golden numbers. | Edward, 7 Oct 2026 | Any server, including UAT and Deloitte's copy, is reproducible from nothing; the bootstrap is also the end-to-end test of the data foundation |
| D27 | **Macro statistics are ingested from the World Bank and the IMF the way the suite does it** (section 13): indicator codes on the series, a fetcher and a parser, preview before commit, a batch of provenance on every observation, a command for the bootstrap and the scheduler, the Reserve Bank series by file, and a governed rule for which source wins. The existing tables and the six forward-looking screens are kept. | Edward, 7 Oct 2026 | Forward-looking information with its source, address and time against every figure, which is what B5.5.49 to B5.5.54 and Deloitte ask for |
| D28 | **The forward-looking adjustment gains a correlation finder, a repaired regression, a manual overlay route and lineage on the loan** (section 14); the arithmetic that applies the adjustment to PDs and feeds the ECL is unchanged. | Edward, 7 Oct 2026 | Which series explains MAIIC's losses is found, tested and approved by two people; judgement has a governed road; every post-FLI PD says where it came from |
| D29 | **The scenario set is a governed object per period and the ECL is weighted across scenarios** (section 15): three or more scenarios with narratives, weights, source vintage, calibration to Malawi's own history, shocks on the base path, two approvals, a lock, back-tests and sensitivity; the weighting moves from the macro path to the loss. | Edward, 7 Oct 2026 | IFRS 9 B5.5.42 asks for the probability-weighted loss over a range of outcomes; IFRS 7.35G asks for the disclosure; the auditors ask for the governance |
| D30 | **The Mega Farm programme loans stay outside the EIR engine and go fully into the ECL module** (section 16): they are Government money that MAIIC runs on its behalf, at 15 percent of which 5 is MAIIC's and 10 the fund's; 95 percent are in default and the losses are the fund's. The engine works out MAIIC's 5 percent share on the amount expected to be recovered, the ECL module stages and provides for the loans against the fund, and the Report Hub produces the Mega Farms notes of the financial statements. | Dupleix, 8 Oct 2026, on the 2025 financial statements; Dr Thom to confirm (O22) | The statements present the programme ring-fenced on every page; an EIR on a programme split would restate nothing they report, and the revenue-shift number is about MAIIC's own lending |
| D31 | **The staging thresholds follow the RBM DFI directive and reproduce the 2025 model** (section 3.6): Stage 3 at 181 days past due for medium- and long-term facilities, 91 for short-term and the Mega Farm programme; Stage 2 at 31 days; the 90-day presumption rebutted in writing on the directive; the four-consecutive-payments trigger and the days-past-due basis governed beside them; the 2026 policy note to say so. | Dupleix, 8 Oct 2026; Dr Thom to sign the rebuttal (O13) | The policy note says 90 days, the model applied 181 and the statements rest on it; the gap is closed by documenting the rebuttal, not by moving the number |
| D32 | **The revenue roll-forward's first month opens on the amortised cost at the end of the month before** (section 3.10): the present value at that date of the flows still to come. | Dupleix, 13 Oct 2026 | Taking it at the end of the month itself counted that month's interest twice. |
| D33 | **A facility still drawing down, or lent within the month, runs on the ledger's drawn balance** (section 3.10): the opening is the drawn balance (nil when lent in the month) and each later tranche is added in the month it is drawn, with interest from that day; a schedule-basis loan never adds a tranche.
