The basis is recorded on every row. | Dupleix, 13 Oct 2026 | The schedule assumes every tranche on day one; Mphunzitsi SACCO was overstated by 216 million at August 2026. |
| D34 | **A prepaid loan opens on the ledger balance** when the ledger at the end of the month before is below half the present value of the remaining schedule; the substitution is audit-logged with both figures. | Dupleix, 13 Oct 2026 | Barnet Agriculture repaid 55.6 million in November 2025; the schedule's value was 38.8 million against 0.4 million owed. |
| D35 | **The actual cash of every loan is loaded from the E-Banker ledger** (`eir:load-ledger-cash`, source `EBANKER_LEDGER` in `eir_actual_transactions`) and is part of the month-end job, so the roll-forward uses receipts, not the schedule, wherever the ledger covers the month. A customer who has paid more than the ledger says is owed is shown as a credit balance, not a negative loan. | Dupleix, 13 Oct 2026 | EIR as at a date read the schedule where the ledger held the receipts. |
| D36 | **Every client is linked to its loans** (`clients:link-loan-book`, by external identity and then by name, recorded on the client) and **each import screen shows only its own import history** (`imports.kind`). | Edward, 13 Oct 2026 | Clients were not linked to the loan book; every import screen listed every import. |
| D37 | **Maintenance is seven jobs, as in the Stress Testing suite** (`maiic:task`): month-end (link clients, stage, PD by segment, forward-looking month-end, ledger cash, revenue run, Mega Farm ECL where present), refresh data, health check, update and bootstrap behind a typed confirmation, with a run history. | Edward, 13 Oct 2026 | The tabs were overwhelming; the month-end order is now one button. |
| D38 | **A floating-rate loan's EIR is re-estimated each month with the rate E-Banker charged** (section 3.10, IFRS 9 B5.4.5): the locked nominal EIR plus the change from the rate the schedule was built on to the month's loan-book rate; a fixed-rate loan keeps its locked EIR; each row records the EIR it accrued at. | Edward, 13 Oct 2026 | The floating loans' schedules were generated at today's rate, not the rate lent at; the roll-forward ran 479.5 million below the ledger with D39. |
| D39 | **Arrears at the opening are booked as arrears** (sections 3.10 and 6.9): where the ledger at the end of the month before owes more than the balance the schedule expects, by more than one percent, the difference is added to the opening, undiscounted, recorded on the row and in the audit log. | Edward, 13 Oct 2026 | The opening assumed every instalment had been paid; Chipala had paid 152.2 million of 219.5 million due. |
| D40 | **A facility drawn in tranches is scheduled and solved on its drawdowns** (section 3.11, E1): each tranche added on its own date, the instalment sized again at each one, the solve from the first drawdown; a fee debit is part of its drawdown, not a tranche; while partly drawn with no stated basis, the interest basis is read from E-Banker's own postings, else refused. | Edward, 14 Oct 2026 | Mphunzitsi SACCO solved to 41.3 percent against 29.0 with no fee; it now solves to 29.1. |
| D41 | **The take-on EIR is solved on the workbook's balance chain, checked against the rates its lines charge, and carried into the EIR engine** (sections 3.11 and 6.9, E2) through schedule review and the maker-checker lock, with the take-on fees once reviewed; a block that fails the governed check (`takeon_eir_plausibility`) stays on its take-on balance with the reason. | Edward, 14 Oct 2026 | 21 of 73 solves were implausible; 19 were the engine's reading of the workbook. 71 now pass, 2 are refused for Tamanda. |
| D42 | **The GL fee postings are the source of record for a loan's fees** (sections 3.11 and 7.8, E3): GL_04 tied to the loan by the batch, the rest held with the reason and a candidate for a person to tie, `fee_source_precedence` marking the lower sources superseded; every line still reviewed before it reaches an EIR. | Edward, 14 Oct 2026 | 180.8 million of 2025 fees on eight loans never reached the EIR; six of those loans were locked without them. |
| D23 | **The three MAIIC extract scripts are retired.** The engine's importers read the tables directly, through CSVs produced with the session settings of the 6 October request (ISO dates, point decimal). | Dupleix, 7 Oct 2026 | Removes the three script faults of section 2.1 at source. |

**Status 14 October 2026, by decision:**

- **D20:** the data path is built (every term, rate, fee and cash flow is read from E-Banker's tables), but the setting `contractual_record` is read by no engine, and Dr Thom's written confirmation is still owed (section 5).
- **D21:** the defaults are seeded effective from inception, 1900-01-01 (GovernanceSettingsSeeder.php:28; the rows first seeded from 1 January 2025 were backdated by the migration of 9 October 2026), as approved technical defaults so that every month can be run; they are not a historical approval by MAIIC. 20 of the 61 settings are read by no engine yet (section 4.2). The test asked for by review item R20, that the table of 4.2 matches `GovernanceService::catalogue()`, is not built.
- **D22:** routes 1 to 3 are built and routes 4 and 5 are not (section 6.5). The gates refuse a pack on its accounts, numbers, counts, hashes and query versions, but the balance-history tie, the month-end rows and the ISO-date check only warn (PackGates.php:121-273, section 6.4). Method C is built for the command line only (`eir:build-loan-books --method=C --file=`); the feed screen's Build cannot take the report file (section 6.8).
- **D26:** built, but `--verify` asserts five kinds of check, not the section 9 baselines and the golden numbers of 6.10.2 (section 6.10).
- **D30:** the programme is kept out of MAIIC's own PD, LGD and ECL (`ifrs9:segment-portfolios`, 14 October 2026) and its ECL is booked by `MegaFarmEclService` as EAD x PD x LGD x MAIIC's share. The interest split, MAIIC's 5 percent receivable, the fund liability and the Mega Farm notes of section 16 are not built; the treatment waits for Dr Thom (O22).
- **D31:** built: the thresholds are seeded by tenor class with the directive's section on every row, the four-payment trigger and the days-past-due basis are governed and read (section 3.6). The rebuttal is not yet signed (O13).
- **D34:** built (EirRevenueService.php:283-310); no test names it.
- **D37:** Maintenance has seven jobs (MaintenanceController.php:40-77): bootstrap, update, build the loan book, month-end, recalibrate the forward-looking model, fetch new data, check the system. The month-end runs, in order (MaintenanceTask.php:43-52): link clients, the programme portfolios (the Mega Farm schemes kept apart), stage, PD and LGD by segment, the forward-looking month-end (route, projection, the ECL discounted at the EIR, the month record), ledger cash, EIR revenue, then the Mega Farm ECL where the month has Mega Farm loans: the ECL runs before the revenue, so Stage 3 revenue nets the allowance. **Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** the month-end now also loads the scheme settings and the over-sanction flag first (`eir:load-schemes`) and builds the GL fee postings with the fee GL gate after the ledger cash (`eir:build-gl-fees`), MaintenanceTask.php.

### 4.2 Decided and seeded: every setting, its default, and where it stands

The Governance Centre now holds 28 settings. The first 13 were there from P1 and P4; the 15 that follow were added on 7 October. "Status" says whether the value is agreed, decided on evidence, or Dupleix's recommendation awaiting Dr Thom.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the catalogue (`GovernanceService::catalogue()`) now holds **61 settings**; the table below lists 51 of them and the 10 it lacked are added at its foot. Every one is seeded as an approved technical default effective from 1900-01-01 (D21). Not every setting is yet used: **20 are read by no engine code** and are marked "Not yet read by the engine" below (`plr_mid_period`, `reset_trigger`, `rate_source_precedence`, `margin_basis`, `cash_source`, `manual_policy_handling`, `modification_threshold`, `counter_reset_handling`, `contractual_record`, `expected_cashflow_basis`, `history_before_dec_2025`, `trueup_gl_account`, `auditor_export_format`, `keyman_insurance_treatment`, `nascomex_preference_shares`, `staging_rebuttal`, `rate_change_classification`, `schedule_approval_control`, `historic_materiality_assessment`, `fee_reclass_journal`). Changing one of them changes no figure until its engine is built; the period lock still records its value. "Decided and seeded" in the heading also covers the rows marked Built or To build (review item Cp4). Several settings that are read fall back to a value in code when no approved value exists, instead of stopping as the Governance Centre requires: `dpd_basis` (LoanBookBuildService.php:630-634), the three Mega Farm settings (MegaFarmEclService.php:90-92 and 255-262), `fli_adjustment_route` and `scenario_weighting_method` (FliRouteService.php:114-115 and 396-403) and the four scenario-set rules (ScenarioSetService.php:45-61). **Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** those readers now stop with a named reason when no value is approved (`FliRouteService::setting`, `LoanBookBuildService::methodInForce` and the `dpd_basis` read, the three Mega Farm settings, whose share is now declined rather than taken as 100 percent when the scope states none, and the scenario rules, which also read "No bounds beyond summing to 100" and "Not required" as they say; they had been read as 40 and 60 and as required). `schedule_approval_control` is read and enforced. The 17 settings still read by no engine (the list above less `schedule_approval_control`, `modification_threshold` and `rate_change_classification`, which the restructuring work reads) are marked "recorded, not yet applied" in the Governance Centre with what the engine does instead (`GovernanceService::notYetApplied`); `cash_source` and `manual_policy_handling` are among them: the engine reads cash from the ledger (D35) and treats policy M as fixed (7.2), and wiring the latter waits on O4 and review item R14.

Seven of these need a genuine choice from Dr Thom rather than a confirmation: the true-up account (O10), the derecognition threshold (O16), reset or modification (O17), the three Phase 0 sign-offs (O13), the fee journal (O21) and Mega Farms (O22). The rest are what the data shows.

Two things are not settings and are tracked elsewhere: the materiality threshold *amount* (O20), which becomes a governed amount once Finance gives the figure; and the fourteen open items that are facts for people to supply (section 5).

## 6. Ingesting E-Banker: the landing zone and the feed

### 6.1 In plain language

Until now the system has been fed by hand: a report printed from E-Banker, saved through Excel, uploaded once a month. Version 3 proposed loading the stored Loan Book Report table instead. The follow-up extracts showed something better is possible: the ledger itself, the primary record of every posting, ties to every other table we hold, so the monthly loan book can be **derived from the ledger** rather than copied from a report. And because the extracts are now ordinary queries with known keys, they can arrive by whichever road MAIIC finds convenient, from a file Barry uploads to a scheduled read over the VPN, without changing anything downstream.

The design has three parts. A **landing zone**: raw tables that mirror E-Banker's, loaded exactly as received and never edited. A **build** of the monthly loan book from the landing zone by one of three methods, all kept and all available at any time: the **bootstrap**, which takes the stored Loan Book Report's latest run for each month as it is; the **derivation**, which builds the row from the ledger; and the **report importer**, the existing upload of the printed Loan Book Report as an Excel file, which MAIIC has used every month until now; one command, re-runnable whenever a rule or a source row changes. And a **feed**: the one door through which every pack of extracts enters, whichever road it came by, with gates that refuse a pack that does not tie.

### 6.2 The three build methods

**Method A, the bootstrap.** The loan book row is the stored Loan Book Report's latest run for that account and month-end (`ebanker_loan_book_runs`, highest row id per account-month), column for column: E-Banker's own carrying amount, principal, interest to date, repayments, arrears, status and rate, mapped once to `loan_books` through a saved template. It is the simplest method, it is what MAIIC's own report says, and it is already tied to the ledger on 2,264 of 2,264 account-months. It covers December 2024 onward, which is where the stored report begins; the months from the take-on at 31 July 2024 to November 2024 are built from the balance history and the ledger and marked as derived.

**Method B, the derivation.** For every account and every month-end from the take-on to the latest month, the row is built from the primary records as follows.

| Loan book field | Source | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Carrying amount, principal, interest to date, cumulative repayments, disbursed | The ledger's running balance and its postings by type | The primary record; proven against the balance history (2,533 of 2,549) and the stored loan book (2,264 of 2,264) |
| Approved amount, value and maturity dates, tenor, product, GL, customer | The loan master and account master | The record of the facility |
| Rate | The rate charged, from the stored loan book's rate column, with the PLR and the rate set-up as the register of changes (section 3.2) | The rate actually posted |
| Arrears buckets, overdue days, status, segment | The stored loan book's latest run for that month-end | E-Banker's own arrears arithmetic depends on its instalment chart, which is unreliable to reproduce, and these are the figures MAIIC reports to RBM |
| Undisbursed commitment | Approved less disbursed, checked against the disbursement schedule | Both sources held |

Under method B every month from July 2024 is built the same way, and beside every derived carrying amount the stored loan book's figure is kept; a difference is a flagged row, never a silent choice.

**Status 14 October 2026:** built as described, with two changes. The overdue days are the report's except where three named corrections apply (past maturity; nothing due yet on the chart in force; a chart that dates several instalments on one day), LoanBookBuildService.php:557-620 and section 3.6. A write-off is never counted as a repayment (`WriteOffClassifier`, section 3.8). On the fresh bootstrap of 13 October method B agrees with the stored report on 2,495 of 2,495 in-scope account-months from July 2024 to September 2026; the Mega Farm accounts carry the report's figure (section 6.6).

**Method C, the report importer.** The printed Loan Book Report (Menu ID 3868), uploaded as the Excel file MAIIC produces today, through the importer that already exists (`ProcessLoanImportJob`), unchanged in what it reads. Two things are added so that it lives inside the same design: the parsed rows are landed in `ebanker_loan_book_reports` with the file's hash and load id before `loan_books` is written, so a month loaded this way traces to its file like any other; and the file passes the date gate of 6.4 (every date unambiguous, the row named on failure), because this is the route on which Excel scrambled dates in September. It needs no query and no access to the database, which is its value: a month can be loaded from the report alone when no pack exists, by whoever has the report. It gives the report's figures, like method A, for the months the report was run.

**Status 14 October 2026:** method C is built for a CSV of the report, not the Excel file: `eir:build-loan-books {period} --method=C --file=<csv>` (LoanBookBuildService::buildFromReport, LoanBookBuildService.php:174-270) passes the d/m/Y date gate, lands the rows verbatim in `ebanker_raw_rows` (query `LBR`, route `METHOD_C_REPORT`) with the file's hash and load, then runs `ProcessLoanImportJob` and stamps the rows with the method and the load. There is no `ebanker_loan_book_reports` table (section 6.3), and the feed screen's Build cannot take the file (section 6.8).

**Choosing between them.** The method in force is a Governance Centre setting (`loan_book_build_method`, section 4.2) with the three methods as its options, changed under maker-checker with an effective date; a month is built by the method in force on its period end, and the method used is recorded on every row. All three read from the landing zone and pass its gates, so switching changes no route and no screen. Dupleix's recommendation is method B, because it covers every month from the take-on on one basis and traces every figure to a posting; method A is the right choice while the derivation is being proven, for a quick reload of a single month, or whenever MAIIC prefers the report's own figures to stand; method C is the right choice for a month with no pack, or if the feed is ever interrupted, since it needs nothing but the report. Whichever is in force, the others remain available on the feed screen for a named month.

### 6.3 The landing zone

One raw table, `ebanker_raw_rows`, holds every source verbatim (each row's columns as JSON), keyed by the query id and the source table's own key and versioned (a row that arrives again with different content is a new version and the earlier one keeps `superseded_at`); `ebanker_loads` and `ebanker_pack_files` record each pack and file, and `ebanker_queries` holds the versioned queries (migration 2026_10_08_000000_create_ebanker_landing_zone). The table names below are logical tables inside `ebanker_raw_rows`, distinguished by query id, not physical tables (corrected 14 October 2026; it said "Raw tables that mirror E-Banker column for column, under their own names, loaded append-only").

| Table | Mirrors | Key | Loaded |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ebanker_ledger` | CUMVOUCH (`P1_01`) | `CUMVOUCH_DET_ID` | Incrementally by key, plus a re-pull of the last two months to catch back-dated postings and deletion flags |
| `ebanker_balance_history` | ACCOUNT_BALANCE (`P2_09`) | `ACCOUNT_BAL_MST_ID` | Incrementally by key |
| `ebanker_loan_book_runs` | LOAN_BOOK_DETAILS_ALL (`P2_08`) | `LOAN_BOOK_DET_ID_A` | Incrementally by key; every run kept, the latest per account-month used |
| `ebanker_account_master`, `ebanker_loan_master`, `ebanker_rate_setup`, `ebanker_plr_master`, `ebanker_charges`, `ebanker_disbursement_schedule`, `ebanker_status_history` | `P1_02`, `P1_03`, `P1_04`, `P2_06`, `P2_07`, `P3_12`, `P3_13` | Their own ids | Whole each time; they are small |
| `ebanker_raw_rows`, query `TB_01`, route `TRIAL_BALANCE_FILE` (TrialBalanceLandingService.php:37-38) (corrected 14 October 2026; it said "`ebanker_trial_balances`") | The monthly trial balance files as received from Finance (20 to date), the AFS bridge workbook and the mapping to the audited accounts | File hash and row number | One table per file; every GL line exactly as printed, with its cumulative year-to-date balance. `gl_trial_balance_lines`, the GL side of the reconciliation, is derived from it on build; the year-to-date rule of section 7.7 is applied on read, never stored |
| `ebanker_raw_rows`, query `LBR`, route `METHOD_C_REPORT` (corrected 14 October 2026; it said "`ebanker_loan_book_reports`") | The printed Loan Book Report as uploaded (method C) | File hash and row number | One table per upload; the parsed rows exactly as read |
| `ebanker_loads` | The packs themselves | Pack hash | One row per pack: period, route, who, when, the manifest, the gate results, the watermark after loading |

Three rules. **Nothing is overwritten**: a source row that arrives again with different content (a back-dated change, a deleted flag set) is stored as a new version with its load date, so a month can be re-derived exactly as it looked at the time. **Every row carries its pack**: the hash of the file it came from and the load id, so every derived figure traces to raw rows an auditor can open. **The zone is read by the build only**: no screen edits it.

### 6.4 The pack: one contract for every route

A pack is a set of files, one per query, plus the month's trial balance from Finance, plus a manifest. The manifest (`manifest.json`) records the period, the run timestamp, the session settings used (ISO dates, point decimal, the RUN 0 settings of the 6 October request) and, for each file, the query id, the query version, the row count and the SHA-256 hash.

- **The queries are versioned in the system**, not in an email. Data Foundation, E-Banker Feed, Queries holds the SQL text of each extract with a version number and a checksum, and hands Barry the file to run. A column added later is a new version; the manifest says which version produced each file.
- **Watermarks.** For each incremental table the system records the last key loaded; the query for the next pack is "where the key is greater than the watermark", plus the two-month re-pull. Masters come whole.
- **Gates, run before anything is derived.** Dates parse as ISO throughout; numerics parse; counts and hashes match the manifest; the query versions are ones the system knows; every ledger account exists in the account master; the balance history ties to the ledger running balance at every month-end in the pack; every in-scope account has a month-end row; the month's movement on each fee GL in `GL_04a` equals the trial balance's movement on that GL (section 3.7), since the account ledger alone does not carry every fee (this gate is new on 11 October 2026 and is built with `GL_04` in P4b). A failure refuses the pack with the file and row named; the raw rows are kept in quarantine against the load; nothing downstream moves. (**Status 14 October 2026:** the numbers, counts, hashes, query versions and accounts-in-the-master gates refuse the pack (level ERROR); the ISO-date check, the balance-history tie and the month-end-row check only warn and never refuse (PackGates.php:121-144, 199-228, 232-273). The fee GL check is not a landing gate: it is run as a report by the GL fee route on every build (`GlFeeBuildService`, `eir:build-gl-fees`, section 3.11) and refuses nothing.)
- **The fee control is a standing monthly query** (added 11 October 2026). `GL_04a` takes every posting to 4871, 4873, 4874 and 4875 since the watermark, and `GL_04b` every other leg of the same batches, which shows the loan account or bank side of each fee. Both are in the register and in pack 4; from the October month-end they join `M09_01` to `M09_03` in every monthly pack.
- **A large extract may come in parts.** Where SQL Developer cannot hold a result (the Mega Farm ledger, `MF_03`, stopped at 107 of about 3,685 accounts on 9 October), the run sheet splits it by account range into parts that each export from the grid. The parts are named `MF_03_ledger_a.csv` to `MF_03_ledger_g.csv`, so that both a hand-written manifest and the route 2 script (which reads the query id from the first two parts of the file name) put every part under `MF_03`; rows are keyed on `CUMVOUCH_DET_ID`, so the parts neither overlap nor need a merge. The counts come in `CHECK_MF_03_row_counts.csv`, a check file that is never landed.

### 6.5 The five routes

The printed report is not a route: it enters by method C with no pack, and is the one way in that needs no query. Every route produces the same pack and enters by the same door; the ingester does not know which road a pack came by, and moving from one route to another changes nothing downstream. Routes 1, 2 and 3 are built; routes 4 and 5 are specified but not built (status at 13 October 2026, below). **MAIIC chooses the route in force at any time**: it is a Governance Centre setting (`ebanker_feed_route`, section 4.2) with the five routes as its options, changed under maker-checker with an effective date like every other setting. The route in force is the one the scheduler runs; the others stay available, so a manual pack can always be loaded, for a missed month or a correction, even while the scheduled read is live. The order below is the order in which MAIIC can have them, not an order of preference.

| Route | How the pack is produced and delivered | What it needs | Place |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1. Manual pack** | Barry runs the versioned queries in SQL Developer with the RUN 0 settings, zips the CSVs, uploads them on the E-Banker Feed screen; the screen builds the manifest from the files and runs the gates | Nothing new: it is what happened on 7 October, made repeatable | Now. Loads the history (section 6.7) and serves any month until route 2 is in place |
| **2. Scheduled export at MAIIC** | A SQLcl or SQL\*Plus script Dupleix writes, parameterised by period and watermark, run by Windows Task Scheduler on a MAIIC server on the first working day after month-end; it writes the pack to an SFTP folder or a network share; the system's scheduler polls the folder and ingests | A read-only Oracle account for the script; one folder; Barry to install the task | The monthly mode. Weeks, not months |
| **3. Push by API** | The same script posts the pack to an endpoint on the system with an API token over HTTPS; no shared folder | A token, outbound HTTPS from the MAIIC server | The alternative to route 2 where a shared folder is awkward |
| **4. Direct read over the VPN** | The system connects to E-Banker's Oracle database read-only (Laravel's OCI8 driver with the Oracle Instant Client), runs the versioned queries itself on schedule and writes straight into the landing zone; the pack and manifest are generated internally, so the audit trail is identical | Read-only credentials over the VPN already in place (the dictionary query DD_18 confirmed what a read-only account can see); the vendor's consent; Dr Thom's authorisation | The destination, and the answer to O9. When ICT agrees |
| **5. Vendor view or API** | Virtual Galaxy exposes a database view or an endpoint carrying the same columns; route 4 reads it | A paid change request to the vendor | Only if route 4 is refused |

**Status at 13 October 2026.** Route 1 is built and in use: the packs of 7, 8, 9 and 12 October were landed by it (Customer & Loan Data, Imports & Feeds, E-Banker Loads). Route 2 is built (`eir:poll-feed-folder`, run daily at 06:30 by the scheduler) and waits for MAIIC's side: the export script on a MAIIC server and the shared folder. Route 3 is built (`POST /api/ebanker-feed/pack` with a bearer token; the poller lands what it receives) and waits for the token to be issued. Route 4 is not built: the route name exists in the code, but there is no Oracle connection, driver or query runner; it needs ICT to agree read-only credentials over the VPN and the Oracle Instant Client on the server. Route 5 is not built and is a paid change request to the vendor. Until ICT agrees route 4, route 1 serves every month and route 2 is the next step.

**Status 14 October 2026, route by route (review items Cp6 and R21):**

- **Route 1, manual pack:** queries registered (`ebanker_queries`); upload built (E-Banker Feed, Land); in use; proven end to end on the packs of 7, 8, 9 and 12 October, which the bootstrap lands.
- **Route 2, scheduled export:** poller built and scheduled (`eir:poll-feed-folder --also-route-3`, daily at 06:30, Kernel.php:29); exporter only a template (`docs/bootstrap/queries/Route 2 - scheduled export (SQLcl) - template.sql`); MAIIC's server task, read-only account and folder not configured; never run end to end. **Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** the template now also spools `P2_10`, `P3_11` and `RS_02a` each month, so the instalment chart history staging reads does not stop at the last manual pack.
- **Route 3, push by API:** endpoint built (`POST /api/ebanker-feed/pack`, routes/web.php); token not issued (`EBANKER_FEED_TOKEN`); never run end to end.
- **Route 4, direct read:** not built (a route name only, `ROUTE_4_DIRECT`); no Oracle driver, connection or query runner; needs ICT and Dr Thom.
- **Route 5, vendor view:** not built (`ROUTE_5_VENDOR` name only); a paid change request.

**Finance's trial balance** is not an E-Banker pack and has its own door: EIR & Revenue Recognition, EIR Data, **Trial Balances** (built 13 October 2026). Finance uploads the month's E-Banker trial balance (xls or xlsx); it is landed through the landing zone's gates (a known month, debits equal to credits, the report's grand total) with its hash, a file already landed is not loaded twice, a corrected file for a month replaces that month's lines and keeps the old version, and months loaded before the landing zone existed are left as they are. The command `eir:import-trial-balances` and the bootstrap use the same service.

Whichever route is in use, the system never writes to E-Banker; the Oracle role is read-only; credentials live in the environment file, not in code; and the extracts carry customer data, so route 2's folder and route 3's endpoint are restricted to the two systems and logged.

### 6.6 Building the loan book

`eir:build-loan-books {from} {to} {--method=bootstrap|derive|report}` builds `loan_books` for the months asked, from the landing zone, by the method in force unless one is named (6.2). It is idempotent on account and reporting period, re-runnable at any time, and run under maker-checker from the feed screen: one person asks for the build, a second approves it, and the audit log records the method and the pack hashes it read. Rebuilding a month already run, by any method, prints every difference first; a locked period is never restated. The ECL columns on `loan_books` (stage, LGD, forward-looking adjustments) are not touched by any of the three methods.

**Mega Farm accounts in the loan book (13 October 2026).** Pack 4 landed the Mega Farm Loan Book Report (`MF_05`) and ledger (`MF_03`), so the build now carries the programme's accounts too (about 3,700 a month, on GLs 1050301 to 1050307; in January 2025 about 56.0bn of a 64.5bn book). Method B reads their own ledger for them, but `MF_03` does not yet rebuild the balance E-Banker reports (in January 2025 it derives about 1.5bn less across 3,668 accounts), so for a Mega Farm account the report's carrying amount stands and the ledger-derived figure and the difference are kept on the row (`build_flag`, `build_basis.ledger_carrying`). The programme stays outside the EIR (D30): the EIR, staging of the EIR population and the other ledger readers do not read `MF_03`. The section 9 tie of method B against the stored report covers the in-scope loan GLs; the Mega Farm gap is reported beside it, not asserted, until the vendor or Finance explains it.

**Status 14 October 2026: the Mega Farm gap explained and closed.** The gap was E-Banker's interest split. Each month-end E-Banker posts a Mega Farm loan's 15 percent interest in one INTP batch of three legs, all on the loan account (`NEW_AC_NUMBER`) and the loan's GL (`AC_GLCODE`): 33 percent (type 303, "BANK_INT 33%") debited to the loan's own interest-receivable GL (105031 to 105037), 67 percent (type 400, "GOVT_INT 67% Int Ac" or "GOVT_INT_PORTION 67%") debited to the programme's government-portion receivable GL (1059 to 1063 and 1065), and that leg's contra (type 401, "GOVT_INT 67%") credited to the fund on GL 2071. Because the reader keeps a posting by its `AC_GLCODE`, all three legs reached the build, and the 401 contra netted the 67 percent leg to nil: the ledger carried 33 percent of the interest while the Loan Book Report carries the full 15 percent (in January 2025 the 1060 legs were 753.8m against a 753.8m gap on 1050301; in September 2026, 6,841.0m against 6,841.0m). In `MF_03` every one of the 75,039 live 400 legs pairs with a 401 leg of the same account, date, batch and amount. Method B now leaves the contra out (it is the fund's side of the entry, not the loan's), so a Mega Farm carrying amount is the ledger with its government-portion legs; the legs are found by their type and narration on the account, and the GLs they were booked to are read from the legs (`build_basis.mega_farm.government_portion_gls`, `contra_gls`), not from a list in code (LoanBookBuildService::TYPE_GOVERNMENT_PORTION, `isGovernmentPortion`). Each Mega Farm row records the split for D30: `interest_own_gl` (33 percent), `interest_government_portion` (67 percent) and `interest_total`, to date and for the month, with the contra left out. On a copy of the 13 October bootstrap with pack 4 (`maiic_ifrs9_mfgap`), 77,842 of the 78,023 Mega Farm account-months with a report run (99.8 percent) now tie to the report to the cent (483 did before). Of the other 181, 180 are a sign error in the report: 60 overpaid accounts whose May, June and July 2026 runs print the ledger's credit balance as a debit (the ledger-derived balance is exactly minus the report's figure; the April and August runs agree with the ledger; 687.4m a month). Decision of 14 October 2026 (correctness first): where the ledger is in credit and equals minus the report to the cent, the ledger stands, flagged "Mega Farm: the Loan Book Report prints the credit balance as a debit; the ledger stands", with both figures in `build_basis.mega_farm.report_sign_error`; the ECL floors the exposure at nil (EclExposure), and the days past due of those rows fall from 91 or more to nil, since a credit balance is not past due. The report's carrying amount stands, flagged with the difference, on one account-month only: 000104500000144 on the September 2025 run (7,569,720.00 in credit), which has no posting in `MF_03`. No other carrying amount of a month with a report run moved. The months before the report begins (July to November 2024, 413 to 521 accounts a month) are derived from the ledger and now include the government's 67 percent: 286.9m more in July 2024, rising to 543.8m in November 2024. The 67 percent legs are interest, not drawdowns (decision of 14 October 2026): on a Mega Farm account they are counted in `interest_to_date` with the 33 percent, and no longer in `disbursed` (type 400 stays a disbursement type on MAIIC's own book), so the undrawn commitment (approved less disbursed) is no longer reduced each month by the interest posted. On the copy the Mega Farm `disbursed` at September 2026 falls from 58.67bn to 48.87bn (the 301 drawdowns), the commitments rise from 2.52bn to 3.23bn on the same 17 accounts, and the EAD the engine measures falls from 57.19bn to 56.64bn, because the utilisation rate (disbursed over approved) that weights the undrawn amount (EclExposure) falls to nil on those 17. The Mega Farm ECL (`megafarm:ecl`, rerun on the copy after the rebuild and the portfolio split): September 2026 programme 45,096,993,029.04 before and 44,661,438,651.32 after (MAIIC's 5 percent share 2,254,849,651.45 and 2,233,071,932.57); December 2025 is declined both times (no Stage 3 programme loan at 2024-12 to follow for the LGD). The approved amounts were not all genuine undrawn facilities, and two build rules now state the commitment from the data (decision of 14 October 2026). First, the take-on: the 2024-season loans came into E-Banker at the migration as type 900 "To Trf Opening Account Balance" debits on 31 July 2024 (413 postings, 8,179.3m, the take-on date TakeonLandingService::TAKEON_DATE), which TYPE_RECEIPT read as receipts, so the build saw nothing drawn and the whole sanction as undrawn (105450000001: 1,674.5m sanctioned, 1,167.4m owed, 1,674.5m shown undrawn). A type 900 debit narrated "Opening Account Balance" on or before the take-on date now counts as drawn (`LoanBookBuildService::isTakeOnDrawdown`; the amount is kept on the row as `build_basis.takeon_drawn`); every other type 900 posting stays a receipt. MAIIC's own book was taken on by type 301 ("Opening Account Balance Disbsument amount", 79 postings) and its ledger holds no type 900 opening debit (its one type 900 debit is a returned payment of 6 May 2025), so nothing there changes. Second, the facility's end: where the report leaves MATURITY_DATE empty, the loan master's EXPIRY_DATE is now the due date (it used to leave the row with none), recorded as `build_basis.due_date_source`. The twelve never-drawn Mega Farm facilities (no balance, sanctions of 50 to 32m, status A) all have an EXPIRY_DATE, the latest 6 July 2026: at September 2026 none can still be drawn, so under IFRS 9 none is a loan commitment, and the existing past-maturity rule drops their undrawn sanction with the amount kept on `build_basis.undrawn_after_maturity`. At December 2025 three of them were sanctioned and unexpired (104480003247, 19.04m to 28 June 2026; 104490002119, 5.04m to 16 June 2026; 104490000078, 50 to 6 July 2026) and stay commitments, as the data supports. Before and after the two rules on the copy: Mega Farm commitments 3,229.3m on 17 accounts to nil at September 2026, and 3,210.2m on 13 to 24.1m on 3 at December 2025; the build's EAD (carrying amount plus commitment) at December 2025 54.52bn from 57.71bn; the EAD the engine measures at September 2026 unchanged at 56.64bn, and the programme ECL unchanged at 44,661,438,651.32 with MAIIC's share 2,233,071,932.57, because those commitments had carried a nil utilisation rate (EclExposure weights the undrawn amount by it); December 2025 is declined as before. No row of either month is left without a due date. Other effects: on MAIIC's own book ten account-months of September to November 2025 (four nil-balance facilities, 24m to 103m, expired by their loan master date) lose their undrawn amount, with no change to the measured exposure (nil utilisation); on the Mega Farm book 69 account-months each in October and November 2025 (41.7m owed) whose runs left MATURITY_DATE empty are now counted past due from their expiry (67 of them 91 days or more), as the past-maturity rule of section 3.6 requires. The Baselines sheet's Mega Farm row now counts only the account-months that still differ (BaselineService::checks).

### 6.7 Loading the history, once

The history is loaded by the bootstrap of section 6.10 from the committed inputs; the steps below are what it does and how it is proven.

1. **The 7 October pack is committed** under `docs/bootstrap/ebanker-pack-2026-10-07/` with its manifest and hashes; it is pack 1 under route 1. The files are never opened in Excel.
2. **Gates.** The pack passes 6.4 with two accepted exceptions recorded against the load: the FInES GL openings of section 3.5 until Finance corrects them, and the Zaithwa Farms balance rows from May 2025 until the vendor rebuilds them.
3. **Build on a copy of the production database** for every month from July 2024 to August 2026, by method A first (the stored report is the quickest proof) and then by method B, and compare the two: the carrying amounts must agree on every account-month except the flagged rows of section 3.5. December 2025 to August 2026 are already loaded from the Excel reports; the build prints every difference before it overwrites.
4. **Prove.** Run one ECL month and one EIR month on the copy and compare with the Excel-loaded results; the Baselines sheet of section 12 must show PASS on every tie of section 9.
5. **Production**, then the same every month by route 1 until route 2 is installed. The first monthly pack, for September 2026, was run on 8 October and behaved as the contract requires: the August re-pull was identical to the first load (no back-dated posting), September arrived complete, and the month-end balances agreed with the ledger on every account.

A side benefit stands: with twenty-six months in `loan_books`, the ECL module can be back-run month by month. (**Status 14 October 2026:** with the September 2026 run of pack 3 the fresh bootstrap builds 27 months, July 2024 to September 2026, and runs staging, PD, LGD, the forward-looking route and the discounted ECL for every one of them; section 6.10.)

### 6.8 The feed screen

Data Foundation, E-Banker Feed: the queries (versioned, downloadable as the file Barry runs); the load history (pack, route, period, the gate results or the refusal reason, who loaded, when); the watermarks; the quarantine; and the Build action (method A, B or C, a month or a range; method C takes the report file) with its approval. Every derived loan-book row links back to its raw rows and its pack.

**Status 14 October 2026:** built (EirFeedController, routes `eir-feed.*`): the queries and their download, the load history with each gate and the failures of a quarantined load, the watermarks, and the Build with a second person's approval (LoanBookBuildService.php:160). Method C cannot be run from this screen: the Build takes no file (EirFeedController.php:132-142), and asking for method C there stops with "Method C takes the report file" (LoanBookBuildService.php:91-92); it runs from the command line only (section 6.2). Each derived row carries its load and source key (`build_load_id`, `build_source_key`).

### 6.9 The take-on schedules

**What they are for.** E-Banker's history of the 109 take-on loans begins on 31 July 2024 with one opening posting each. The effective interest rate needs what happened before that: the origination date, the original principal, the contractual rate and instalment pattern, and the fees charged at the start. Tamanda's workbook of amortisation schedules at 31 October 2024 (100 blocks, mapped on 7 October to 105 of the 109 facilities) is the only record of that. The engine uses it for two things: to solve the EIR at origination and roll the amortised cost forward to 31 July 2024, so that the loan enters E-Banker at its true amortised cost rather than its take-on balance; and as the version 1 schedule of those loans (`schedule_source = TAKEON_WORKBOOK`) in place of a generated one.

**The fees.** Neither the workbook nor E-Banker records the fees charged when a take-on loan was granted, and the EIR cannot be solved without them. The mapping workbook returned to Tamanda on 7 October (`Take-on schedules with mapping - with fee columns - for Tamanda to confirm - 7 Oct 2026.xlsx`) carries, on its Upload summary sheet, seven columns for her to complete per facility: the arrangement fee, the legal fees, any other fee that was a condition of the loan, the date charged, whether the fees were deducted from the amount paid out, the offer letter or receipt the figures come from, and a total. A blank means no such fee; a zero means known to be nil. This replaces the separate fee template of 25 September for the take-on loans (O14 is unchanged in substance: Finance supplies the fees; the system reads them).

**Status 14 October 2026:** the workbook was returned on 13 October 2026 with the fees completed by MAIIC for 101 facilities (362,307,251.23; `Take-on schedules with mapping - fees completed by MAIIC - received 13 Oct 2026.xlsx`); the 7 October copy is kept under `docs/bootstrap/takeon/superseded/`. Review item R16 (accepted) is applied only in part: a block whose whole fee row is blank is treated as unknown, flagged and never assumed fee-free (TakeonLandingService.php:215-216, 722-724), but a blank single fee column beside others is still read as no such fee, and the screen's fee entry still says "A blank means no such fee" (EirTakeonController.php:150). The rule should be: blank means unknown, zero means confirmed nil. A pre-migration history reconstructed from the schedule is an approved estimate, not evidenced cash, and is to be labelled so. The arrears at the opening of the roll-forward follow decision D39 (section 3.10): where the ledger owes more than the schedule expects by more than one percent, the difference is added to the opening, undiscounted.

**Landed, not typed.** The workbook enters by the feed door as a pack of its own kind: its hash, then two raw tables. `takeon_blocks` holds one row per block: title, principal, rate, term, start date, the mapped account, the mapping confidence, Tamanda's tick, the fees, and the sheet and cell each value came from. `takeon_schedule_lines` holds one row per instalment line: due date, instalment, principal, interest, balance, serial, with its cell reference. Every figure traces to the cell in the workbook she signed; the Upload summary's formulas already point there.

**Gates.** Every block is mapped to one account that exists in the master, or is explicitly marked not matched or refused (the three equity positions); no account has two blocks; the block principal equals the take-on posting (exact on 77) or the difference is named; the schedule's balance at 31 July 2024 is compared with E-Banker's take-on balance and the difference flagged as arrears or prepayment at take-on; every date is unambiguous; serial numbers out of due-date order are sorted by date and the anomaly raised (F16); a mapped facility with no fee row is flagged, never assumed fee-free. A refusal names the block and the cell. (**Status 14 October 2026:** built as gates (TakeonLandingService.php:693-748) except the comparison of the schedule's balance at 31 July 2024 with the take-on balance, which is not a gate: the roll-forward of a migrated loan starts from its 31 July 2024 ledger balance with the arrears of D39 instead.)

**The build** writes `contract_takeon` per account: origination date, original principal, contractual rate, fees, and the pre-migration cash flows. Those flows are contractual, not actual: nobody holds the receipts before E-Banker. How the engine treats them is a governed setting, `takeon_history_basis`:

| Option | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Recompute from origination where the block and fees exist, else start at the take-on balance** (seeded) | Where a facility has a mapped block and its fees, solve the EIR on the original schedule and fees, assume instalments were paid as scheduled to 31 July 2024 except where the take-on balance says otherwise, and book that difference as arrears at take-on. Where it has not (the four unmatched facilities, the eight closed accounts without a block, any block still without fees), treat the 31 July 2024 carrying amount as the opening amortised cost with no day-one history, and flag the loan |
| Recompute from origination for every take-on loan | As above, and refuse a loan whose block or fees are missing until they are supplied |
| Start every take-on loan at its take-on balance | No pre-migration history for any of them; the EIR is solved from 31 July 2024 on E-Banker's flows |

The seeded option is the reasonable one: it uses the history wherever MAIIC can evidence it and is honest where it cannot, and every loan carries the basis it was built on so an auditor sees which. Changing the setting rebuilds the take-on population only.

**Where.** The workbook is committed under `docs/bootstrap/takeon/` and loaded by the bootstrap (6.10); the returned version with the fees replaces it as a new file. Data Foundation, Take-on Schedules: upload a workbook (the alternative to the committed copy), see each block with its mapping, confidence, tick and fees, the gate results, and the Build with approval. Tamanda may confirm a mapping or enter a fee in the screen instead of in Excel; the screen's entry is the one that counts and is audit-logged. Re-uploading creates a new version; the previous build is kept. (**Status 14 October 2026:** the returned workbook sits in `docs/bootstrap/takeon/` and the 7 October copy was moved to `takeon/superseded/`, not kept beside it. The screen is built (EirTakeonController: upload, confirm, fees, build) but its Build is one person's action with no second approval (EirTakeonController.php:167-175), and the build replaces `contract_takeon` in full (TakeonLandingService.php:208).)

**Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** the Build is now proposed by one person and approved by another (`TakeonLandingService::proposeBuild` and `::approveBuild`, `takeon_build_requests`; the screen's button proposes, a second person's approves and builds; `eir:land-takeon --build` proposes and `--approve-build=` approves); the bootstrap approves under its label.

**Built 14 October 2026 (results review E2, decision D41).** The recompute reads the workbook as a balance chain in sheet order (each line's principal and interest due on its end date; a rise in the balance the line does not explain advanced on its date; a fall outside the instalments received) and solves from the date the schedule starts, at the balance it opens with, flagging a different Loan Book value date or header principal. The governed check `takeon_eir_plausibility` asks the schedule, solved with no fee, to give back the rates its lines charge and refuses one that does not retire the loan; a refused block stays on its take-on balance (`contract_takeon.eir_check`, `eir_check_reason`). The fees of the block land in `contract_fees` (source `TAKEON_WORKBOOK`, PENDING, the cell in the description) and count once reviewed. The schedule goes to Schedule Review as `PENDING_REVIEW`; once approved the engine calculates the EIR on the checked chain and the maker-checker lock locks it. The roll-forward of a migrated loan starts in August 2024 from its balance at 31 July 2024. On the workbook of 13 October 2026, 71 of 73 pass and COMSIV and BenCal Technologies are refused (section 3.11).

### 6.10 The bootstrap: a clean install that loads itself and proves itself

The Dupleix suite installs a client system with one command that wipes a clean database, seeds it, loads the client's own input files from a folder committed in the repository, runs the engines and checks the result against the golden numbers. MAIIC adopts the same method (decision D26), so that any server, including Deloitte's copy and the UAT copy, is built from nothing to a proven state without anyone sending a file.

**The committed inputs.** `docs/bootstrap/` in the repository holds, exactly as received and never re-saved: the E-Banker pack of 7 October 2026 (the 18 extracts and the 5 afternoon queries, 23 files), the data-dictionary results of 6 October (24 files), the 20 monthly trial balances of January 2025 to August 2026, the December 2025 AFS bridge in both received versions, and the mapping of every GL line to the audited accounts with the year-end balances of 2023 to 2025 and August 2026, the take-on workbook with its fee columns, the SQL that produced the pack, and `manifest.json` with every file's query id, row count and SHA-256 and the accepted exceptions the gates allow. The committed copy is read first; a OneDrive path is only a local fallback for development. A replacement, for example the workbook Finance returns with the fees filled in, is a new file beside the old one and a new manifest entry; nothing in the folder is edited in place. The files are MAIIC's data (borrower names and balances); the repository is private and access to it is governed as access to the production database is.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the committed inputs now also hold packs 2 to 4: `ebanker-pack-2026-10-08/` (7 files), `ebanker-pack-2026-10-09/` (12 files and its manifest) and `packs/ebanker-pack-2026-10-12.zip` (pack 4, 42 files, zipped because the CSVs are 143 MB), each landed through the gates by `Bootstrap::landLaterPacks` (Bootstrap.php:170-210) after the first pack. The trial balances are landed by `TrialBalanceLandingService` (Bootstrap.php:146-158). The take-on workbook is the one returned with the fees on 13 October; the 7 October copy was moved to `takeon/superseded/`, not kept beside it. The results of packs 5 to 8 (investigative probes) are not committed and not landed.

**The command.**

`php artisan eir:bootstrap --fresh --with-client-inputs --build --run-engines --verify`

| Step | What it does | Idempotent |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `migrate:fresh` on `--fresh`, refused if user data exists unless `--force-wipe` is also passed | Safety, not a step |
| 2 | Seeds: roles and permissions, the Governance Centre defaults (section 4.2), the help centre, the fee rulebook, the compliance-audit modules (section 12) | Yes: a key that exists is left alone |
| 3 | Lands the committed pack and the trial balances into the landing zone by route 1 (section 6.4): the manifest is checked file by file, the gates run, the accepted exceptions are recorded against the load | Yes: a file whose hash is already loaded is skipped |
| 4 | Lands the take-on workbook (section 6.9) and the fees it carries | Yes |
| 5 | On `--build`: builds the loan books for every month from July 2024 to the last month in the pack by the method in force (section 6.2), builds the take-on population under its basis, generates the version 1 schedules | Yes: a locked period is never restated |
| 6 | On `--run-engines`: runs the engine chain of 6.10.1 end to end for every period from the first full month to the last month in the pack, in the order the modules depend on each other, synchronously (the queue driver is set to `sync` for the run, so no worker is needed and nothing is left waiting) | Yes: a period already run and locked is skipped |
| 7 | On `--verify`: runs the baselines of section 9 and the golden numbers of 6.10.2 against the database, prints the table, PASS or FAIL per row, and exits non-zero on any FAIL | Yes |

**Status 14 October 2026.** Step 5 also builds the GL fee route (section 3.11) and approves the version 1 schedules under the bootstrap label. Step 6 runs, for every period: staging, then the EIR solve and lock, then by period the PD and LGD by segment, the forward-looking route (the overlay at zero), the ECL discounted at the EIR and the Mega Farm ECL, then the revenue roll-forward. For the last period only it then seeds and approves the scenario set, runs the forward-looking chain, the route and the ECL again, the scenario sensitivity, stress testing, the reports, the compliance register and the GL reconciliation (Bootstrap.php:286-470). Step 7 asserts five kinds of check, not every section 9 tie and none of the golden numbers below (BaselineService.php:22-90): the interest posted from January 2025 to July 2026 (5,293,988,207.06); method B against the stored report on the in-scope loan GLs; the year-end batch contra on 4215 and on 4216; and the loan book by GL at 31 December 2025 against the trial balance, with the two FInES differences accepted. A Mega Farm row is printed as reported, not tested.

**Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** step 5 now loads the scheme settings after the contract master (`ContractInputsBuildService::schemes`, from `SM_01` with `DD_10` where it lacks the scheme or a field) and flags contracts drawn above their sanction; the take-on Build is proposed and approved under the label; the version 1 schedules are approved under the label recorded on the contract. Step 6 still locks the EIRs with the administrator override (section 5).


#### 6.10.1 The engine chain the bootstrap runs

The suite's bootstrap runs its engines in dependency order and treats the result as the proof of the installation. MAIIC's chain, in the order the modules depend on each other, with what each needs and how the bootstrap supplies it where a person would normally act:

| Order | Engine | Entry point | Needs | How the bootstrap meets it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Macro statistics | `macro:import-worldbank`; the WEO snapshot | Internet, or the committed snapshot | Snapshot under `docs/bootstrap/macro/` when the fetch fails (13.3) |
| 2 | Scenario set | the seeded first set of 15.8 | An approved set | Seeded as *proposed*; the bootstrap approves it under the automated label so the chain can run; it is not a MAIIC approval and the screen says so |
| 3 | Staging and SICR | `StagingClassifier` on each month's loan book | Governed thresholds; the loan book | Thresholds seeded; loan books built in step 5 |
| 4 | PD: transition matrices | `TransitionMatrixService`, cumulative | Twelve or more months of graded loan books | The 26 months from the landing zone |
| 5 | LGD | `CalculateLGDJob` and its chunks | Payments and collateral | Payments from the ledger; collateral from the register; runs synchronously |
| 6 | Forward-looking adjustment | the route in force (14.6) | An approved model, or an overlay | With no approvable model on a clean install the bootstrap runs the **manual overlay route at zero** and marks every post-FLI PD "no adjustment: bootstrap"; the regression route is used once a model has been approved by two people |
| 7 | ECL | `ifrs9:recalculate-ecl --pd=pd_post_fli`, the time-phased service | PD, LGD, EAD, stage, the EIR for discounting | Steps 3 to 6 and 9 |
| 8 | EIR: schedules | `eir:generate-schedules` | Terms, drawdowns, the take-on basis | Steps of 6.9; version 1 approved under the automated label |
| 9 | EIR: solve and revenue | `CalculateEirJob`, `eir:run-revenue {period}` | Approved schedules, fees, rates, the governed conventions | Step 8; fees from the charges table and the take-on workbook |
| 10 | GL reconciliation | `EirGlReconciliationService` | The trial balances | Landed in step 3 of the bootstrap |
| 11 | Stress testing | `StressTestingController` logic as a service | The ECL of step 7 | Run for the seeded scenario set |
| 12 | Reports and the audit workbooks | the 30 reports; `tools/compliance` | Everything above | Generated for the last period; the Baselines sheet reads the live figures |

Two orderings matter and are enforced: the ECL is discounted at the EIR, so step 9 runs before step 7 for each period (the chain runs 8 and 9 first, then 3 to 7, then 10 to 12); and the forward-looking adjustment runs once per scenario under the seeded weighting method of 15.5, so step 6 is a loop over the set of step 2.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the entry points in the code differ from the table. Step 3 is `StagingService` (the legacy `StagingClassifier` is not run). Step 4 is the PD by segment, `PdSegmentationService`, after the Mega Farm schemes are split into their own portfolio (`ifrs9:segment-portfolios`). Step 5 is `LgdSegmentationService` with `LgdEngineService`; collateral is not used (no collateral is read by the LGD). Step 7 is `ifrs9:segment-ecl --pd=pd_post_fli --discounting=discounted`. Step 9 calls `EirCalculationService` directly and locks with the administrator override (Bootstrap.php:310-315). Step 11 is `StressTestService::runSet`. The order run is: 3 staging; 9 the EIR solve and lock; then for each period 4, 5, 6 and 7 and the Mega Farm ECL; then 9 the revenue for each period, after the ECL so that Stage 3 revenue nets the allowance (system audit C6); then, for the last period only, 2, 6 and 7 again, 11, 12 and 10. The scenario set of step 2 is therefore seeded and approved after the per-period ECL, and only for the last period.

**Status 14 October 2026, later (audit finding M17):** the set being seeded for the last period only left every earlier month's route without a set, so its Stage 3 loans carried neither the set nor a scenario PD and the time-phased ECL left them unresolved ("The loan is not on the scenario set the rest of the run is measured under"); on a fresh bootstrap December 2025 was discounted on 18 of MAIIC's 123 loans. The bootstrap now runs, for every period from the first month with a PD to the last, the Maintenance month-end chain in the order of `Bootstrap::periodChain()`: 4 and 5 the PD and LGD by segment; 2 the month's scenario set (the first set of 15.8 seeded for the month and approved under the bootstrap label, never as MAIIC; its paths read as known at the month-end); 6 the route under the model version and the set in force for the month (no version is in force on a clean install, so the PD holds and the loan says so); the scenario projection (evidence; it records why it cannot run without a model version); 7 the ECL on the post-FLI PD discounted at the EIR; the month record (`fli_run_snapshots`, not signed off); then the Mega Farm ECL. `fli:month-end --all` itself is not called because it stops at a month with no model version in force. The revenue then runs for each period as before. For the last period the correlation sweep follows (it proposes fits, approves none), then 11, 12 and 10; the route and the ECL are no longer run a second time. The compliance register step also runs again: `compliance_audit_rows.test` is text (migration 2026_10_14_250000), as a committed test is longer than 500 characters. Tests: `tests/Feature/FLI/BootstrapMonthEndChainTest.php`; `ComplianceAuditServiceTest::test_the_committed_modules_load_with_tests_longer_than_500_characters`.

Three engines are queued jobs today (LGD, EIR solve, revenue). Under `--run-engines` they run synchronously; on a server they still run through the queue, and the installation guide carries the worker command with a memory ceiling, because the suite found that a worker left on its default limit stops itself part-way through a full chain and later jobs wait in silence.

#### 6.10.2 Golden numbers

`--verify` checks the section 9 baselines and, once the engines have run, these figures. They are MAIIC's own, so a bootstrap on any server either reproduces them or fails:

| Golden number | Expected | Source |
|---|---|---|
| ECL at 31 December 2025, total and by stage | The audited impairment allowance in the 2025 financial statements | The AFS mapping workbook (`TB_AFS_MAP`), the impairment lines; the signed statements |
| ECL at 31 December 2024 | The audited 2024 allowance | The same workbook's December 2024 column |
| Contractual interest posted, January 2025 to July 2026 (corrected 14 October 2026, review item R18; it said "Contractual interest 2025, by loan GL") | MWK 5,293,988,207.06 for January 2025 to July 2026 (section 9), split by month | The ledger |
| Interest income 2025 against the trial balance | The year-to-date tie, 83 of 83 account-months | Section 9 |
| Loan balances by GL at each year-end | The audited figures: 2024 and 2025 by GL | The AFS mapping workbook |
| The revenue shift 2024 and 2025 | Recorded on the first run that Dr Thom approves, then held as the number later builds must reproduce | The engine, once approved |

The last row is how a golden number is born: the first approved run writes it, and from then on `--verify` fails any build that changes it without a governed reason.

Anything the bootstrap approves (a load, a build, a generated schedule, the seeded scenario set) is stamped with the approver label "System Bootstrap (automated data-readiness, not a MAIIC approval)", so that no one can mistake it for a sign-off by MAIIC; the maker-checker approvals of the Governance Centre and the register are never given by the bootstrap.

**Status 14 October 2026:** of the six golden numbers, `--verify` asserts only the interest total, as one figure, not by GL or by month (BaselineService.php:27-36). Not asserted: the ECL at 31 December 2025 and 2024; the 83 of 83 interest-income tie; loan balances by GL at year-end against the audited figures (31 December 2025 is checked against the trial balance only, and 2024 not at all); the revenue shift, since no run has been approved. Review item R18 is not yet applied beyond the label: separate the file-evidence baselines, the reproduction of the historical model and the current results, and say how an approved source correction retires an exception count. On the approvals: the bootstrap does not review fees, but it locks every EIR it solves with the administrator override (Bootstrap.php:314), recorded with the bootstrap user and no label, which the sentence above rules out; the loan book build, the schedules and the scenario set carry the bootstrap label. The Governance Centre defaults are seeded as approved technical defaults (D21).

**What it is for.** The first installation on MAIIC's server; every UAT and acceptance round, which starts from a bootstrap so that the result is reproducible; Deloitte's copy; and the developers' own daily state, since a bootstrap with `--build --run-engines --verify` is the end-to-end test of the data foundation and of every engine on it. The monthly packs of section 6.5 are loaded by the feed, not by the bootstrap; the bootstrap loads what is committed.

### 6.11 The EIR as at any date

A requirement in its own right: **the system shows the EIR computation for any loan as at any date the user names**, not only at month-ends and not only for the latest run.

- **What "as at a date" means.** The engine takes every posting in the landing zone dated on or before the date, the rate in force on the date, the governance values in force on the date, the schedule version in force on the date, and the take-on basis of 6.9, and produces for that date: the EIR in force and when it was last re-solved, the amortised cost, the gross carrying amount, the interest recognised to the date in the period and the year, the cumulative difference between the EIR interest and the contractual interest posted, the remaining expected cash flows, and the modification history. Each figure is shown with the inputs that produced it.
- **Any date, not only a period end.** A date inside a month uses the actual-day convention of section 3 (prior month-end balance, rate, days over 365) for the part-month. A date in a locked period reproduces the locked figures exactly, because a locked period keeps the settings and the schedule it was locked under; a date after the last loaded pack is refused with the last loaded date named, never estimated.
- **For the whole book.** The same view at portfolio level: the EIR interest, the contractual interest and the difference for the year to the date, by product, by GL and in total, which is the revenue shift Dr Thom asked for, at any date.
- **Where.** The Contract Profile carries a date picker ("View as at") beside the schedule; the Report Hub carries "EIR as at a date" for the book, with the Excel and PDF downloads; both read the same service (`EirAsAtService`), so a figure on the screen and in the download are the same computation.
- **The dates that matter now.** 31 December 2024, 31 December 2025 and 31 December 2026 are the year-ends Deloitte will ask for. Section 9 gains them as baselines once the first two are run.

**Status 14 October 2026:** built (`EirAsAtService`, `eir-as-at.index` and `eir-as-at.export` with Excel and PDF, the "View as at" on the schedule screen), with three gaps. The part-month interest is computed at the contractual rate, simple, on the prior month-end balance (EirAsAtService.php:123-130), but is returned as `eir_period_to_date` (EirAsAtService.php:188), so contractual interest is labelled as EIR interest (review item R12); it should use the EIR in force for the part-month. The modification history reads `rate_reset_events`, which nothing writes yet (section 3.9). A date after the last loaded posting is refused with that date named (EirAsAtService.php:287). The year-end baselines of the last bullet are not in section 9 or `--verify` yet.

**Status 14 October 2026 (reports build), review item R12 built:** inside a month the EIR interest is now the monthly EIR on the prior month-end amortised cost for the days of the month elapsed, so the month-end equals the revenue run's month, and it moves the amortised cost; the contractual accrual (section 3: the contractual rate, simple, on the prior month-end ledger balance, actual days over 365) is returned beside it as `contractual_accrued_period_to_date`, accrued and not yet posted, and the contractual year to date and cumulative add it so the difference compares like with like (EirAsAtService; EirAsAtServiceTest, whose part-month test had asserted the defect). A Stage 3 loan's part month is accrued on the gross amortised cost; the month-end row nets the allowance.

## 7. The importers, reworked

Version 3 section 5 described five input files. The five remain, but four of them are now read from E-Banker's own tables. This section says what each importer does and the rules it enforces. Every importer refuses an ambiguous date and names the row (D19), logs what it ignored, and is idempotent.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the file importers refuse a file that fails their column check and name the row (one registry, `App\Support\Imports\ImportSpecs`, commit 7a2a96f); the E-Banker pack gate `dates_iso` is a WARNING, not a refusal (`app/Services/Ebanker/PackGates.php:115-144`), so a pack with non-ISO dates still lands.

### 7.1 The ledger importer (replaces Extract B and Extract C)

Lands `P1_01` in `ebanker_ledger` (section 6.3) and reads it from there: every posting on the in-scope accounts with its signed amount, transaction type, value date and narration. Rules: debits negative as received, never ABS'd; a reversal (306, 343, 901) is kept as its own row and paired to the posting it reverses by amount and date; the year-end type 120 is interest of December; a deleted row (nil value, deleted flag) is loaded and marked, not dropped. The ledger's running balance per account is the carrying amount for any date, and the reconciliation reads interest (303 and 120) from it. Extract C is retired as the source; `GlInterestImportService` remains the writer of the interest posted per account and month, now fed from the ledger's 303 and 120 postings by `ContractInputsBuildService::glInterest` (`app/Services/Ebanker/ContractInputsBuildService.php:143-171`) (corrected 14 October 2026, consolidated review Cp5; it said "The old `GlInterestImportService`, which skipped annual rows, is retired").

**Status 14 October 2026:** the pairing of a reversal to the posting it reverses "by amount and date" is not built. Reversals (306, 343, 901) are receipt types and come through as negative cash that reduces the month (`app/Services/Ebanker/LoanBookBuildService.php:42`, `app/Services/Eir/EirLedgerCashService.php:85`); no pair is recorded (system audit M15).

**Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** built: `EirLedgerCashService::pairReversals` pairs each reversal with the nearest earlier posting of the opposite amount on the same account that is not itself a reversal, records the pair on both rows (`reverses_external_id`, `reversal_status`) with the days between, and names a reversal with no such posting as UNPAIRED; no fixed window is imposed, so a long gap is shown. The month's cash is unchanged. On the clone `maiic_ifrs9_controls`: 40 reversals, 29 paired (18 on the same day, the longest 336 days), 11 unpaired.

### 7.2 The rate-history importer (new)

Reads three things and keeps them apart: the PLR master (`P2_06`) as the reference-rate series with its Reserve Bank effective dates; the monthly loan-book rate as the rate charged; and the rate set-up table (`P1_04`) as the register of when each account's rate was keyed and under which Interest Policy. Rules: the rate type is P floating, S and M fixed, read from `INTEREST_POLICY`; the Floating Flag is recorded verbatim and a disagreement is noted, never used to derive the type; the E-Banker build derives the type from `INTEREST_POLICY` (P floating, any other policy fixed: `app/Services/Ebanker/ContractInputsBuildService.php:64,77`), but the file importer still maps a delivered `RATE_BASIS` column to `rate_type` (`app/Imports/ContractMasterImport.php:71-76`) (corrected 14 October 2026; it said "(the old `RATE_BASIS` mapping, which was inverted, is removed)"); the spread is charged rate less PLR, derived monthly, and a change in it is an event for the reset-or-modification rule (O17). The PLR master has one row (id 2) whose effective date was blank in the second export but present in the first; the first export is used.

**Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** the `RATE_BASIS` alias is removed from the file importer (`ContractMasterImport::aliases`); a file states the rate type as `RATE_TYPE`, and with none the type is taken from the Interest Policy as the E-Banker build takes it, a disagreement noted on the row (`ContractMasterImportService::rateTypeFor`).

**Status 14 October 2026:** the spread is derived (`SpreadDerivationService`, a spread that wanders by more than 0.15 point is flagged `DRIFT`), but no spread-change event is written: nothing writes `rate_reset_events` (0 rows on the fresh bootstrap `maiic_ifrs9_results2`; the table is only read, by `EirAsAtService.php:171` and `AuditTraceController.php:48`). This is P5 (section 8).

**Status 14 October 2026 (reset register built):** `RateResetService` writes `rate_reset_events`: every month whose loan-book rate differs from the month before, with the PLR at both month-ends (`reference_rate_series`) and the spread; a move whose spread held within the 0.15 point tolerance is a `PLR_RESET` (suggested in-contract), otherwise a `SPREAD_CHANGE` for a person to classify (O17). A loan the engine treats as fixed-rate whose rate moved with the PLR is recorded as a reset with that named on the evidence; any other move of a fixed-rate loan is a restructure candidate. One person proposes the classification, a different person approves, a move in a locked period is refused, `rate_change_classification` is read (its "Every rate change is a reset" closes the negotiated option) and a move approved as negotiated becomes a restructure candidate. The B5.4.5 treatment stays the monthly re-estimation of D38; the register does not gate it. On the clone: 281 PLR resets on 40 loans (59 on 8 loans the engine treats as fixed-rate) and 42 spread changes on 22 loans, September 2024 to September 2026; none approved.

### 7.3 The contract-master importer (reworked)

Reads `P1_02` and `P1_03` and loads the verbatim E-Banker code columns that P2 introduced (Interest Policy, Floating Flag, calculation base, instalment basis, moratorium type, the EMI type, the sanction-versus-balance basis). `MARGIN_PERCENT` is zero on every loan master row and is not used. The sanction amount and date are loaded; an account whose drawn balance exceeds its sanction is flagged for Credit (six today, all take-on balances; two need the offer letter).

**Status 14 October 2026:** the verbatim code columns are loaded (`contract_eir.interest_policy`, `floating_flag`, `interest_calc_base`, `installment_based_on`, `emi_calc_type`, `moratorium_type_verbatim`). The over-sanction flag is not built: no code compares the drawn balance with the sanction (system audit M15).

**Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** built: `ContractInputsBuildService::flagOverSanction` writes the excess of the drawn amount over the sanction to `contract_eir.over_sanction_amount` on every contract-master build and month-end; 2 contracts on the clone `maiic_ifrs9_controls` (104420000009, 104430000002), against the six the paragraph above counted from the take-on balances. The scheme settings are loaded too (`schemes`, 0 rows before): the 6 partly drawn facilities refused for INTEREST_BASIS_MISSING now take the balance basis their scheme states and generate drafts on the clone.

### 7.4 The fee importer (two routes)

Section 7.8 sets out the whole path of a fee, from E-Banker to the EIR, with the sources, the delivery routes, the link to the loan, the checks and the settings. This section keeps the importer's own detail.

Loans disbursed through E-Banker: `P2_07`, the charges table, with the fee name, amount, posting date and income GL; the standing fee rulebook (25 rules) classifies each as integral or not. Take-on loans: Tamanda's template. Both land in the same table with their source recorded. The control total for both routes is the fee income GLs, 4871 and 4873, not the account ledger: about 44 percent of 2025 fee income, and nearly all of it from March 2026, was credited to income in the drawdown batch with no fee leg on the loan account (section 3.7). The importer therefore reconciles each month's fees to the GL spools and flags a GL fee line it cannot place on a loan.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the standing fee rulebook now holds 30 rules (`database/seeders/EirAccountingRuleSeeder.php`, changed 8 October; 30 active in `eir_accounting_rules` on the fresh bootstrap). The "about 44 percent" was measured by matching GL fee lines to type-301 fee debits on the loan account by date and amount, closing vouchers excluded (consolidated review R05); the batch link of `GL_04` (7.8.4) now replaces that estimate: of the 178 fee postings of the pack of 12 October 2026, 51 are tied to a loan through the batch and 125 have no loan leg in their batch (section 3.11). `P2_07` is no longer the only source for loans disbursed through E-Banker: the GL postings are the source of record and supersede the `P2_07` lines of the same contract (7.8.2). Take-on fees land from the take-on workbook with the source `TAKEON_WORKBOOK`, PENDING.

**Built 11 October 2026: the fee GL check.** After every fee import, `FeeGlReconciliationService` takes each year the file covers and sets every fee held for that year (all imports, not only this file) beside the trial balance movement of its GL, by month and for the year, inside the `recon_tolerance` band. The GL of a line is its `gl_account_ref` when that names a fee GL, otherwise 4873 for an arrangement fee and 4871 for a legal fee; a PAID line (a cost MAIIC paid out, 6752) is left out of the income GLs and listed apart; a line with no date is reported because it cannot be placed in a month. The monthly GL figure is `TrialBalanceMovementService`'s, so December 2025 is read pre-closing from the AFS workbook (the 2025 year then totals 171,128,135.00 and 311,342,792.00, as in section 3.7). The result shows on the import (EIR Data Intake) and on Fee Classification, which gains an **Import fees** button. It reports and never blocks, and the Governance Centre setting `fee_gl_reconciliation` switches it off. A new permission, `eir.fees.import`, opens EIR Data Intake for the fee type only and Fee Classification read-only, so Finance can load fees by loan account without the broad settings permission; classifying and reviewing still need settings. The fee sample file now carries 4873, 4871 and 6752 in `gl_account_ref`, with a received legal fee and a paid legal cost. Tests: `tests/Feature/Eir/FeeGlReconciliationServiceTest.php`. The `COLLECTION_TYPES` list in `EirRevenueService` wrongly includes 'Interest' and treats an interest charge as cash collected; it is corrected.

### 7.5 Expected cash flows (generated, not imported)

The chart exists for 75 of 184 accounts and is unreliable where it exists, so the version 1 schedule is generated from the loan's terms on actual dates (P4) and compared with the chart and with the LOS schedule as references, as D20 and O7 provide. Drawdowns come from `P3_12`, with the tranches.

**Status 14 October 2026:** drawdowns now come from the ledger, not `P3_12`: every type-301 debit on a loan account is a drawdown on its date, several on one day are one drawdown, a separate fee debit is added to its drawdown as `capitalised_fees`, and the take-on opening of 31 July 2024 is not a drawdown (`ContractInputsBuildService::disbursements`, `app/Services/Ebanker/ContractInputsBuildService.php:200-273`; `P3_12` is landed for reference only; tests `GlFeeRouteTest::test_fee_debits_join_their_drawdown_and_the_takeon_opening_is_not_a_tranche`, `TrancheScheduleTest`). The generated schedule follows the drawdowns (D40, section 3.11). E-Banker's instalment chart is now read for staging and PD, as the chart in force at each month-end (`app/Services/Ebanker/InstalmentChartHistory.php`, commit 216aee2), but not for the EIR schedule.

### 7.6 The take-on loader (new)

As section 6.9: the workbook landed as `takeon_blocks` and `takeon_schedule_lines`, the gates, the build of `contract_takeon` under the `takeon_history_basis` setting, and the Take-on Schedules screen.
