### 7.7 The trial balances: landed with the rest, and the GL opening balances

The trial balance is the check on everything else, so it enters by the same door as everything else. The 20 monthly files, the December 2025 AFS bridge (the 10 September 2026 version is the one loaded; the 19 August version held its amounts as text) and the mapping of every GL line to the audited accounts are committed under `docs/bootstrap/trial-balances/` and landed in `ebanker_raw_rows` as query `TB_01`, one load per file in `ebanker_loads` with route `TRIAL_BALANCE_FILE` (`app/Services/Ebanker/TrialBalanceLandingService.php`; 21 such loads on the fresh bootstrap) (corrected 14 October 2026; it said "landed as `ebanker_trial_balances`", a table that does not exist) (section 6.3); each later month arrives as one more file in the monthly pack, with its hash in the manifest. `gl_trial_balance_lines`, which the reconciliation reads, is derived from the landed files by the build, and `gl_account_scope` still says what each GL code is. The two rules of the September importer are unchanged: a P&L balance is cumulative year-to-date and is turned into a month on read (January taken whole); a balance-sheet balance is never differenced. `GL_02` is landed as the small table of keyed GL opening balances so that the bridge shows the two FInES differences as what they are. The ties the trial balance gives (section 9: the loan GLs to the cent, the FInES differences, interest income year-to-date on 83 of 83) are run by `eir:bootstrap --verify` like every other baseline.

**Status 14 October 2026:** a later month is not part of the E-Banker pack: it is uploaded on EIR Data, Trial Balances (`eir-trial-balances.index` and `.upload`, commit 91f567d) and lands through the same `TrialBalanceLandingService`. `GL_02` is read for account names only (`app/Services/Ebanker/LandingZoneReader.php:30-31,241`); the two FInES differences are carried as accepted exceptions in the baseline check (`app/Services/Eir/BaselineService.php:63-75`), not shown from `GL_02`. Of the ties named here, `--verify` asserts only the loan GLs at 31 December 2025 (with the two FInES exceptions); every month and the 83 of 83 interest months are not asserted (section 9). The `TB_03` proof is not built: no code reads `TB_03`.

**The two charts of accounts** (added 11 October 2026). E-Banker's trial balance is not the ledger the accounts are prepared from. Finance keeps the statutory books in QuickBooks, and the AFS mapping workbook's "TB December 2025" sheet maps every E-Banker line to a QuickBooks code. For the lines the engine touches:

| QuickBooks (accounts) | E-Banker (trial balance) | 2025, MWK | What changes between them |
|---|---|---:|---|
| 4206 Interest on Term Loans-MAIIC | 4215 + 4216 + 42019 | 2,106,561,165.01 | The audit added the interest-in-suspense reversal (21,865,867.35); the accounts then presented 259,317,000 of arrangement fees here, a manual adjustment recommended by the auditors from an extrapolated sample and not posted, because E-Banker has no EIR calculation (section 3.7) |
| 4209 Term loan interest-FInES | 4218 + 4219 | 447,829,531.72 | Nothing |
| 4870 Services Income: 4871 Legal fees | 4871 | 171,128,135.00 | Nothing in 2025 |
| 4870 Services Income: 4873 Arrangement fees | 4873 | 311,342,792.00 | Shown as 52,025,792 in the accounts after the presentation above |
| 1320 Interest Suspense | 1320 | 21,865,867.35 (credit) | Reversed to 4206 by the audit |

The reconciliation therefore runs in two steps: E-Banker's ledger to E-Banker's trial balance (the ties of section 9), then E-Banker's trial balance to the accounts through this mapping, with each audit adjustment as its own named line. The mapping is seeded in `gl_account_scope` (chart `QUICKBOOKS`, codes 4206, 4209 and 4870) and the full 191-row map is landed with the AFS bridge. E-Banker's own year-end posting, the re-rating batch of 31 December (section 3.4), is expected every year and sits inside the E-Banker figures, not among the audit adjustments.

### 7.8 Fees: how they are ingested and reconciled (added 11 October 2026)

**In plain language.** A fee is part of a loan's effective interest rate, so every arrangement and legal fee has to reach the engine once, on the right loan, at the right amount and date. MAIIC holds the same fee in several places: the GL fee accounts (4871, 4873), the loan account in E-Banker, the loan origination system's charges table, Finance's own workbook, and the offer letter. Only the GL posting is the accounting record; the others are evidence of it. This section says which record the engine takes, how it arrives, how it is tied to its loan, how it is proved against the trial balance, and which choices MAIIC can change. The findings behind it are in section 3.7 and in the note `docs/MAIIC_Fees_2025_Workbook_GL_and_Design_2026-10-11.md`.

**Status 14 October 2026:** (consolidated review Cp6, R21) built: the fee GL check of 7.4 (11 October); `GL_04a` and `GL_04b` in the query register and in pack 4, landed; the batch link and the held list `fee_gl_unassigned` (`app/Services/Ebanker/GlFeeBuildService.php`, run by `eir:bootstrap` and `php artisan eir:build-gl-fees`); `fee_source_precedence` (`app/Services/Eir/FeeSourcePrecedence.php`); separate fee debits capitalised into their drawdown; the fee GL gate as a report on every build, closing vouchers excluded; the source cross-check as a computed list (tests: `tests/Feature/Ebanker/GlFeeRouteTest.php`, 4 tests; `tests/Feature/Eir/TakeonAndTrancheEirInputTest.php`). Uncommitted in the working tree: the Fee Classification panels for the held lines (with the tie action, route `eir-fee-classification.tie-held`) and the source differences, and the Drawdowns column for the capitalised fees. Not built: the fee GL gate as a refusal when a pack lands (`PackGates` has no fee check), the GL fee build in the pack landing or the month-end job (`app/Console/Commands/MaintenanceTask.php:43-51` has no step for it), precedence per fee event and period (R03), the reversal link and known-at date on a fee line (R02), the branch in the batch key (R22), and the two-chart bridge. **Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** the fee GL gate now refuses a pack at landing where a month of `GL_04a` differs from the trial balance (`PackGates`, gate `fee_gl`, as section 6.4 requires; a month whose trial balance has not landed is listed and checked again by the build), and the month-end runs `eir:build-gl-fees` after the ledger cash. On the clone: 39 months agree, none differ, 12 without a trial balance.

**Status 15 October 2026 (checked against the code on master `fdfac85`; items marked branch are on `eir-readiness-control`, awaiting approval):** the go-live controls the paragraph above calls uncommitted are on master: the fee GL gate refuses a pack at landing (`PackGates::feeGl`, gate `fee_gl`, level ERROR) and the month-end job runs `eir:build-gl-fees` (`MaintenanceTask.php:128`); the Fee Classification panels for the held lines and the source differences are on master. Built since: Finance's workbook proved against the GL before it loads (`FeeWorkbookTieOut`, 7.8.8); (branch) precedence per fee event (R03, below); (branch) the gate checks both ways: every fee GL in every month from the first to the last month the legs reach, so a month the trial balance moves but `GL_04a` does not carry fails as `MISSING_IN_GL04`; (branch) the EIR fee-readiness control: no EIR is solved or locked while a held posting names the loan as its likely owner (`FEE_HELD_FOR_LOAN`, `HeldFeeEvidence`; a person ties it or records that it is not the loan's), and a locked EIR whose fee evidence arrives later is flagged for review, never reopened by the system (`EirLockReviewService`, `eir_lock_reviews`). The gate's tolerance is 1.00 (to the unit), stricter than `recon_tolerance`, on purpose. Still not built: the reversal link and known-at date (R02); the branch in the batch key (R22; the payloads carry `CB_BRANCH_MST_ID`, and whether batch numbers are unique per branch is a question for ICT); a re-check of GL lines already loaded when a later pack deletes or un-passes a leg, or a better tie rule moves a voucher (a rebuild skips a voucher already in `contract_fees`); 4874 and 4875 land as fee type other, kept out of the EIR only by classification; an unknown `source_system` counts as the Finance tier; the year-end bridge from E-Banker's trial balance to the audited accounts.

### 7.8.1 How E-Banker records a fee

The fees are read off the offer letter, keyed by Finance and deducted from the amount advanced: a facility of 100 million with 5 million of fees pays out 95 million and is carried at 100 million. E-Banker posts it as one balanced batch on the drawdown date: the loan account is debited with the gross amount, the bank is credited with the cash paid out, and 4871 and 4873 are credited with the fees. The fee therefore appears in two places (on the loan and in income) because a double entry has two sides; it is not recorded twice. The loan side is written in one of two ways: a separate fee debit on the loan account, type 301 with a fee narration (about 56 percent of 2025 fee income), or one gross drawdown line with the fee inside it (the rest, and nearly all of 2026). The GL printout of 4871 and 4873 carries no account number; the link to the loan is the batch, which every leg of the drawdown shares.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the "about 56 percent" is the complement of the 44 percent of 7.4 and has the same method (GL lines matched to type-301 fee debits by date and amount, closing vouchers excluded; consolidated review R05). The pack of 12 October 2026 also shows a third layout the text above does not name: the fee credited in a separate transfer batch with no loan leg (125 of the 178 fee postings, held in `fee_gl_unassigned`, section 3.11).

### 7.8.2 The sources, and which one counts

| Source | What it holds | How it arrives | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| **GL fee postings by loan** (`GL_04a`, `GL_04b`) | Every posting to 4871, 4873, 4874 and 4875, and every other leg of the same batches | The E-Banker feed (7.8.3) | **The source of record** once landed: it is the GL, so it ties to the trial balance by construction, and it carries the posting date |
| **Finance's fee workbook** | The fees by loan account for a year (for 2025: `MAIIC EIR Assessment as of 31st December 2025 with account numbers.xlsx`, 34 loans, ties to the GL within 800) | EIR Data Intake, type fees, by Finance (`eir.fees.import`) | The source of record for 2025 until `GL_04` is landed; afterwards a cross-check |
| **LOS charges** (`P2_07`, LOS_DISB_CHARGES_POST) | What the origination system captured at application, with no date | The E-Banker feed (the whole table is RUN 5 of pack 4) | Evidence only: it differed from what was posted on 9 of 21 loans in 2025 |
| **Extract A fee columns** | arrangement_fee and legal_fees on the contract master | EIR Data Intake, contract master | Evidence only |
| **Take-on fees** | Fees of the 109 loans taken on in July 2024, which E-Banker never posted | The take-on workbook or the Take-on Schedules screen (section 6.9) | The only record for those loans; they never compete with the others |
| **Offer letters** | The contract | Read by Credit and Dupleix | Evidence (decision D20) |

**One fee, one source.** The engine reads a contract's fees from the highest source that holds them, in the order of the governed setting `fee_source_precedence` (7.8.6). A line from a lower source is kept and shown, marked `SUPERSEDED`, and never enters the EIR; where its amount differs from the source of record, Fee Classification lists the difference as an exception for Finance. This is what stops the same fee being counted twice: today the bootstrap loads `P2_07` into `contract_fees`, and importing Finance's workbook as well would count the fees of 21 loans twice and overstate the EIR.

**Status 14 October 2026:** built per contract, not per fee event: for each contract the highest tier holding any fee line wins and every line of a lower tier is marked with the winner in `contract_fees.superseded_by_source` (`app/Services/Eir/FeeSourcePrecedence.php:83-136`); the EIR reads only lines with no mark (`app/Services/Eir/EirContractInputService.php:166-177`). Consolidated review R03 (precedence per fee event and coverage period, so that a source holding one year's or one tranche's fee never suppresses another's, with overlaps quarantined) is not built: a contract with one GL fee posting supersedes every `P2_07` line of the same contract, whatever its date. Any line loaded through EIR Data Intake counts as the Finance workbook tier (`FeeSourcePrecedence::tierOf`). The LOS charges are not only evidence: where they are the highest source a contract has, they are its source of record (38 contracts on the rerun of section 3.11). The cross-source differences are computed on each run and returned; the Fee Classification panel that lists them is uncommitted.

**Status 15 October 2026 (branch `eir-readiness-control`): R03 built.** Precedence is resolved per contract and fee type, then per event: the highest source holding a line of that type is the source of record, and each lower-source line is paired with one of its lines for the same event (by date within 120 days where both are dated, otherwise by amount, equal first then the nearest). A paired line is superseded, and any difference of amount is stored per event in `fee_source_differences` (read by Fee Classification, no longer recomputed). A lower-source line with no pair while the source of record holds that fee type is an overlap: not superseded, marked in `contract_fees.source_overlap`, and it blocks the loan's EIR (`FEE_SOURCE_OVERLAP`) until a person with `eir.govern` decides, with the reason, a fee of its own or the same fee (`FeeSourcePrecedence::decide`, `fee_source_decisions`, Fee Classification tab Overlaps to decide). A fee type the source of record does not hold is never suppressed: a missing row is not a confirmed zero. Tests: `EirFeeReadinessControlTest`.

**Status 15 October 2026, later (branch `eir-fee-coverage`, from the impact report of 15 October): R03 corrected, and the per-period rule built.** The type-by-type comparison counted money twice: the GL and the LOS split the same fee into different types (Focus Trading, The Food Empire, Ebenezer Midian: the LOS total equals the GL total to the cent, yet a type the GL does not hold stood). Precedence is now resolved per contract and fee event across all fee types: the highest source holding any line for the contract is the source of record. (1) A lower-source line dated in a month the source of record does not cover stands: coverage is kept in `fee_source_coverage`, written by the GL fee build (the months the `GL_04a` fee legs span, replaced on each build) and by each Finance fee workbook load (the months its tie-out accounts for, added per file); a tier with no coverage recorded is taken to cover every month. (2) The rest fall into fee events: dated lines within 120 days of each other chain into one event; an undated line makes the contract one event. (3) Where the lower lines of an event total the source of record's lines to the cent, every one is superseded and no difference is recorded. (4) Otherwise each is paired with a line of record of its own type; a paired line is superseded with any difference stored, and an unpaired line is an overlap for a person to decide (it no longer stands because its type is missing from the source of record). With the setting at its default (GL first), Finance's workbook lines dated before October 2024 stand; with `Finance workbook, GL posting, LOS capture, Extract A` approved in the Governance Centre, the GL fills only the months Finance's files do not cover. On the test bootstrap: 12 of the 20 loans holding GL and LOS fees tie by total, 7 per-type differences remain for review, no overlap. Tests: `EirFeeReadinessControlTest`.

### 7.8.3 How the GL fee postings arrive

`GL_04a` and `GL_04b` are two read-only queries in the register (section 6.4). They arrive like every other extract, by the route in force (`ebanker_feed_route`, section 6.5):

| Route | What happens | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Manual pack (in force) | Barry runs the queries in SQL Developer (RUN 6 of pack 4), exports two CSVs and sends them; they are uploaded on Data Foundation, E-Banker Feed | Ready. The first run covers 1 January 2024 to date |
| 2. Scheduled export | A Windows scheduled task at MAIIC runs the export script on the first working day after month-end and writes the pack to a shared folder that the system polls (`eir:poll-feed-folder`) | The script template already carries `GL_04a` and `GL_04b`; needs a read-only Oracle account and the folder from ICT |
| 3. Push by API | The same script posts the zipped pack to the system with a token | Built; needs the token issued |
| 4. Direct read over the VPN | The system reads E-Banker with a read-only account | Needs Dr Thom and ICT |
| 5. Vendor view or API | The vendor exposes the same rows | Not started |

After the first run the queries are incremental: postings with a `CUMVOUCH_DET_ID` above the watermark, plus the two months before the period again, so that a back-dated correction is caught. From the October 2026 month-end they are part of every monthly pack with `M09_01` to `M09_03`. Landing is unchanged: the pack passes the gates and the rows are kept verbatim in the landing zone, keyed on `CUMVOUCH_DET_ID`.

**Status 14 October 2026:** route 1 delivered: `GL_04a` and `GL_04b` came in pack 4 (`docs/bootstrap/packs/ebanker-pack-2026-10-12.zip`) and are landed by `Bootstrap::landLaterPacks`. The queries carry the branch fields and the pass and delete flags (`docs/bootstrap/queries/Pack 4 - 11 Oct 2026.sql:405-426`, consolidated review R22 section A), and the build drops a deleted or unpassed leg (`GlFeeBuildService.php:347`), but the batch key is still the date, batch type and batch number without the branch (`GlFeeBuildService.php:360`). The build of fee lines from a landed pack runs in `eir:bootstrap` and `php artisan eir:build-gl-fees` only; nothing yet runs it when a monthly pack lands or in the month-end job. **Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** the month-end job now runs it after the ledger cash (`maiic:task month-end`), and the gate at the door is built (7.8.5).

### 7.8.4 Tying each fee to its loan

The build joins `GL_04a` to `GL_04b` on the batch (transaction date, batch type, batch number) and writes one fee line per loan, fee type and posting, with the GL code, the amount, the posting date, the batch and the voucher, into `contract_fees` with the source `E-Banker GL fee posting`.

| Case | Rule |
|---|---|
| One loan account in the batch (most drawdowns) | The fee belongs to that loan, whether the loan shows it as its own line or inside the gross drawdown |
| Several loans in the batch (2 January 2025: Ebenezer Midian and Doorbell Services) | Split by matching each loan's own fee debit; where the loan has none, by the narration; what cannot be split is held as an exception, never guessed |
| No loan account in the batch (a fee paid in cash, or a loan outside the eighteen schemes, such as the BRFF fees of June 2026) | Held as an unassigned fee line and shown on the reconciliation; never dropped |
| A correction or reversal (Mchinji: 3,020,000 reversed on 24 October 2025 and 2,920,000 posted) | Netted on the loan, both lines kept for audit |

**A fee debit is not a drawdown.** The disbursement builder (`ContractInputsBuildService::disbursements`) turned every type-301 debit on a loan account into a tranche until 14 October 2026; it is now built as below (section 3.11), the fee debit added to the drawdown of its date (or, with none that day, the first) and recorded as `capitalised_fees`, and the take-on opening balance of 31 July 2024 no longer read as a drawdown. Only a separate fee debit is the fee capitalised into the loan: a type-301 line in a batch whose only other legs are fee GL credits of the same total (`GlFeeBuildService::feeDebitVouchers`, `app/Services/Ebanker/GlFeeBuildService.php:155-180`), or whose narration names an arrangement, legal or similar fee (`ContractInputsBuildService.php:232,277-280`). It is added to the drawdown of its date and never becomes a tranche of its own. A gross drawdown line with the fee inside it (Malawi Police SACCO, 1,046,210,000, one line, the fees credited by transfer) stays a drawdown at its own date, and a later genuine tranche stays a tranche on its own date (both in `GlFeeRouteTest::test_fee_debits_join_their_drawdown_and_the_takeon_opening_is_not_a_tranche`) (corrected 14 October 2026, consolidated review R01 and Cp8; it said "A type-301 line in a batch that also credits a fee GL, or whose narration names a fee, is instead the fee capitalised into the loan: it is added to the amount drawn at origination and never becomes a tranche of its own; a reversal reduces it"). With that, the initial net investment is the gross amount drawn less the fees, which is the cash MAIIC paid out.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the four cases of the table are built in `GlFeeBuildService::assign` (`:252-283`): one loan in the batch; several loans, by the loan's own debit of the same amount, then by the account name in the narration, else held (`SEVERAL_LOANS`); no loan in the batch, held (`NO_LOAN_IN_BATCH`, or `MEGA_FARM` when the only loan is a programme account), with a candidate loan shown, never used, and tied by a person with a reason (`assignHeld`, audit-logged); a reversal on a fee GL kept as its own negative line. Not built: a link from a reversal to the line it reverses and the known-at date of a fee line (R02: `contract_fees` has `transaction_date` only); the reduction of a capitalised fee by a reversal on the loan account (a credit on type 301 is skipped, `ContractInputsBuildService.php:223`). The netting of reviewed integral fees in the cash advanced on a ledger-basis month is built (section 3.11). Every GL fee line lands PENDING and needs a person's classification and review before it reaches an EIR.

**Status 15 October 2026:** a fifth case is built and was missing from the table: no loan in the batch, but the batch pays money out of GL 2010. In October 2024 a drawdown went from the loan to GL 2010, then a second batch paid GL 2010 to the bank net of the fee; `GL_07` (pack 5) finds the one loan drawn into 2010 for that amount, and the fee is that loan's (`GlFeeBuildService::clearedThrough2010`, rule `RULE_CLEARING`). Since 15 October a held posting can also be tied by Finance's workbook once `FeeWorkbookTieOut` has proved the whole file against the GL (7.8.8).

### 7.8.5 How the fees are reconciled

| Check | When | What it proves | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Fee GL check** (`fee_gl_reconciliation`) | After every fee import, and always on Fee Classification | The fees held for each year against the trial balance movement of 4871 and 4873, by month and for the year, inside `recon_tolerance`; PAID lines and undated lines listed apart; reports, never blocks | **Built 11 October 2026** (7.4) |
| **Fee GL gate** on the monthly pack | When the GL fee route is built (bootstrap, `eir:build-gl-fees`) | The month's movement on each fee GL in `GL_04a` equals the trial balance's | **Built 14 October 2026** as a report on every build (39 months agree, none differ); not yet a refusal at landing. **Status 14 October 2026 (go-live controls, uncommitted in the working tree):** a refusal at landing (`PackGates` `fee_gl`, level ERROR; `GlFeeRouteTest`) |
| **Assignment completeness** | After the batch link | Every `GL_04a` line is on a loan, or listed as unassigned or held, with its reason | **Built 14 October 2026**: 51 on a loan, 127 held in `fee_gl_unassigned` with the reason and, for 30, a candidate loan |
| **Source cross-check** | After each load | Where a lower source differs from the source of record: the loan, the fee type, both amounts and the difference, on Fee Classification | **Built 14 October 2026** in `FeeSourcePrecedence::apply` (17 differences on the rerun); the Fee Classification panel is to build |
| **Two-chart bridge** | Year end | The fees in E-Banker's trial balance to the audited accounts (section 7.7) | Mapping seeded; bridge to build |

For 2025 the arithmetic is already known: Finance's workbook ties to the GL at 311,342,792 (arrangement) and at 171,128,135 less 800 (legal: one keying slip on Chikwendeni General Dealers), and every GL fee line of 2025 traces to a loan in it. The workbook's own check rows show a doubled figure because row 46 sums a range that includes the row-39 subtotal; that is a spreadsheet range, not a second posting.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the fee GL gate follows consolidated review R04: closing vouchers (batch type `YRCLO`, types 800 and 801) are left out (`GlFeeBuildService.php:320`) and December is compared with the pre-closing trial balance read by `TrialBalanceMovementService`. The gate and the cross-check are returned by the build and audit-logged (`GL Fee Postings Built`) but not stored as a table; the Fee Classification panels that show the held lines and the differences are uncommitted work in the tree (`EirFeeClassificationController::heldGlFees`, `::sourceDifferences`, `resources/js/Components/Eir/FeeSourcesPanel.vue`). The figures in the table (39 months, 51 and 127, 17 differences) are from the rerun on the copy `maiic_ifrs9_eircheck`, not from a fresh bootstrap.

### 7.8.6 The choices MAIIC can make

| Setting (Governance Centre) | Options | Default | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fee_gl_reconciliation`: fee imports checked against the fee GLs | On; Off | On | Built |
| `fee_source_precedence`: which source a contract's fees come from | GL posting, then Finance workbook, then LOS capture, then Extract A; or Finance workbook first; or LOS capture first | GL posting first | Built 14 October 2026 |
| `takeon_eir_plausibility`: the check on a take-on EIR | Inside the schedule's own rates, 0.5, 1 or 0.25 point either side | 0.5 point | Built 14 October 2026 |
| `ebanker_feed_route`: how the packs, `GL_04` included, arrive | Routes 1 to 5 (7.8.3) | Route 1 | Built |
| `recon_tolerance`: the band for every reconciliation | As in section 4.2 | 1 percent, floor MWK 1 | Built |
| `takeon_history_basis`: how the take-on fees are used | As in section 6.9 | Recompute where block and fees exist | Built |

### 7.8.7 What Finance does, in order

1. For 2025, import the fee workbook through EIR Data Intake (type fees) with the GL posting date as `transaction_date` and 4871 or 4873 in `gl_account_ref`. Dupleix prepares the mapped file from the workbook and the GL lines, with the source `MAIIC Finance EIR assessment 2025`. This waits for `fee_source_precedence`, so that it supersedes the `P2_07` lines rather than adding to them.
2. Check the fee GL card on Fee Classification: every month of 2025 agrees, or the difference is named.
3. From the October 2026 month-end nothing is typed: the fees arrive with the monthly pack through `GL_04`, are tied to their loans by the batch, and are proved against the trial balance by the gate and the check.
4. Classify each line (integral or not) and have it reviewed, as today; only reviewed integral lines enter the EIR.
5. For the take-on loans, fill the fee columns of the take-on workbook or of the Take-on Schedules screen (section 6.9): one of the two, not both.

**Status 14 October 2026:** step 1 no longer waits: `fee_source_precedence` is built, and a file loaded through EIR Data Intake is the Finance workbook tier; under the default order (GL posting first) it is superseded, for every contract that has a GL fee posting, by the `GL_04` lines and serves as the cross-check. Step 3 is not yet automatic: the GL fee build is not part of the pack landing or the month-end job (7.8.3). The take-on fees were returned on 13 October 2026 in the workbook and land PENDING with the source `TAKEON_WORKBOOK` (section 3.11).

### 7.8.8 Finance's workbook proved against the GL postings (added 15 October 2026)

On 15 October 2026 Finance's two workbooks were reconciled to the fee GLs posting by posting. The 2024 file (the version of the December 2025 assessment that holds every loan tested) agrees for all 33 loans drawn in 2023 and 2024, 65 lines, with no difference; the old ledger (`CUMVOUCH_BK240824`) carries January to July 2024 and E-Banker (`GL_04`) from 3 October 2024, with no fee posting in between. The 2025 file (34 loans) agrees with the GL totals except 800 on Chikwendeni's legal fee (1,808,000 against 1,808,800), and supplies the loan for the 29 held "By Trf" postings the batch cannot place. On that evidence the workbook becomes the record of whose fee each posting is, but only through a gate, never by trust (`FeeWorkbookTieOut`, run by the EIR Data Intake import of type fees before anything loads):

1. Every line needs its loan, its date and a fee GL (`gl_account_ref`, or 4873 and 4871 for arrangement and legal fees). A line that repeats a fee the take-on workbook holds for its loan (same type, same amount within the band) is refused: those fees never compete, so a second copy would count twice. A later fee on a take-on loan (Kaning'a's November 2024 postings, for example) is proved like any other. A line dated before the first `GL_04` posting held is refused.
2. Every line must be accounted for by the postings: to the unit first (a posting tied to the loan, a held posting whose candidate is the loan, a held posting naming the customer, a fee in two or three parts or with its corrections, one held posting paying two lines' fees, the same amount near the date with no name, a posting the system tied to another loan of the same customer), then within `recon_tolerance`.
3. The file must be complete: for each fee GL it reaches, the postings of the months from its first to its last matched posting that no line accounts for must net to nil (a posting and its reversal cancel, in whichever months they fall). Mega Farm programme postings (held as `MEGA_FARM`, decision D30) are outside both sides.
4. Any failure refuses the whole file; the exception file lists every reason. A file that ties loads, and each held posting a single line accounts for is tied to that line's loan through `GlFeeBuildService::assignHeld`, with the file and row as the reason, all in one transaction; `fee_source_precedence` is then applied. A posting paying two loans is not tied (it cannot be split): the workbook line stands for each loan. Matches on amount and date only, differences within the band and postings the system had tied to another loan are written to the exception file as notes for Finance.

Run against the December 2025 file on 15 October 2026 (read only): PASS, 67 lines, 27 held postings to tie, two conflicts (Mthirakuwiri's 19 June 2025 fees, tied by the batch to 104430000077, which the workbook puts on 104460000110) and Chikwendeni's 800 within the band. Changing `fee_source_precedence` to "Finance workbook first" remains MAIIC's decision in the Governance Centre; with the gate in place it changes no amount the GL does not already support.

## 9. Acceptance baselines

The ties achieved this week become regression tests, run by `eir:bootstrap --verify` (section 6.10) and shown on the Baselines sheet of every audit workbook (section 12). A build that cannot reproduce them has broken something.

**Status 14 October 2026:** only part of the table is a regression test. `eir:bootstrap --verify` (`app/Console/Commands/Bootstrap.php:473-485`), `php artisan eir:baselines` and the workbooks' Baselines sheet all run `app/Services/Eir/BaselineService.php::checks`, which asserts five things: the ledger interest total (`:36`), the derived carrying amount against the stored report on the in-scope loan GLs (`:42`), the year-end batch contra on 4215 and on 4216 (`:56-57`), and the loan GLs against the trial balance at 31 December 2025 only, the two FInES differences accepted (`:59-80`); a sixth row reports the Mega Farm ledger gap and is never a failure (`:46`). Every other row below is stated, not asserted. `BaselineService` has no test of its own and no write-off check. The column "Kind" separates, as consolidated review R18 asks, a figure read from MAIIC's files (file evidence), a reproduction of what E-Banker did (historical model) and a result of this system (current result). A fixed exception count (the 16 Zaithwa months, the 2 FInES lines) stays in the baseline until the source is corrected; once Finance or the vendor corrects it and the correction is approved, the expected figure changes in `BaselineService` and here in the same commit.

**Status 14 October 2026 (later):** two reported rows changed or were added, neither a failure. The Mega Farm row now counts only the account-months whose ledger, with the government-portion legs, still differs from the report and on which the report stands (1 of 78,023 on the bootstrap copy `maiic_ifrs9_mfgap`; section 6.6), and a new row counts the report's sign errors on which the ledger stands (180). A new row compares E-Banker's own overdue run at month-end (`OD_01`, pack 9 RUN 8b, landed with the pack) with the days past due staging reads (`StagingService::daysPastDueOf`), per account-month: the run's days are counted from its oldest overdue principal or interest date to its calculation date (BaselineService::overdueRunComparison). On the bootstrap copy: 2,241 account-months in both, 1,782 agree and 459 differ (on 71 of the 131 accounts; 229 by more than 30 days, 123 by three days or less; the run is higher on 352, the book on 107); 104 of the differing rows are ones where the build counted the days on the instalment chart against the ledger receipts instead of the report (for example 104450000056, 0 days against the run's 1,063 at December 2025), and 43 are past maturity, where the build counts from the maturity date (for example 104460000017, 1,345 days against the run's 334 at September 2025). The differences are for review with Credit; RESCHEDULEMENT_DATE is empty on every overdue-run row received, so the run says nothing about reschedules. The daily rows (`OD_02`, 2025 Q3 to 2026 Q1) are landed and not yet read.

## 10. Risks and how they are held

| Risk | Effect | Held by |
|---|---|---|
| An extract is re-saved through Excel on its way to us | Dates scrambled, as in September | ISO-only importers that refuse and name the row; the session settings in every request; the files never opened in Excel at our end |
| The loan book is re-run after we load a month | A back-dated posting changes a carrying amount | The bootstrap keeps the latest run and prints every difference before overwriting; a locked period is never restated |
| The FInES GL openings are not corrected | A 600,000 net line in every reconciliation | Carried as a named line until Finance adjusts |
| The fee template is late | The 2024 and 2025 shift cannot be computed for the take-on book | Post-migration loans proceed on `P2_07`; the take-on figure is marked incomplete rather than estimated |
| A pack arrives by a new route before its gates are proven | A month derived from a file that did not tie | The gates are route-independent and run on every pack; a route is accepted only after one pack from it has passed on a copy |
| The daily accrual exists for 14 accounts only | The daily test cannot be run on the rest | The narration test covers all 2,047 postings |
| A governed default is confirmed late | Nothing stops; the month runs on the seeded value | The Governance Centre records which value each month ran under; a later change applies forward only |

**Status 14 October 2026:** two rows are out of date. Row 1: the file importers refuse and name the row, but the E-Banker pack gate for ISO dates only warns (`PackGates.php:115-144`), so the hold is the session settings and the files' handling, not a refusal at the door. Row 4: the take-on fees were returned on 13 October 2026 and land from the workbook (139 lines, PENDING, section 3.11); post-migration loans no longer rest on `P2_07` alone but on the GL fee postings first (7.8.2). The risk that remains is the fee review: 107 EIRs are blocked until each fee line is classified and reviewed. Row 2 holds: the loan-book build refuses to restate a locked period (`app/Services/Ebanker/LoanBookBuildService.php:95-97`).

## 11. The user interface: the Dupleix-suite layout

### 11.1 In plain language

Every system Dupleix now delivers uses the same shape of screen, the Dupleix suite layout, so that a finance officer who has learned one of them can find their way around the next. MAIIC's system was built earlier, and its menu still follows the headings of the contract schedule ("Customer & Loan Data", "IFRS 9 Model Setup", "ECL Processing") rather than the way the work is actually done. Decision D24 adopts the suite layout for MAIIC.

The shape is simple. On the left is a dark navigation panel with six working groups, each with its own colour: **Data Foundation** (what comes in), **Governance Centre** (the rules and settings that govern the calculations), **Financial Modelling** (the engines), **Risk & Regulatory** (the regulatory views), **Monitoring** (the watch-lists and alerts) and the **Report Hub** (what goes out), followed by System Documentation and Administration. Across the top is a bar with the menu toggle, the financial period the system is working in, an appearance switch (light, dark, or follow the device), notifications and the user's menu. Every page opens with a header that says where you are (the group, then the page) and what the page is for. The panel can be folded to a narrow rail of icons when the screen is small or the user wants room for a wide table. Nothing about the calculations changes; the screens move to where a user would look for them.

**Status 14 October 2026:** this section describes the layout of 8 October 2026 (commits c84898f and f324814), which was reverted on 9 October 2026 at Wadzanai's request (commit 4fd3dd5: the contract-aligned menu, the old sidebar and header, light mode only). What runs today: `config/menu.php` is still the single tree, filtered by permission on the server; Dashboard, Workspace and one Reports entry (the hub) at the top; the contract-schedule groups under four headed blocks, Data foundation, Financial modelling, Risk & regulatory and Administration & support (commit e168b4f), with related screens as tabs of one entry (commit 4719d28); the Governance Centre under Administration & support, Governance (not a group of its own); no Monitoring group and no Report Hub group (the report tiles stay in the hub, never in the menu); a collapsible icon rail remembered as `maiic.sidebar.collapsed` (`resources/js/Layouts/AppLayout.vue:260`); breadcrumbs on every page built on the server from the user's visible menu (`app/Support/Breadcrumbs.php`, shared as `breadcrumbs` by `HandleInertiaRequests.php:93`, commit 405cc70); the Product Groups page rebuilt with cards and a searchable, sortable table (commit e168b4f); and every import with its sample file, column guide and a fail-closed check (commit 7a2a96f). Not in the running layout: the appearance switch and dark mode (pinned to light, 11.5), the period chip (`currentPeriod` is shared but no component shows it), and the removal of the search box (still in `AppLayout.vue:60-67`). D24 is in that sense superseded; the six suite groups below are the design, not the build.

### 11.2 What the layout is made of

| Element | What it does | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Navigation panel (sidebar) | The brand at the top, then the two top-level links (Dashboard, Workspace), then the groups. One group is open at a time; the group that contains the current page opens by itself. Each group has an icon tile in its own colour, and the page in use is marked with a bar in that colour. | The suite's sidebar; MAIIC keeps its own recursive renderer so that a group can hold sub-groups (the modelling group needs them) |
| Icon rail | The hamburger in the top bar folds the panel to 68 pixels of icons; the choice is remembered in the browser. On a phone the same button opens the panel as a drawer over the page. | The suite's shell |
| Top bar | Menu toggle; a chip showing the financial period the system is working in and whether it is open or closed; the appearance switch (11.5); the notification bell; the user menu (profile, API tokens, log out). The decorative search box that does nothing today is removed. | The suite's top bar |
| Page header | An icon tile, the breadcrumb (group, then page) derived from the navigation tree and the current route, the page title, a one-line description, and a slot on the right for the page's actions. A page that already supplies its own header keeps it, inside this frame. | The suite's shell |
| Processing pill | The floating "Processing" and "Done" indicator while a request is in flight. Already in MAIIC; unchanged. | Already in MAIIC |
| Fail-loud error dialogue | The plain-language explanation of a 403, 419, 404 or server error. Already in MAIIC; unchanged. | MAIIC |
| Per-page help | MAIIC's help centre and the Help button on every page stay as they are. The guide text that names menu groups is re-worded to the new groups. | MAIIC |

Everything in the suite's shell is adopted, including light and dark mode (11.5). MAIIC's pages were not written with a dark variant, so the dark mode is delivered in two steps: the shell and the shared styles first, then a sweep of the pages (11.9).

### 11.3 Where every screen lives

The tree is the single source of truth: the server holds it in `config/menu.php`, filters it by the user's permissions, and sends it to the browser, which renders the panel and derives the breadcrumb from it. Nothing is duplicated in the Vue code. Routes do not change; only the grouping, the group names and the order do. "Today" is the group each screen sits in now.

Where the screens sit now: Clients, Loan Book and Imports & Feeds (Imports, E-Banker Loads, Loan Book Builds, Periods Built, Query Register, Take-on Blocks, Take-on Population) under Customer & Loan Data; Loan Portfolios, Product Groups and Sector Types under Portfolio Setup; the collateral screens under Collateral Management; EIR Data (with Drawdowns, Reference Rates, Trial Balances, Write-offs, Schedule Review), EIR Rules and EIR Calculations (with Coverage, EIR as at a Date and GL Reconciliation) under EIR & Revenue Recognition; Staging & SICR Rules (with SICR Trigger Alerts), PD Model and LGD Model under IFRS 9 Model Setup; Macro Statistics, the forward-looking model and the scenario screens under Forward-Looking Information; ECL Calculation and Mega Farm Programme under ECL Processing; Governance Centre, Audit Trail, Audit Trace and Compliance Audits under Governance; Users, Roles, Financial Periods and Settings under Access & Setup; Support Tickets, Maintenance and System Documentation under Support & Maintenance (`config/menu.php`). Stress Testing, the IFRS 9 report tiles, ECL Reconciliation, Loan Book Reconciliation, Disbursements, RBM Return and Auditor Pack are reached from the Reports hub, not from the panel. Screens added since this table that it does not list: Trial Balances, Write-offs, Mega Farm Programme, FLI Overview and Impact Analysis, Model Versions, Manual Overlays, Audit Trace, Compliance Audits, Auditor Pack, RBM Return and Maintenance.

Three rules behind the placement. A screen that **captures or shows what came in** is Data Foundation, whichever module uses it, so the EIR's data, drawdowns and reference rates sit beside the loan book and the collateral. A screen that **sets a rule the engines obey** is Governance Centre: the EIR settings, the accounting and fee rules, the staging thresholds, the periods and the audit trail that proves who changed what. A screen that **produces a figure** is Financial Modelling; one that **re-presents figures for a regulator or a reader** is Risk & Regulatory or the Report Hub. The hub's catalogue of 30 reports is unchanged; the eleven listed above are also reachable from the panel because they are the ones used weekly.

### 11.4 Behaviour, stated precisely

- **Permissions.** The server drops any entry whose permission the user lacks, and any group left empty, before the tree is sent (`HandleInertiaRequests::visibleMenu`, unchanged). A user never sees a link that would answer 403.
- **Active state.** An entry is active when the current route name equals its route (or its `route_check`). The group containing the active entry is open on page load and its header carries the group colour; one group is open at a time; the user may open another, which closes the first.
- **Breadcrumb.** Derived on the client from the tree and `route_name`: top-level entries give one crumb; a grouped entry gives group, then page; a sub-grouped entry gives group, sub-group, then page. A page outside the tree (an edit form reached from a list, for example) shows the crumb of the nearest list by its `route_check`.
- **Collapse.** Stored in the browser as `maiic.sidebar.collapsed`; on a screen narrower than 768 pixels the toggle opens the drawer instead. In the rail, each group shows its icon tile and, under it, the icons of its leaves with the label as a tooltip; sub-groups are flattened into their parent's icons.
- **Period chip.** The top bar shows the latest financial period and "Open" or "Closed" from its `closed` flag, shared from the server as `currentPeriod`. It is a display; it does not change the period.
- **Page header.** `title` and `description` may be passed as props; otherwise the title is the active entry's name and the description is empty. The `#header` slot, where a page provides one, renders inside the header frame in place of the derived title; the `#actions` slot renders on the right.
- **Colours.** Group colours are Tailwind families (teal, amber, sky, indigo, emerald, violet, slate, rose) written in full in one accent map so that the build includes them. The brand remains MAIIC navy and gold on the dark gradient.

### 11.5 Light and dark mode

Every user chooses how the system looks, and the choice follows them.

- **Three settings.** Light, Dark, and Follow the device. The switch is in the top bar and cycles through the three; the same choice is on the user's profile page. The default for a new user is Follow the device.
- **How it is applied.** Tailwind runs in class mode (`darkMode: 'class'`): the whole system is dark when the `dark` class is on the `<html>` element and light when it is not. Nothing else decides the theme, so there is one switch to test.
- **No flash of the wrong theme.** A four-line script in `app.blade.php` runs before the page paints: it reads the saved choice from the browser (`maiic.theme`), falls back to the device setting when the choice is Follow the device or absent, and sets the class. The Vue code then mirrors that state in one shared composable (`useTheme`) so the top-bar switch, the profile page and any other consumer stay in step.
- **Where the choice is kept.** In the browser, so that the first paint is right, and on the user's record (`users.theme_preference`, values `light`, `dark`, `system`), so that it follows the user to another machine: on login the server's value is written to the browser; when the user changes it, the browser value is written and the server is told. If the browser's storage is unavailable, the theme still applies for the session.
- **What dark mode must look like.** The sidebar keeps its dark gradient in both modes (it is dark by design). The page background, cards, tables, inputs, buttons, badges, modals, charts and the help panels carry a dark variant: slate backgrounds, light text, the group colours unchanged, MAIIC gold kept for the active marks. Contrast meets WCAG AA in both modes; no screen may show light text on a light ground or dark on dark.
- **How the pages get there without rewriting 281 components.** The shared classes the pages already use (`.card`, `.th`, `.td`, `.primary-btn`, `.secondary-btn`, the form inputs, the badges, the flash messages) are given their dark variant once in `app.css`, so most pages inherit the theme. The pages that style elements directly are then swept one group at a time (11.9), each page checked in both modes before it is signed off. Printed and exported outputs (PDF, Excel) are always rendered in the light palette, whatever the screen shows.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the machinery was built on 8 October 2026 (`darkMode: 'class'` in `tailwind.config.js:6`, `resources/js/composables/useTheme.js`, `users.theme_preference` by migration `2026_10_08_400000`, the `profile.theme` route, the page sweep of commit f324814), but since 9 October 2026 the pre-paint script in `resources/views/app.blade.php:12-19` writes `light` to `maiic.theme` on every load and the top bar has no appearance switch, so the system shows light mode only.

### 11.7 Acceptance

1. Every entry in 11.3 is reachable from the panel by a user with the right permission, and absent for one without it.
2. The breadcrumb on each of the 60 screens reads group, then page, as 11.3 lists them.
3. The rail, the drawer and the open-one-group rule behave as 11.4 states, on a 1366-pixel laptop and a phone.
4. The help centre and the two manuals name no group that no longer exists.
5. `SystemDocsTest` and the new navigation test pass; the EIR suite is unaffected.
6. The appearance switch cycles Light, Dark and Follow the device; the choice survives a reload, a new tab and a login on another browser; there is no flash of the wrong theme on load.
7. Every screen in 11.3 is checked in both modes: no unreadable text, no white panel on a dark page, charts and modals themed; PDF and Excel outputs unchanged.

**Status 14 October 2026:** against the running layout: item 1 is held by `tests/Feature/NavigationTest.php` (every leaf names a registered route; the contract entries, the hub and the forward-looking entry in order; report tiles never menu items) and by the permission filter; item 2 by `NavigationTest::test_a_menu_page_has_its_breadcrumb_trail` and `::test_a_page_under_an_entry_closes_the_trail_with_its_title`, with the restored groups, not the groups of 11.3; items 6 and 7 do not apply while the theme is pinned to light (11.5). The `accent` key of 11.6 is `color` and `heading` in `config/menu.php`.

### 11.8 Order of work

UI-1 the tree and the accent map (half a day); UI-2 the shell: rail, header, period chip, the appearance switch, the no-flash script and the shared dark classes (one day); UI-3 help text and the manuals' navigation chapter with new screenshots in both modes (half a day); UI-4 the walk-through of 11.7 items 1 to 6 on a copy of the database; UI-5 the page sweep for dark mode in the order of 11.9 (two days), with item 7 signed off group by group. UI-1 to UI-4 are done before P8, so that the new EIR screens of P8 are placed in the suite layout, and in both modes, from the start; UI-5 may run beside P5 to P7. All of it follows P4b, which does not touch the interface.

### 11.9 The dark-mode sweep, in order

The pages are swept in the order a user meets them, and each group is signed off in both modes before the next starts: (1) Dashboard, Workspace and the login pages; (2) the Report Hub and the IFRS 9 reports, which are what the CFO and the auditors open; (3) Data Foundation; (4) Governance Centre, including the Governance Centre screen itself; (5) Financial Modelling; (6) Risk & Regulatory and Monitoring; (7) System Documentation and Administration. The EIR screens are written with both variants from the start and are not part of the sweep.

## 12. Compliance audit workbooks

### 12.1 In plain language

An auditor's first question is not "what is the number" but "show me where the standard says so, and show me the system doing it". Dupleix's compliance-audit engine, in production in the suite, answers that with one audit workbook per standard or directive: every section of the directive on its own row, what the system does about it, where to see it, a status, and a place for the reviewer to sign. The same engine builds the Excel, a Markdown twin and a PDF from one data file, so the three can never say different things. Decision D25 adopts it for MAIIC, with two additions the EIR work makes possible: each row names the Governance Centre setting that governs it, and each row names the test that proves it.

The result is five workbooks, a register in the system where MAIIC signs each row, and an auditor's pack per period that bundles them with the figures. Deloitte gets a document they can walk from paragraph to screen to test; Dr Thom gets a count of what is done, partly done, outstanding, not applicable or waiting on evidence, which is the project's status in one line.

### 12.2 The five workbooks

| Workbook | What the rows are | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|
| **IFRS 9: the EIR and amortised cost** | The paragraphs the engine implements: the Appendix A definitions of effective interest rate, amortised cost and gross carrying amount; 5.4.1 to 5.4.4 (interest at the EIR; Stage 3 on the net amount, 5.4.1(b)); B5.4.1 to B5.4.7 (fees that are integral, the floating-rate reset, re-estimation of cash flows); 5.4.3 and B5.4.6 (modification gain or loss); 3.3.2 and B3.3.6 (derecognition and the 10 percent test) | Dr Thom, then Deloitte |
| **IFRS 9: impairment** | 5.5 and B5.5: staging, significant increase in credit risk, 12-month and lifetime losses, forward-looking information, write-off; the ECL module already built | Dr Thom, then Deloitte |
| **IFRS 7 and IAS 1: presentation and disclosure** | IAS 1.82(a), interest revenue calculated using the EIR shown as its own line; IFRS 7.20 and 7.35A to 7.35N, the credit-risk disclosures; each row mapped to the disclosure report that produces it | Deloitte |
| **RBM classification and provisioning** | The Reserve Bank directive behind the "IFRS 9 vs RBM" report, section by section, with the report line that answers each | MAIIC Risk |
| **Contract Schedule 1** | Each deliverable and acceptance item of the implementation agreement, with the screen, document or test that discharges it | Dr Thom, at acceptance (P9) |

**Status 14 October 2026:** all five are built as data modules (`tools/compliance/standards/ifrs9_eir.py`, `ifrs9_impairment.py`, `ifrs7_ias1_disclosure.py`, `rbm_dfi_directive.py`, `contract_schedule1.py`) and generated to `docs/compliance/` in four files each (Excel, Markdown, PDF and the JSON the register loads): 66 rows in all (19, 14, 12, 8 and 13). They were last generated on 9 October 2026 (commit f5b08fc) and are stale: they predate the write-off register, the GL fee route, the tranche and take-on EIRs and the discounted month-end ECL, and they carry governance keys that do not exist (for example `day_count_basis` for `day_count`, `cash_source_precedence`, `reconciliation_tolerance` for `recon_tolerance`) and a count of 48 settings. The RBM workbook's paragraph references do not match the Gazette (audit of 13 October 2026). They must be corrected in the modules and generated again before they go to Deloitte.

### 12.3 What a workbook contains

Four sheets. The first three are the engine's standard shape; the fourth is added for MAIIC.

| Sheet | Content |
|---|---|
| **Contents** | The standard's own order of sections, each hyperlinked to its row on the Audit sheet; the status counts at the top |
| **Audit** | One row per section of the standard, eleven columns (12.4), a status drop-down with colour coding, and conditional formatting that flags a "Done" with no test named |
| **Findings** | What needs a decision or a fix: number, reference, finding, what was found, impact, recommended action, owner, status |
| **Baselines** | The acceptance ties of section 9, one per row: the test, the expected figure, the system's current figure read at generation, and a live PASS or FAIL formula. The MAIIC counterpart of the engine's golden-numbers check |

### 12.4 The eleven columns of the Audit sheet

The engine's nine standard columns, then two MAIIC additions.

| # | Column | What goes in it |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Reference | The paragraph or section number of the standard |
| 2 | Section name | Its heading |
| 3 | What it requires | The requirement in one or two plain sentences |
| 4 | Status | One of: Done; Partially done; Outstanding; Not applicable, documented; Evidence needed from MAIIC |
| 5 | What the engine does | The behaviour, and the evidence checked when the status was set |
| 6 | General comment | Anything a reader needs that the other columns do not carry |
| 7 | Compliance comment | How the behaviour satisfies the requirement, or why it does not yet |
| 8 | Where to see it | The screen (as a route the register turns into a link) and the report or export |
| 9 | Reviewer sign-off | Initials and date; in the register this is captured under maker-checker, not typed |
| 10 | **Governance setting** | The key of the setting in section 4.2 that governs the behaviour (for example `rate_change_classification` on B5.4.5), so a reviewer sees which choice each paragraph turned on |
| 11 | **Test that proves it** | The PHPUnit test, by class and method, that fails if the behaviour changes; a row may be Done only if this column is filled |

**Status 14 October 2026:** each row of a module holds eleven fields, but not these eleven: the fields are part, reference, section name, requirement, status, engine comment, general comment, compliance comment, where to see it, governance setting and test (`build_audit.py`, the JSON it writes). Column 9, the reviewer sign-off, is not a field of the module; it is held in the register (`signed_by`, `signed_at`, `approved_by`, `approved_at` on `compliance_audit_rows`) and written back into the files through `compliance:audits --export-signed`.

The five statuses are the engine's standard ones: Done is implemented and visible in an approved output; Partially done is mechanics in place with a parameter, input or piece of evidence still differing from the requirement; Outstanding is required and not built; Not applicable carries its reason and reference; Evidence needed means the system is ready and MAIIC must supply a document, minute or dataset.

### 12.5 Where it lives in the system

| Place | What is there |
|---|---|
| **Governance Centre, Compliance Audits** | The register. One card per workbook with its status counts and the three downloads (Excel, PDF, Markdown). Opening a card shows the rows; a reviewer with the govern permission sets a row's status and signs it, a second person approves, and the audit log records both. Column 8 renders as a link to the screen. The workbook is generated from the register, so the signed state is the database, never a file someone edited |
| **Report Hub, Auditor Pack** | Per period: the Deloitte export (O12), the five workbooks as at that period, and the Baselines sheet, bundled in one archive with a checksum per file and a manifest, the way the suite archives a regulatory return. This is what is handed to Deloitte |
| **Governance Centre, Audit Trail, Audit Trace** | The suite's per-record trace: open any contract and see, oldest to newest, every change to its schedule, every reset and modification, and the governance values each of its months was run under. The workbook states the rule; the trace shows the rule applied to one loan |

**Status 14 October 2026:** built, in different places. The register is `compliance-audits.index` (Administration & support, Governance, Audit, Compliance Audits; `app/Http/Controllers/ComplianceAuditController.php`, pages `Governance/Compliance/Index` and `Show`): sign and approve are two routes, and `ComplianceAuditService::approve` refuses an approver who signed the row (`app/Services/Compliance/ComplianceAuditService.php:78-89`, `tests/Feature/Compliance/ComplianceAuditServiceTest.php`); a null approver (a console run) is not checked. The Auditor Pack is `auditor-pack.index` (reached from the Reports hub; `AuditorPackController`), which calls `php artisan compliance:audits --pack=YYYY-MM`: the pack holds the workbook files as they stand in `docs/compliance/` (not as at the period), the baselines read now as JSON, the EIR as at the period end as CSV, the ECL by stage, and `manifest.json` with a SHA-256 per file (`app/Console/Commands/ComplianceAudits.php:49-114`). It does not hold a Deloitte export in the O12 format (that setting, `auditor_export_format`, is read by no code). The Audit Trace is `audit-trace.index` (Governance, Audit; `AuditTraceController`, page `Audit/Trace`): the audit log, schedule versions, rate resets (none are written yet), fee lines, earlier solves, the roll-forward months and the governance values each month ran under.

### 12.6 How it is built

- **The engine is ported, not rewritten.** The suite's `tools/compliance/` (`audit_workbook.py`, `build_audit.py`, `md_to_pdf.py`) becomes `tools/compliance/` in MAICC-IFRS9, with the two columns and the Baselines sheet added. Each standard is a data module in `tools/compliance/standards/` exposing `META`, `ROWS`, `FINDINGS` and `BASELINES`; the builder validates them (eleven fields per row, a known status, no duplicate reference, a reason on every Not applicable, a test on every Done) and refuses to write a misleading workbook.
- **One source, three outputs, one register.** The data modules are the source. The builder writes the Excel, the Markdown and the PDF to `docs/compliance/`; a seeder loads the same rows into `compliance_audit_rows` for the register; the register's generation step reads the signed state back and rebuilds the three files. Nothing is typed twice.
- **The Baselines sheet reads the system.** At generation the builder runs the section 9 checks against the database and writes the current figure beside the expected one; the PASS or FAIL formula lives in the sheet so Deloitte can see it recompute.
- **Tables and code.** `compliance_audits` (one per workbook: key, title, standard, reviewer), `compliance_audit_rows` (the eleven columns, status, signed_by, signed_at, approved_by, approved_at), `compliance_findings`; `ComplianceAuditService`, `ComplianceAuditController`, pages under `Pages/Governance/Compliance`; the trace page `Pages/Audit/Trace.vue` ported with the EIR subjects (contract, schedule, reset, modification, setting); the pack builder `php artisan compliance:audits --pack={period}` and the Auditor Pack screen (`auditor-pack.build`) (corrected 14 October 2026; it said "the pack builder `eir:build-auditor-pack {period}`", a command that does not exist).

**Status 14 October 2026:** built as described, with these differences. The register is loaded from the JSON files by `php artisan compliance:audits --load` (`ComplianceAuditService::load`, also run by `eir:bootstrap`, `Bootstrap.php:457`), not by a seeder. The Baselines sheet reads the system through `php artisan eir:baselines --json` (`tools/compliance/build_audit.py:27-42`), so it carries the five asserted checks of section 9, not every tie. The builder's refusals are `validate` in `tools/compliance/audit_workbook.py:69-88` (eleven fields, a known status, no duplicate reference, a reason on every Not applicable, a test on every Done) and `check_tests_exist` in `build_audit.py:45-54` (every test class named exists; the method is not checked).
- **The IFRS 9 EIR module is drafted first**, from this specification: every paragraph it cites already maps to a service and a test, so its rows can be written now and signed as the phases land. The impairment, disclosure and RBM modules follow from the existing reports; Schedule 1 from the contract.

### 12.7 Acceptance

1. Each of the five workbooks builds from its module in the three formats, and the Markdown and Excel carry identical rows.
2. The builder refuses a Done row with no test, a Not applicable with no reason, and a duplicate reference.
3. In the register, a status change and a sign-off need two people, and both appear in the audit log with the old and new values.
4. Every column 8 link opens the screen it names for a user with the permission; every column 11 test exists and passes.
5. The Baselines sheet shows PASS on every section 9 tie against the production copy.
6. The auditor's pack for a period contains the export, the five workbooks and the manifest, and every checksum in the manifest verifies.

**Status 14 October 2026:** 1 met on 9 October 2026 (the four files of each workbook come from one module), but not since regenerated. 2 built in code (`audit_workbook.py:69-88`) with no automated test. 3 met for the different-person rule and the audit log (`ComplianceAuditService.php:78-89`, `ComplianceAuditServiceTest::test_sign_and_approve_are_two_people_and_done_needs_a_test`). 4 partly: the test classes are checked to exist; the methods, whether they pass, and the column 8 links are not checked, and the audit of 13 October 2026 found rows citing the wrong test. 5 not met: the Baselines sheet carries only the five asserted checks of section 9, and there is no production copy yet. 6 partly: the pack has the workbooks and a manifest with a checksum per file, but no Deloitte export (O12), and nothing verifies the checksums after the build.

### 12.8 Order of work

CA-1 port the engine and add the two columns and the Baselines sheet (one day); CA-2 the IFRS 9 EIR module, drafted from this document (one day, then kept current as P5 to P7 land); CA-3 the register, the trace and the pack (two days); CA-4 the four remaining modules (two days, Schedule 1 last, at P9). CA-1 and CA-2 start with P4b; CA-3 sits with P8, since the register and the pack are screens of the suite layout; CA-4 is finished before UAT.

**Status 14 October 2026:** CA-1 to CA-4 are done (all five modules exist and the register, the trace and the pack are built). What remains is upkeep: correct the modules (keys, counts, RBM references, the rows changed by the work of 13 and 14 October 2026), regenerate the five workbooks, and add a test of the builder's refusals.

## 13. Macro statistics: ingestion from the World Bank and the IMF

### 13.1 In plain language

The forward-looking part of IFRS 9 (B5.5.49 to B5.5.54) asks MAIIC to adjust its expected credit losses for what is reasonably expected to happen to the economy: growth, inflation, the exchange rate, interest rates, the harvest. Today those figures are typed into the system by hand from whatever source the analyst had open, with no record of where a number came from or when. The Dupleix suite does this differently: the system fetches the published series itself from the World Bank's open data service and the IMF's World Economic Outlook, shows the analyst what it found, and writes it only when the analyst says so, with the source, the address and the time kept against every figure. Decision D27 adopts that for MAIIC.

### 13.2 What MAIIC has today

The module exists and its tables are sound. `macro_statistics` holds the definition of each series (code, name, unit, frequency, how many historical and forecast periods, source, website link, active flag); `macro_statistics_data` holds one value per series, period and scenario, with an actual-or-forecast flag, an FLI flag, confidence bounds, assumptions and a free-text source, unique on series, period and scenario. Six screens use them under IFRS 9 Model Setup: Macro Elements (manual entry), Scenario Profiles, Weighted Forecast, Credit Loss Data, Adjusted Forecast and Regression Analysis, and the Management Overlays group holds the economic scenarios and external calculations.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the menu has changed. All these screens now sit under Financial modelling, Forward-Looking Information (config/menu.php:156-184): Macro Statistics with its five tabs; Forward-Looking Model (Credit Loss Data, Correlation Finder, Regression, Model Versions, Adjusted Forecast, Weighted Forecast); Scenarios & Overlays (Scenario Profiles, Scenario Sets, Economic Scenarios, Manual Overlays, External Calculations, Calculation History). The old Regression Analysis is retired: the Regression tab opens `fli-adjustments.index` and the legacy `regression.*` routes are off the menu behind `reports.ifrs9` (routes/web.php:1077-1090; LegacyRegressionApprovalTest).

What is missing is everything before the first screen: no external source, no preview before a write, no record of where a value came from, no command a bootstrap or a scheduler could run, and no rule for what happens when two sources disagree. The scenario-on-the-row design is in one respect ahead of the suite's and is kept.

**Status 14 October 2026:** these gaps are closed: the World Bank fetch, the IMF WEO and RBM files, preview before commit, a batch of provenance per commit, `macro:import-worldbank` with a committed snapshot, and the governed `macro_source_precedence` are built (app/Services/Macro/*, migration 2026_10_08_500000_macro_statistics_sources.php). The remaining gaps are listed under 13.5 to 13.7.

### 13.3 What the suite does, and is adopted

| Element | What it does | MAIIC adoption |
|---|---|---|
| **Indicator codes on the series** | Each macro variable carries the external codes that identify it at each source (`external_codes`, for example `{"world_bank": "NY.GDP.MKTP.KD.ZG"}`), seeded once. A series with no code says so on the screen; it is not an error | Two columns added to `macro_statistics`: `external_codes` (JSON) and `country` (ISO3, default `MWI`); a seeder with the Malawi codes of 13.4 |
| **World Bank fetcher** | Pulls an annual series for a country from `api.worldbank.org/v2/country/{ISO3}/indicator/{code}`: public, no key, 60-second timeout, two retries, year range optional, country from configuration with a screen override. Returns a normalised preview; writes nothing | `WorldBankFetcherService` ported as it is; `services.worldbank.country = MWI` |
| **IMF WEO parser** | Reads the World Economic Outlook download (a tab-delimited UTF-16 text file despite its `.xls` name), filters to the country and indicator, and marks each year actual or forecast from the "Estimates Start After" column. This is where the forecast periods come from | `ImfWeoParserService` ported; the file is uploaded by hand from the IMF site, which has no key-free API |
| **Preview, then commit** | Two endpoints per source: a preview the viewer may call, and a commit that writes, for the manager only. Nothing reaches the table until a person has seen the rows | Routes `macro-statistics.preview-worldbank`, `.import-worldbank`, `.preview-imf-weo`, `.import-imf-weo`; permissions `macro.view` and `macro.manage` added to the role set |
| **Provenance per import** | Every commit is a batch: source, address, fetched-at, who, how many rows, the file's hash where there is one; every observation points to its batch | `macro_source_import_batches`; `source_import_batch_id` on `macro_statistics_data`; the free-text `source` kept for manual entries |
| **Upsert, never duplicate** | Observations are keyed on series, period and period type, so a refresh updates the value and keeps the key | MAIIC's unique key on series, period and scenario already does this; an API import writes to the base scenario |
| **A command** | `macro:import-worldbank {--code=}` imports every series with a code, non-fatal per series, for the bootstrap and the scheduler | The same command; run by `eir:bootstrap` and monthly by the scheduler; a committed JSON snapshot under `docs/bootstrap/macro/` is the offline fallback so a server without internet still bootstraps |
| **The screen** | One page with five tabs: Dashboard, Variables, Data Entry, governed assumptions, Import / Export (World Bank with country and year range, IMF upload, CSV template and export) | Data Foundation, Macro Statistics (section 11.3), five tabs; the governed-assumptions tab is **Scenario Assumptions**, the base, mild and severe paths that feed Scenario Profiles and the Weighted Forecast, with the same approval lineage |

Two things the suite does not have are added for MAIIC. **Reserve Bank of Malawi series by file**: the policy rate and the prime lending rate are not on the World Bank; the PLR is already in the landing zone (`P2_06`) and the policy rate arrives as a small CSV through the same preview-and-commit screen, one more source tab rather than a special case. **A rule for disagreement**: a Governance Centre setting, `macro_source_precedence`, says which source wins where two overlap.

**Status 14 October 2026:** built, with three differences from the table. The endpoints are one pair, `macro-statistics.preview` and `macro-statistics.commit`, with a `source` field (world_bank, imf_weo, rbm_file), not four per-source routes (routes/web.php:1007-1008; MacroStatisticsController::preview and ::commit). The screen is `resources/js/Pages/Macro/Index.vue` under Financial modelling, Forward-Looking Information, Macro Statistics, not under Data Foundation (config/menu.php:162-168). The PLR reaches the monthly series from `reference_rate_series` through the FLI bridge (FliBridgeService:104-112). Permissions `macro.view` and `macro.manage` are seeded (MaiicAdminPermissionsSeeder:48-49) and enforced (MacroStatisticsController:32-33); the command runs in the bootstrap (Bootstrap.php:288-289) and monthly (app/Console/Kernel.php:31), with the snapshot `docs/bootstrap/macro/worldbank-MWI.json`; the precedence setting is read (MacroImportService:48).

### 13.4 The Malawi series, seeded

| Code | Series | World Bank indicator | Why MAIIC needs it |
|---|---|---|---|
| GDP_GROWTH | Real GDP growth, annual % | `NY.GDP.MKTP.KD.ZG` | The headline driver of default rates |
| CPI | Inflation, consumer prices, annual % | `FP.CPI.TOTL.ZG` | Real income of borrowers; the policy-rate path |
| MWK_USD | Official exchange rate, MWK per USD | `PA.NUS.FCRF` | Import-dependent borrowers; the industrial book |
| LENDING_RATE | Lending interest rate, % | `FR.INR.LEND` | Debt service burden; cross-check to the PLR series |
| REAL_RATE | Real interest rate, % | `FR.INR.RINR` | Affordability after inflation |
| PRIVATE_CREDIT | Domestic credit to private sector, % of GDP | `FS.AST.PRVT.GD.ZS` | Credit conditions |
| AGRI_GROWTH | Agriculture, forestry and fishing value added, annual growth % | `NV.AGR.TOTL.KD.ZG` | MAIIC's book is agricultural and agro-industrial; the harvest is its own driver |
| BROAD_MONEY | Broad money growth, annual % | `FM.LBL.BMNY.ZG` | Liquidity conditions |
| RESERVES | Total reserves in months of imports | `FI.RES.TOTL.MO` | Exchange-rate and import-cover stress |
| DEBT_GDP | Central government debt, % of GDP | `GC.DOD.TOTL.GD.ZS` | Sovereign stress and crowding out |
| CURRENT_ACCOUNT | Current account balance, % of GDP | `BN.CAB.XOKA.GD.ZS` | External balance |
| POLICY_RATE | RBM policy rate, % | by file (RBM) | The rate the PLR follows |
| PLR | Prime lending rate, % | the landing zone, `P2_06` | The floating-rate reference of the EIR engine, so the ECL and EIR modules read one series |

The IMF WEO gives the forecast years for GDP growth, inflation, the current account and government debt; the World Bank gives the actuals. A code that the World Bank later retires is a seeder change, not a code change.

**Status 14 October 2026:** `MacroSeriesSeeder` seeds all 13 series with these codes, plus the WEO codes for those four series (NGDP_RPCH, PCPIPCH, GGXWDG_NGDP, BCA_NGDPD). The fresh bootstrap of 13 October (database maiic_ifrs9_results2) holds 261 observations of the 11 World Bank series, all from the committed snapshot. It holds no WEO forecasts, no RBM policy-rate file and no `published_at` dates, because no WEO or RBM file is committed under `docs/bootstrap/`.

### 13.5 Behaviour, stated precisely

- **Country.** `MWI` by default; the screen may fetch another country for comparison, and such rows carry their country and are never used by the FLI model.
- **Periods.** World Bank and WEO series are annual; MAIIC's FLI model runs on the frequency the series definition states. An annual value is held as the year's observation; the Weighted Forecast interpolates to quarters or months as it does today, and says so on the row.
- **Actual against forecast.** A World Bank value is an actual. A WEO value is an actual up to the estimates-start year and a forecast after it; a later WEO vintage replaces an earlier forecast and the earlier one is kept in the batch history. A forecast is never overwritten by hand without a reason.
- **Precedence** (`macro_source_precedence`, section 4.2): World Bank for actuals, IMF WEO for forecasts, the RBM file for rates; a manual entry overrides any of them only with a reason, and the override is shown on the row and in the audit log.
- **Failure.** A series the source cannot supply is reported on the preview and skipped on the command; the import never fails as a whole because one indicator is missing. A fetch that cannot reach the source after the retries says so and leaves the table as it was.
- **Provenance to the audit workbook.** The IFRS 9 impairment workbook of section 12 cites, for B5.5.49 to B5.5.54, the batch behind each series in use: source, address, fetched-at, who committed it.

**Status 14 October 2026:** the precedence and failure rules are built. A forecast never replaces an actual (MacroImportService:72-75); the governed order decides between sources (MacroImportService:76-80; MacroImportPrecedenceTest); a manual entry needs a reason (MacroStatisticsController::manual); a series the source cannot supply is skipped and the command carries on (ImportWorldBankMacro:46-66). Three rules are not built:
- **Country.** A preview may name another country, but a commit writes onto the Malawi series. The observation row carries no country, and only the batch records it (MacroImportService:57-58), so a comparison fetch that is committed overwrites MAIIC's values.
- **Earlier vintages.** A revised value overwrites the row (MacroImportService:81-83), and the batch keeps only a summary of each series, not the values it replaced. The earlier WEO forecast is therefore not kept (FLI decision D-F10 is still open).
- **Provenance to the audit workbook.** The impairment workbook's B5.5.49 to B5.5.54 row cites the settings and a test, not the batches (docs/compliance/IFRS9_Impairment_Compliance_Audit.md:33).

### 13.6 What changes in the code

| Change | Where |
|---|---|
| `external_codes`, `country` on `macro_statistics`; `macro_source_import_batches`; `source_import_batch_id` on `macro_statistics_data` | Two migrations |
| `WorldBankFetcherService`, `ImfWeoParserService`, `SourceImportBatch`, the RBM file parser | `app/Services/Macro`, `app/Models` |
| `MacroExternalCodesSeeder` with the series of 13.4 | `database/seeders` |
| Preview and commit endpoints for the three sources; `macro.view` and `macro.manage` | `MacroStatsController`, `routes/web.php`, the permission seeder |
| `macro:import-worldbank`; a step in `eir:bootstrap`; a monthly schedule; the JSON snapshot | `app/Console/Commands`, `routes/console.php`, `docs/bootstrap/macro/` |
| The Macro Statistics screen with five tabs in the suite layout | `resources/js/Pages/FLI/MacroStats` |

| Tests: the fetcher against a recorded response, the WEO parser against a sample file, preview writes nothing, commit writes a batch, the command skips a missing series | `tests/Feature/Macro` |

Not changed: Scenario Profiles, Weighted Forecast, Credit Loss Data, Adjusted Forecast and Regression Analysis read the same tables as before; the FLI model's arithmetic is untouched.

**Status 14 October 2026:** built, with these differences from the table:
